# Volume 1

## Life 0: Prologue

### Section 1: The Geometry of Desire

#### Sub Section 1: Kendo Peeping

Kuoh Academy was supposed to be a paradise built specifically for the teenage male fantasy. Once a prestigious, high-end all-girls academy, its recent transition to a co-educational institution left the student body flooded with an overwhelming eight-to-two female-to-male ratio. To an outsider, walking through the sunlit wrought-iron gates meant stepping into a garden of blooming youth, perfume, and soft laughter.

To the male minority, however, it was a bloody, unforgiving minefield—and at the absolute bottom of the food chain sat the infamous social pariahs of the second-year class.

"I'm telling you, the mathematical probability of a successful breach increases by forty percent if we use the western maintenance alcove," Ren Nocturnus Veritas said, his voice quiet, flat, and chillingly precise.

He sat hunched over his wooden desk in the back corner of the classroom, tapping the end of a mechanical pencil against a hand-drawn blueprint of the school's gym building. Surrounding his desk like three ravenous wolves were Issei Hyoudou, Matsuda, and Motohama.

"Look at the line of sight," Ren continued, pointing the tip of his pencil at a crude illustration of the tree line adjacent to the girls' locker room window. "The Kendo club clears out by 4:15 PM. The cleaning crew doesn't rotate to the rear corridor until 4:30. That gives us a fifteen-minute window where the foliage provides seventy percent visual cover against the main courtyard."

Issei leaned in, his eyes practically bloodshot with a mix of awe and desperate lust. "Ren... you crazy bastard. You actually mapped out the guard rotations?"

"It's not rocket science, Issei, it's basic spatial awareness," Ren replied, his eyes narrowing slightly behind a fringe of dark hair. "You guys sprint in like brainless ape-men, heavy-footed and breathing through your mouths. That’s why you get caught, beaten, and publicly humiliated every single week. If we want to actually catch a glimpse of high-grade lace—or better yet, accidentally bump into one of them in the narrow hallway on the way out—we have to operate like a ghost squad."

"A ghost squad... for peeping!" Matsuda gasped, wiping a solitary tear from his eye. "He's a mad genius!"

"Adjust your glasses all you want, Motohama," Ren added, darting a sharp glance at the group's resident 'data pervert.' "Your digital camera's shutter click is too loud. If we get compromised, we drop to the floor, use low-profile belly crawls through the bush, and break line of sight around the storage shed. No screaming, no leaving anyone's wallet behind."

Underneath the desk, out of sight from his comrades, Ren’s left hand flexed rhythmically. He squeezed his fingers into his palm, slowly relaxing them, mentally tracing the subtle friction of his skin against his fingernails. It was a minor habit he had picked up from a obscure neuro-somatic paper he’d scavenged online—an attempt to map out motor-neuron awareness through passive concentration. Between that, his self-taught martial arts drill routines, and his endless late-night binges of *Metal Gear*, *Kingdom Hearts*, *Castlevania*, and martial arts webtoons like *Gosu*, Ren’s mind naturally translated everyday actions into tactical grids.

But make no mistake: Ren was not practicing stealth for the noble pursuit of mastery. He was doing it because he was a seventeen-year-old high school boy dying for female warmth.

He wanted the reward. He wanted the soft, sweet smell of a girl standing too close to him in the crowded hallway. He wanted the thrill of a sheer, unfiltered view through a cracked window shutter. He wanted, more than anything, to break out of the pathetic background-character status that the universe seemed to have forced upon him. The calculating approach wasn't a mask—it was simply how his intense, obsessive brain tried to guarantee success where his natural charm continuously failed.

"So," Issei grinned, slamming a fist into his palm. "We execute the 'Operation Rear Window' today after fifth period?"

"Correct," Ren said, folding the blueprint into a neat square and tucking it into his blazer pocket. "And try not to drool on the carpet this time, Issei. You're ruining our stealth threshold."

The golden glow of late afternoon washed over the rear courtyard of Kuoh Academy, casting long, dramatic shadows against the weathered brick of the main gym building.

At exactly 4:18 PM, three minutes behind Ren’s master schedule, four figures crawled through the thick hydrangeas lining the western alcove.

Ren led the formation in a textbook low-crawl, his elbows digging into the damp dirt and his hips staying flush against the ground. In his head, he wasn't just creeping around a school building; he was snake-crawling through Tselinoyarsk, a ghost in the grass. He monitored his own breathing—in through the nose, four seconds; hold, two seconds; out through the mouth, four seconds. It was a basic parasympathetic control technique he’d read about in a military stress-management manual, paired with the neuro-somatic focus he practiced during boring lectures. Every nerve ending in his forearms registered the coarse texture of the soil, the damp chill of the earth beneath his uniform.

Behind him, however, was a disaster of acoustic signatures.

Issei was hyperventilating like an asthmatic engine, his knees scraping against the brickwork. Motohama was clutching his digital camera to his chest like a sacred relic, his glasses fogged up with sweat, while Matsuda dragged himself forward with all the stealth of a wounded bear.

"Quiet down, you apes," Ren hissed over his shoulder without turning his head. "Issei, your footwork sounds like a herd of cattle. Settle your center of gravity."

"I can't help it, Ren!" Issei whispered back, his eyes wide and bloodshot. "The holy ground... we're five meters from the sacred sanctuary! I can smell the fabric softener!"

"Focus," Ren snapped softly, though his own heart was hammering against his ribs like a trapped bird.

He wasn't immune. Far from it. As they reached the blind spot beneath the frosted glass transom of the girls' changing room, the faint, sweet scent of berry shampoo and laundry detergent drifted through the slightly open gap at the top. Ren’s throat went dry. His skin prickled with a sudden, intense wave of heat. This was the moment. The calculated risk, the meticulous mapping of guard routes—it was all for this single, intoxicating proximity to a world he was completely shut out from. He wanted to see. He wanted to be near them. He wanted to prove that a background extra like him could outsmart the universe and claim a prize.

Ren carefully raised a small, angled dental mirror—scavenged from his bathroom cabinet—up toward the lower edge of the frosted window, tilting it to catch the reflection inside.

"What do you see?" Motohama whimpered, his fingers twitching on his camera shutter. "Ren, talk to me! Is it lace? Is it cotton?!"

"Visual contact," Ren murmured, his eyes widening as the mirror angled toward the interior. The soft lighting reflected a blur of white towels, discarded blue gym uniforms, and the smooth, pale curve of a girl's shoulder near the lockers. "Target acquired. High-density coverage. If we hold position for two minutes, the shift change will—"

"LET ME SEE!" Issei's restraint snapped like a dry twig.

Driven by pure, unadulterated horniness, Issei lunged forward, abandoning all tactical protocol. He shoved past Ren, slamming his heavy leather loafers directly into the dry brush with a loud, distinct *CRUNCH*. In his desperate bid to climb the air conditioner unit for a direct look, his knee clipped Motohama’s shoulder, sending the data-pervert crashing sideways into Matsuda.

Matsuda let out a high-pitched, pathetic squeal as he fell into a metal watering can, which clattered loudly across the concrete floor of the alcove like a siren going off.

Ren’s soul practically left his body. "Abort," he whispered in horrified disbelief. "Abort! Break line of sight! Low crawl to the storage shed, NOW!"

It was too late.

The frosted window snapped wide open from the inside. A sharp, icy voice echoed from the ledge above. "I thought I smelled garbage accumulating near the walls."

Ren looked up slowly, his tactical composure instantly shattering into cold dread. Standing on the window ledge, framing the afternoon sun like a terrifying sentinel, was Koneko Toujou. The petite, white-haired first-year from the Occult Research Club stared down at them with eyes like frozen steel, a half-eaten chocolate bar held nonchalantly in her hand.

"K-Koneko-chan!" Issei gasped, frozen mid-climb on the air conditioner.

"Perverts," Koneko said softly, her voice devoid of any human warmth. She didn't scream. She didn't run. She simply turned her head toward the interior of the locker room and spoke at a normal volume. "Vice President. The trash has gathered outside again."

A heavy, suffocating silence descended over the alcove for precisely two seconds.

Then, the metal side door of the gym exploded open.

Out poured six senior members of the girls' Kendo club, fully armored in bogu, their faces obscured behind dark steel grills, wielding thick, solid oak *shinai*. The air was instantly filled with a chorus of furious, bloodthirsty war cries.

"EXECUTE THE PEEPERS!"

"NO MERCY! SMASH THEIR SHINS!"

"Tactical retreat! Smoke route!" Ren yelled, reacting on pure instinct. He executed a sharp, fluid hip-roll to the left, springing off his hands into a low crouch to sprint for the gap between the storage sheds. His self-taught footwork kicked in—light on the balls of his feet, keeping his profile low to slip past the lead Kendo girl's swing. The wooden sword whistled past his ear, missing his scalp by an inch.

*I can make it,* Ren thought, his heart soaring with a brief flash of hope. *The gap is three meters away! I analyzed the terrain! I can escape!*

"REN! DON'T LEAVE ME!"

A heavy, desperate hand grabbed Ren’s ankle from behind. Issei, having been struck across the back, had lunged blindly toward safety and caught Ren’s shoe, dragging him down onto the gravel with a brutal, knee-scraping thud.

"Issei, you absolute bastard, let go—!"

Ren didn't get to finish the sentence. A heavy wooden *shinai* came down with the thunderous impact of a falling tree trunk directly across his shoulder blades.

*CRACK!*

The air was violently expelled from Ren’s lungs. Before he could roll away or use his hands to guard his ribs, three more wooden swords rained down in a chaotic, rhythmic beat.

"DIE! DIE! DIE!"

"MY LENS! NOT MY CAMERA!" Motohama shrieked somewhere to the right.

"SPARE MY FACE! I HAVE A DATE WITH DESTINY!" Matsuda howled as he was mercilessly battered into the dirt.

For three agonizing minutes, the western alcove became a war zone of blunt-force trauma and pathetic teenage cries. Ren curled into a tight defensive ball, tucking his chin into his chest and locking his forearms over his temple—a basic combat cover posture he’d memorized from a martial arts manual. But while he managed to protect his head, the wooden swords left brutal, stinging welts across his thighs, ribs, and back.

When the storm finally passed, the Kendo girls stood breathing heavily, resting their wooden swords on their shoulders, glaring down at the four broken shapes twitching in the dirt.

"If we catch you near the gym again," the Vice President growled through her menuki mask, "we won't use wooden swords next time. Clear out."

The heavy metal door slammed shut, leaving behind an absolute, oppressive silence broken only by the soft rustle of leaves and the faint, pathetic groans of four beaten teenagers.

Motohama lay face down in a patch of mud, his glasses snapped in half. Matsuda was curled in a fetal position, clutching his stomach. Issei was sprawled on his back, staring up at the sky with swollen cheeks, bleeding slightly from his nose.

Ren lay motionless beside them, his uniform torn at the shoulder, covered in dirt and crushed hydrangea leaves. A sharp, throb of pain radiated from his left ribs down to his hip.

Slowly, painfully, Ren pushed himself up onto his elbows. His hands were shaking—not just from the physical shock of the beating, but from a dark, suffocating wave of humiliation that welled up from the pit of his stomach.

He had mapped it out. He had calculated the guard shifts, the visual angles, the acoustic dampening of the foliage. He had spent hours studying spatial mechanics, trying to elevate himself above the brainless, stumbling failures of his peers. And yet, here he was, bleeding in the dirt, dragged down into the exact same pathetic joke of an ending.

He looked over at Issei, who let out a long, dramatic sigh.

"Ah man..." Issei groaned, wiping his bloody nose with his sleeve, a goofy, invincible grin somehow returning to his face. "Did you see the white ones on the third locker? Totally worth it."

Ren’s teeth ground together so hard his jaw ached.

*Worth it?*

Issei didn't care. Matsuda didn't care. They got beaten half to death, laughed it off, and moved on to the next pathetic delusion. But to Ren, every welt on his back felt like a physical brand, a reminder of his absolute uselessness. The universe didn't care about his calculations. It didn't care about his effort. To the world, to the girls, to the very narrative of this school, Ren Nocturnus Veritas was just a nameless, powerless extra destined to lie in the dirt while better men walked in the sun.

He pulled himself up to a sitting position, resting his forehead against his bruised knees, his dark hair veiling his face. Deep inside his chest, behind the throbbing pain of his ribs, something bitter and cold began to take root.

#### Sub Section 2: Brothers in Bruises

"Ow, ow, ow, cold, cold, COLD!"

Issei’s dramatic yelping echoed across the empty courtyard as he splashed freezing tap water onto his swollen nose. The four of them were huddled around the row of outdoor sinks near the track field, nursing their wounds under the dying orange light of the late afternoon sun.

"Stop whining, you baby," Matsuda groaned, leaning over the adjacent sink and carefully dabbing at a massive red welt on his forehead. "You just got a bloody nose. My face took the brunt of a high-speed oak strike! What if this scars? What if my beautiful mug is permanently ruined? My future wife is going to weep!"

"Your future wife is going to weep regardless of what your face looks like," Ren shot back, leaning heavily against the brick wall beside the sinks. He let out a loud, exaggerated groan of pain, clutching his side. "And for the record, Issei, you owe me a new ribcage. You didn't just blow our cover, you literally grabbed my ankle and offered me up as a human sacrifice."

"I panicked!" Issei argued, turning around with water dripping from his chin, his goofy grin completely unfazed by the beating. "It was fight or flight, man! My legs just gave out! But hey, you gotta admit, Ren... when the window opened, you saw them too, right? The promised land. It was totally worth taking a few hits."

Motohama adjusted the taped-together bridge of his broken glasses, sniffling pathetically. "Speak for yourself. My camera lens is cracked. Do you know how many weeks of allowance I saved up for that optical zoom? The promised land requires preservation, Issei. We lost a historical archive today."

Ren burst out laughing, a genuine, chest-deep sound that made him immediately wince and grab his ribs again. "You guys are absolute idiots," he wheezed, shaking his head. "Next time, I'm drawing up a liability waiver before we deploy."

Outwardly, Ren was perfectly in sync with them. He loved this. Despite the bruises and the dirt, standing here trading insults with three other hopeless losers was the only time during the school day he actually felt like he belonged somewhere. They were the bottom of the barrel, the undisputed outcasts of Kuoh Academy, but they were in it together.

Inwardly, however, Ren’s mind was running a cold, silent diagnostic on his own body.

While Issei and Matsuda continued to loudly debate whether the Vice President was wearing white or pale pink lace, Ren shifted his weight onto his right leg. He took a slow, measured breath, expanding his diaphragm to push against his bruised left ribcage.

*Sharp pain on inhalation,* his mind calculated, isolating the sensation from the noise around him. *No grinding sensation in the bone. Muscle contusion, severe but localized. Flexibility drops by roughly fifteen percent on the left pivot.*

He casually reached down, pretending to stretch his back, but he was actually pressing his thumb into the bruised tissue of his thigh, forcing his brain to map the exact circumference of the damage. He focused on sending slow, rhythmic pulses of relaxation to the inflamed area, attempting to manually lower his local muscle tension—a neuro-somatic trick he had been drilling for months.

"Hey, keep it down, the trash is making noise," a sharp, disgusted voice drifted over the courtyard.

The four boys froze. Walking along the paved path a few yards away were three girls from their year. They were carrying their school bags, eyeing the group at the sinks as if they were staring at a pile of rotting garbage.

"Ugh, it's the Pervert Trio," one of them whispered loudly, pulling her skirt slightly tighter as if proximity alone could contaminate her.

"Quartet now, apparently," another girl scoffed, her eyes briefly flicking toward Ren. "I don't know why that quiet one hangs out with them. He's just as creepy, mapping out the school like some kind of stalker. Did you hear they got busted at the gym again?"

"Pathetic. Let's just go."

The girls quickened their pace, completely ignoring Issei’s weak attempt to flash them a charming smile.

Once they were out of earshot, the four boys collectively sagged, the brief burst of adrenaline from their banter completely fading away. The heavy reality of their social standing crashed back down on them.

Issei sighed, leaning his back against the sink. "Man... why are they always so mean? We just want a little love. Is that so wrong?"

"We're pioneers, Issei," Motohama said solemnly, wiping his broken glasses on his shirt. "Society never respects pioneers in their own time."

"Yeah, well, this pioneer's back is killing him," Matsuda muttered, grabbing his bag from the ground. "I'm going home to put ice on my face before my mom asks if I got into a fight with a bear."

Ren watched them, his genuine smile fading slightly into a mask of weary camaraderie. He reached over, slapping Issei lightly on the unbruised shoulder. "Come on. Let's get out of here before the Kendo club decides they want round two."

"Yeah, yeah," Issei grumbled, slinging his bag over his shoulder. He looked at Ren, his eyes briefly shining with that endless, unkillable optimism. "Hey, despite the total failure today... good plan, Ren. Really. We almost had 'em. Next time, I won't step on a branch, I promise."

"Sure you won't, Issei," Ren replied, a faint, amused smirk returning to his lips. "Next time, I'm tying bells to your shoes so I know when to run."

They shared a final, tired laugh as they began the slow, limping walk across the courtyard toward the front gates. But as they rounded the corner of the main building, the laughter died in their throats, replaced by a collective, burning silence.

Standing beneath the shade of the grand oak tree near the gates, looking like he had stepped straight out of a shoujo manga, was Yuuto Kiba.

#### Sub Section 3: The Solitary Grind

"Alright, this is my stop," Ren said, pausing at the crosswalk where their routes home diverged. The streetlights overhead were just beginning to flicker on, casting long, artificial shadows across the pavement.

"Take it easy, Ren," Matsuda groaned, rubbing his lower back. "And seriously, next time you draw up a map, include the Kendo club's patrol routes."

"I'll factor in your lack of stealth as a primary variable," Ren shot back with a dry smirk.

Issei chuckled, adjusting his grip on his bag. "Get some rest, man. Tomorrow's a new day! And tomorrow, we're definitely finding a blind spot near the tennis courts. I can feel it in my bones."

"Your bones are currently pulverized, Issei," Motohama pointed out, though he was already pulling out a small notebook to jot down the tennis court idea.

"See you idiots tomorrow," Ren said, giving them a two-finger salute. He kept a warm, easy smile on his face as he turned and crossed the street, listening to their loud, bickering voices fade into the ambient noise of the evening traffic.

He walked for precisely one block. As soon as he turned the corner onto his quiet residential street, out of sight of his only friends, the smile instantly vanished.

His shoulders dropped. The casual, slouched gait of a careless teenager evaporated, replaced by a rigid, hyper-aware posture. He didn't look like a boy who had just been laughing about a failed peeping mission. His eyes grew cold, flat, and intensely focused.

Every step he took was calculated to minimize the shockwave traveling up his bruised left leg. The public mask was off. The exhausting performance of being the "quirky tactician" of a joke group was done for the day. Here, in the quiet of his own space, there was only the bitter reality of his own weakness.

Ten minutes later, the front door of his empty house clicked shut. Ren didn't bother turning on the main hallway lights. He walked straight to the bathroom, stripped off his dirt-stained, torn uniform, and stepped into the shower.

He turned the dial to maximum heat. The scalding water hit his shoulders, tracing the angry purple welts blooming across his ribs and back. Ren closed his eyes, resting his forehead against the damp tiles. He began his neuro-somatic breathing cycle. *Inhale for five. Hold for two. Exhale for seven.*

Instead of letting his mind wander, he drove his focus entirely into the bruised tissue of his left side. He visualized the capillaries, the inflamed muscle fibers, the rush of oxygenated blood circulating to the damaged areas. He mentally commanded the surrounding muscles to release their guard-tension, forcing his nervous system to bypass the pain signals and prioritize cellular repair. It wasn't magic, but it worked—the intense, localized meditation tricked his body into accelerating its natural recovery process, dulling the sharp throbs into a distant, manageable ache.

After ten minutes of static visualization, he turned off the water, dried off, and pulled on a pair of loose-fitting track pants and a white t-shirt.

He walked into his bedroom and pushed his desk and chair against the wall, clearing a six-by-six-foot square of empty floor space.

Ren stood in the center, barefoot. He didn't play music. He didn't set a timer.

He dropped into a low, balanced orthodox stance and began to move. First came the evasions. He visualized the vice president of the Kendo club swinging that oak *shinai* at his head. He pivoted on his back foot, slipping his head off the centerline by a mere two inches—just enough for a phantom weapon to miss. *Wasted energy is death,* he reminded himself, smoothly returning to his center.

He transitioned into dodging, keeping his feet planted while aggressively tilting his torso back and laterally, bending at the waist and knees. He parried invisible strikes, his forearms moving in tight, economical deflections, immediately snapping out a counter-jab. Block, slip, strike.

The pace increased. He threw elbows, keeping his guard glued to his temples. He shot forward for a grapple, sprawling his hips hard against the floor to defend an imaginary takedown, popping right back up to his feet without using his hands. Sweat beaded on his forehead, stinging his eyes. His bruised ribs screamed in protest with every violent torso twist, but Ren ruthlessly suppressed the pain through sheer cognitive control.

He wasn't training to fight demons or save the world. He didn't even know the supernatural existed. He was training because the universe handed out power and protagonist-level charm to people who didn't even have to try, leaving the rest of the world to be trampled. He refused to remain in the dirt.

For forty-five minutes, the room was filled only with the sharp *hiss* of his exhales and the squeak of his bare feet on the hardwood.

When his muscles finally reached the edge of failure, he stopped. He wiped his face with a towel, took a long drink of water, and sat down at his computer desk.

He booted up his PC and logged into his chess client. His rating sat firmly at 1600 Elo—not grandmaster level, but high enough to filter out the casuals.

He queued for a 10-minute rapid game. Playing Black, he responded to his opponent's e4 with the Caro-Kann Defense. Solid, unassuming, defensive. As the mid-game approached, he systematically stripped the board of tension, coldly trading down pieces to nullify his opponent's aggressive attacks. He didn't play for flashy checkmates. He played a suffocating, mechanical positional game, choking his opponent's movement until they blundered a knight out of sheer frustration.

He won the game on time, staring blankly at the *Victory* screen. The geometry of the board was just like the geometry of the school courtyard, or the angle of a punch. Everything could be mapped. Everything could be calculated.

Ren closed the client and shut down the computer. The digital clock on his nightstand read 11:30 PM.

He lay flat on his back on his bed, arms resting straight at his sides, palms facing up. He stared at the ceiling, beginning his final descent into sleep. He started at his toes, mentally shutting down the nerve clusters, moving up through his calves, his thighs, and his core. By the time his focus reached his chest, his heart rate had plummeted to a slow, rhythmic crawl.

Tomorrow was another day of playing the fool. But tonight, he was building the machine.

#### Sub Section 4: The Engine of Inspiration

To an outside observer, the paradox of Ren Nocturnus Veritas made no logical sense.

How did a solitary orphan—a teenager who played cold, positionally ruthless 1600-Elo chess and spent his nights methodically mapping out neuro-somatic nerve pathways—end up rolling around in the dirt as the four-man 'ghost squad' tactician for Kuoh Academy's most infamous perverts? How could a mind capable of calculating precise line-of-sight angles for a high-risk stealth operation genuinely share high-fives and drool over high-grade lace with Issei Hyoudou?

The answer lay in the sheer, unadulterated volume of fiction that crammed every corner of his brain.

Ren was not a cold, monastic soldier born in a vacuum. He was a high school kid who had spent more than half his life starving for a life bigger than the one he was assigned. While his nights ended with brutal, self-taught martial arts drills, his afternoons and weekends were overwhelmingly consumed by an endless, ravenous appetite for pop culture, gaming, and fiction.

He didn't just consume stories; he inhaled them until they rewrote his perception of reality.

His shelves and hard drives were digital and physical monuments to epic absurdities. He had spent hundreds of hours mastering tight parry windows and combo cancels in *Devil May Cry* and *Tekken*, analyzing spatial distance and frames of movement as if they were real-world physics. He had ground his way through every entry of *Final Fantasy*, losing himself in tragic, world-ending stakes. He had braved the claustrophobic dread of *Biohazard*, *Silent Hill*, and *Project Zero*, training his nervous system to sit quietly in darkness while ambient horrors hunted in the background. He knew the lore of *Valkyrie Profile*, *The Elder Scrolls*, and the sweeping, historical massacres of *Dynasty Warriors* and *Samurai Warriors* like second nature, and he replayed *Metal Gear Solid* until tactical stealth felt as natural as breathing.

When he wasn't holding a controller, his eyes were glued to screens reading endless streams of manga, anime, and webtoons. He drew his raw, unvarnished inspiration from the grand, planet-shattering scales of *Dragon Ball*, *One Piece*, *Bleach*, *Naruto*, and *Fairy Tail*. He obsessed over the violent, hyper-precise martial arts mechanics in webtoons like *Gosu*, *The God of High School*, and *Eleceed*. He devoured military power-fantasies like *Teenage Mercenary* and *God of Blackfield*, and took a dark, vicarious thrill in the ruthless, unyielding logic of *Return of the Bloodthirsty Police* and countess Murim novels centered on Heavenly Demons who forged supreme strength out of sheer, unbending will.

He didn't just read about these characters—he desperately wanted to be like them.

That intense, nerdy obsession was the exact bridge that connected him to Issei, Matsuda, and Motohama. When he stood in the hallway arguing about *Tekken* frame data, debating power-scaling across shonen universes, or hyping up a new game release, Ren wasn't wearing a mask. He was being a hundred percent authentic. He was just a fellow nerd who shared their wild, untamed teenage passions. When they dreamed of getting a girlfriend or building a legendary harem, Ren’s brain merely translated those same basic, hormonal desires into the epic, grand-scale narratives he loved. If a shonen protagonist could break through his limits to win the girl and save the world, why couldn't a background extra like him use tactical genius to at least catch a glimpse of the promised land?

This massive, chaotic mix of fiction was also the precise reason Ren had not become a world-class Olympic athlete.

A true elite martial artist dedicated eight hours a day purely to physical conditioning, diet, and recovery. Ren couldn't do that. His attention was constantly split. One week he would spend four hours a night drilling evasions because a chapter of *Gosu* hyped him up; the next week, his physical training would take a backseat because a new *Kingdom Hearts* release demanded fifty uninterrupted hours of his life. His routine was a messy, hybrid patchwork—part teenage slacker, part obsessive gamer, part self-taught martial artist.

Yet, beneath his mortal physical limits, this endless consumption of media was secretly constructing something far more dangerous: an unshakeable cognitive framework.

Where a normal human's mind would shatter or freeze in sheer disbelief when confronted with the impossible, Ren’s brain had already spent a decade exploring concepts of spiritual energy, spatial rifts, inner power reserves, and supernatural hierarchies. He had already accepted, conceptually, that a mortal body possessed hidden depths waiting to be unlocked by those crazy enough to push past the brink.

As his breathing slowed and his consciousness finally slipped into the deep, dark ocean of sleep, his mind rested on the quiet, unyielding foundation he had built for himself. He was still weak. He was still just a powerless, bruised high school kid lying alone in an empty house. But the blueprint was already drawn, buried deep within his soul, waiting for the spark that would ignite it.

### Section 2: The Hierarchy of Fate

#### Sub Section 1: The Prince and the Paupers

Standing beneath the golden canopy of the grand oak tree near the front gates, looking as though he had been hand-drawn by a high-budget animation studio, was Yuuto Kiba.

He was surrounded by a thick circle of second and third-year girls. Kiba’s signature blonde hair caught the dying sunlight perfectly; his smile was warm, effortless, and entirely disarming. Every soft laugh he let out elicited a chorus of suppressed giggles and blushing cheeks from the girls hanging on his every word.

"Look at him," Issei spat, his knuckles turning white as he clutched his school bag straps. "Just look at that blonde bastard. Existing like he owns the universe!"

"It's a crime against humanity," Matsuda hissed, his bruised face contorting into an expression of raw, demonic rage. "How can one guy hoard that much female interaction while the rest of us are out here taking wooden swords to the spine?!"

"My data indicates a ninety-nine percent concentration of romantic interest directed solely at his demographic," Motohama whimpered, pushing up his taped glasses. "The rest of us are living off biological crumbs."

Ren chimed in loudly, his voice echoing their furious, theatrical outrage. "It's a complete distribution error! The spatial density of girls around him is violating basic social equity! He's running an illegal monopoly on school morale!"

They all burst into a chaotic, bitter tirade, cursing the skies, cursing Kiba's princely face, and swearing vengeance on a society that favored handsome sword-club prodigies over battered peepers. Ren laughed along with them, slinging an arm over Matsuda’s shoulder as they shared in the ridiculous, agonizing solidarity of the bottom-tier outcast. He meant every word of his complaints—he genuinely hated how unfairly the social deck was stacked, and screaming about it with his brothers in arm was therapeutic.

Yet, behind his loud venting, Ren’s eyes narrowed into sharp, analytical slits.

He wasn't just looking at Kiba’s face or the girls around him. He was breaking down Kiba’s body mechanics.

*Look at his stance,* Ren noted mentally, his gamer and martial artist instincts running an involuntary scan. *Zero wasted energy. His weight is evenly distributed sixty-forty between his heels and the balls of his feet. Even while standing casually with his hands in his pockets, his posture is perfectly aligned—spine straight, shoulders relaxed, core stable.*

When one of the girls laughed and lightly tapped Kiba's arm, Kiba shifted his hips by half an inch, effortlessly absorbing the contact without breaking his smooth, liquid balance. To a normal person, he just looked charming and graceful. To Ren, who spent hours in his room attempting to master basic evasions and center-of-gravity shifts, Kiba’s movement profile was unnerving.

He moved like a high-level boss character. He moved like an elite swordsman straight out of a Murim webtoon or a *Devil May Cry* cutscene—someone whose body naturally operated at peak kinetic efficiency without having to drill it into his nervous system until his ribs bruised.

Issei envied Kiba because Kiba had a harem of cute girls.

Ren envied Kiba because the universe had clearly chosen Kiba as a "protagonist." It had given him the face, the natural physical poise, the effortless grace, and the social untouchability that Ren could grind for a hundred years and still never naturally inherit.

*Systemic privilege,* Ren thought bitterly, watching Kiba bow politely and wave goodbye to the swooning girls before walking toward the gate. *Some people are spawned with maxed-out base stats, while the rest of us have to fight for scraps in the mud.*

"Man... I swear, one of these days, a beautiful girl is going to drop from the sky and fall in love with me," Issei muttered, glaring at Kiba’s retreating back with an unbeatable, tragic hope. "Just you wait. My rom-com storyline is gonna start any day now!"

"Keep dreaming, Issei," Matsuda groaned. "Your storyline is a horror manga, and we're the side characters who get eaten in chapter one."

"Shut up! A guy can dream!" Issei yelled back, though a wide, goofy grin returned to his face as they finally stepped through the gates and out onto the main street.

### Sub-section 2: Parting Ways at the Crossroads

The evening air carried the cool breeze of early autumn as the four of them reached the major four-way intersection two blocks from the school. The streetlights were fully lit now, buzzing quietly against the twilight sky.

"Alright, this is where I split," Motohama said, tapping his broken glasses. "I need to get to the electronics shop before it closes to see if my camera sensor is salvageable."

"I'm heading straight home," Matsuda sighed, stretching his arms up. "If I don't ice these bruises, my mom's gonna think I joined an underground fight club."

"What about you, Issei?" Ren asked, resting his hands in his blazer pockets.

"I'm taking the scenic route home through the park," Issei said, stretching his neck with a loud crack. "Need to walk off the sting in my legs. Plus... who knows? Maybe a cute girl is waiting for me on the bridge."

"If a girl is waiting for you on the bridge at this hour, Issei, she's either a ghost or a mugger," Ren deadpanned, letting out a soft laugh. "Don't get yourself kidnapped."

"Screw you, Ren! You'll see! Tomorrow I'm coming to school with a girlfriend!" Issei shouted triumphantly, giving them a dramatic wave as he turned down the tree-lined pedestrian path leading toward the local park bridge.

"See ya tomorrow, pervert!" Matsuda called out.

"Don't die out there!" Motohama added.

Ren stood at the crosswalk, watching Issei's retreating back until his friend's red blazer disappeared into the shadow of the park trees.

"See you tomorrow, Issei," Ren murmured quietly.

Then, he turned on his heel and stepped onto the crosswalk toward his own neighborhood.

The moment his back was turned to his friends, the friendly, casual warmth in his expression vanished. His shoulders dropped their slouched, teenage posturing, locking into a precise, balanced alignment. His eyes flattened, shifting from the bright, expressive energy of a high school banter-partner into the cold, calculating glare of an isolated strategist.

The public noise was gone. The jokes were over.

As he walked down the dimly lit pavement alone, his mind began counting his pulse, monitoring the slight inflammation in his left ribs, and mentally arranging his evening schedule. He had two hours of fundamental footwork drills, an hour of neuro-somatic breathing control, and a few rapid chess games to play before sleep.

He didn't know that down at the park bridge, Issei Hyoudou was about to meet a girl named Yuma Amano. He didn't know that the fragile, mundane reality of Kuoh was about to shatter into a blood-soaked nightmare of Fallen Angels, Sacred Gears, and Devils.

All Ren knew was that he was weak, bruised, and invisible—and he had work to do.

#### Sub Section 3: The Architecture of Movement

Ren didn't take the turn toward his house.

Two streets past the intersection, he veered left into a narrow, shadow-drenched alleyway that cut behind a row of commercial storefronts. He unzipped his school bag, folded his school blazer into a tight square, and shoved it inside alongside his dress shoes. From the bottom of his bag, he pulled out a pair of worn, thin-soled athletic sneakers he carried everywhere, swapping his footwear with practiced efficiency.

He slung his bag tightly across his back, securing the straps across his chest. He took a deep, grounding breath through his nose, letting his gaze sweep over the concrete canyon before him. Fire escapes, dumpsters, air conditioning units, low brick walls, and rusted water pipes.

To a normal person, it was an ugly back alley. To Ren, whose brain was continuously wired by hundreds of hours of *Mirror's Edge*, *Assassin's Creed*, *Dying Light*, and hyper-kinetic urban webtoons, it was an obstacle course designed for spatial conquest.

He exploded forward.

His first three strides were explosive, driving the balls of his feet into the cracked asphalt. He launched himself at a four-foot dumpster, planting his palm on the metal lip and executing a clean, horizontal speed-vault. His body skimmed over the edge, landing silently on the far side in a low, sprung crouch.

*Impact absorption: seventy percent on the right leg,* he noted internally. *Keep the left side guarded.*

Without losing momentum, he took two hard steps up a brick wall, catching the top lip of a low concrete awning with his fingertips. He pulled his chin over the ledge, hooking his right heel over the masonry, and rolled his body up onto the roof section in one continuous, fluid motion.

The bruised ribs from the Kendo club's beating flared with a sharp, white-hot sting as his torso compressed during the climb. Ren bit his tongue, suppressing a groan. He didn't slow down. Instead, he forced his brain to process the pain as pure data—a temporary signal from bruised intercostal muscles that could be compensated for by shifting his center of gravity two inches to the right.

He ran across the flat gravel roof of the commercial building, sprinting toward the three-meter gap separating it from the adjacent residential complex. The drop below was two stories of hard concrete.

*Three steps. Plant. Launch.*

He hit the edge at full speed and sprang into the air. For a suspended, breathless second, Ren was weightless against the darkening purple sky, his arms reaching forward like a predator. He caught the iron railing of the residential fire escape, his fingers locking around the metal bars. The kinetic impact slammed his chest against the railing, knocking the wind out of him, but he immediately dissolved the force into a tight, fluid cat-leap, dropping three feet down to the metal mesh platform below.

He exhaled sharply through his teeth, his lungs burning, sweat dripping off his chin. He leaned against the brick wall, performing his neuro-somatic breathing cycle to force his heart rate back down from its peak.

*Inhale five. Hold two. Exhale seven.*

He loved this. He loved the absolute, uncompromising honesty of physical movement. Up here, away from the fake smiles of school and the suffocating reality of his background-character status, the world reduced down to pure geometry, friction, and momentum. If his calculations were off by an inch, he fell. If his grip failed, he bled. There was no divine intervention, no protagonist armor, no unearned privilege. There was only his own effort against gravity.

After a minute of rest, Ren climbed the final iron ladder leading to the main roof of the four-story residential building.

The cool evening wind pulled at his damp t-shirt as he stepped onto the gravel rooftop. The building sat on a natural elevation near the edge of the district, offering a wide, panoramic view of the surrounding neighborhood and the sprawling river park that cut through Kuoh.

Ren walked to the concrete ledge at the northern edge of the roof, resting his hands on the cool stone and looking down.

The streetlights along the river embankment were coming on, forming a long, glowing ribbon through the twilight shadows. The park below was quiet, almost entirely deserted as the evening chill settled in.

And there, roughly two hundred yards away, walking along the paved pedestrian path toward the center of the arched concrete bridge, was a familiar figure in a crimson school blazer.

Ren leaned forward slightly, his sharp eyes locking onto Issei’s distinct, heavy-footed stride.

Issei had reached the bridge.

### Section 3: The Illusion of Fortune

#### Sub Section 1: The Anomaly

The wind pulled at Ren’s sweat-damp hair as he crouched on the concrete lip of the roof, his dark eyes locked on the tiny, distant figure in the crimson blazer two hundred yards below.

Issei had reached the midpoint of the arched pedestrian bridge. From this height, surrounded by the quiet hum of the city and the rustle of autumn leaves in the river park, everything looked like a miniature set piece.

Then, a second figure stepped out from the shadow of the weeping willows at the end of the bridge.

Ren squinted, his vision narrowing as he adjusted his posture to get a clearer line of sight. It was a girl. She was wearing an unfamiliar, high-class school uniform—not Kuoh Academy’s standard outfit—and carrying a sleek brown leather bag. She walked with a hesitant, almost fragile gait, her head bowed as she stepped onto the concrete arch directly into Issei’s path.

Ren watched as Issei halted mid-step. Even from two hundred yards away, Ren could practically hear the high-pitched, panicked squeak his friend undoubtedly made.

The girl stopped a few feet from Issei. She wrung her hands together, took a timid step forward, and bowed her head sharply, her dark hair falling forward over her shoulders.

For three agonized seconds, Issei stood completely frozen, like a statue carved out of pure shock.

Then, Issei exploded into a wildly hysterical, uncoordinated victory dance. He threw his arms into the air, fell back onto his heels, pumped his fists violently toward the sky, and nearly tripped over his own feet before bowing back to her so fast his forehead almost hit his knees.

On the roof, Ren stared for a beat, completely stunned.

Then, a massive, brilliant grin broke across Ren’s face. He let out a loud, breathless laugh, shaking his head in sheer disbelieving joy.

"No way..." Ren whispered, resting his forearms on the concrete ledge, his eyes crinkling with genuine happiness. "You crazy, loudmouth bastard... you actually did it."

It was a miracle. One of the Four Perverts had actually done it. After all the beatings, the public humiliation, the broken camera lenses, and the bruised ribs from the Kendo club, Issei had somehow pulled off a total statistical anomaly. A cute girl had just intercepted him on a bridge to talk to him.

Ren felt a warm, genuine wave of pride swell in his chest. Issei was his brother-in-arms in the trenches of social pariahdom. Seeing him get a win felt like a victory for their whole pathetic group.

### Sub-section 2: The Glitch in the Matrix

Ren kept watching as the girl took a step closer to Issei, her body language modest, almost deferential. Issei was rubbing the back of his head, laughing nervously, his chest puffed out like a proud rooster.

And that was when Ren’s tactical, gamer-wired brain kicked in.

The warm smile on Ren’s face slowly began to falter. His eyes narrowed, his internal diagnostic mechanics overriding his initial thrill.

*Wait,* Ren thought, his mind rapidly sifting through the variables. *Run the geometry.*

Kuoh Academy had only gone co-ed a few years ago. Every girl in the local district knew the infamous reputation of the Pervert Trio. Issei Hyoudou’s face was practically on a warning poster in every female locker room within a three-mile radius. Yet this girl—wearing a completely different uniform—was standing alone on an unlit, empty bridge at dusk, waiting specifically for the most notorious social outcast in the city.

*It doesn't make sense,* Ren calculated, his mind spinning like a chess grandmaster analyzing an impossible blunder. *The spatial probability of a random, high-tier girl approaching Issei at night, in an isolated location, with zero prior interaction... is practically zero.*

To his media-drenched brain, it looked like an unearned cutscene. Or worse—it looked like an ambush script straight out of an action RPG. It was the classic setup where an NPC baits a naive player into an unwinnable trap zone.

Suddenly, a violent, unnatural sensation hit Ren in the chest.

It wasn't the physical ache of his bruised ribs. It was an abrupt, icy drop in the pit of his stomach—a sudden, suffocating pressure that made the hair on the back of his neck stand up on end.

Ren gasped, taking a sharp step back from the ledge. His pulse spiked violently. For a terrifying, fleeting second, his subconscious mind—tuned by months of neuro-somatic focus—felt a prickle of raw, suffocating coldness radiating from that distant bridge. It was a heavy, razor-sharp weight in the air, like being locked in the crosshairs of a heavy sniper rifle.

His heart hammered against his ribs like a trapped beast. His hands twitched into tight fists. He stared back down at the distant girl on the bridge, his instincts screaming that something was fundamentally, terrifyingly wrong with the picture before him.

He was picking up on something that no mortal high schooler was supposed to feel: the faint, masked leak of a Fallen Angel's murder-intent.

### Sub-section 3: Descent into the Grind

Ren stood on the edge of the roof for nearly a minute, his chest heaving, his dark eyes locked on the two figures beneath the streetlights.

Down on the bridge, Issei was still grinning, chatting away completely oblivious to the world, while the girl stood beside him, bowing politely as they began to walk off together.

Ren closed his eyes, taking a deep, ragged breath through his nose. He forced his neuro-somatic breathing routine to engage, driving his skyrocketing heart rate back down through sheer willpower.

*Inhale five. Hold two. Exhale seven.*

Slowly, the icy, suffocating prickle on his neck faded away into the cool autumn night air.

Ren opened his eyes and rubbed his forehead, letting out a long, weary sigh. "Get a grip, Ren," he muttered to himself, his voice laced with dry, self-deprecating annoyance. "You've been reading too many horror webtoons and playing too much *Biohazard*."

He shook his head, running a hand through his damp fringe. He was being paranoid. He was so used to analyzing threat angles and expecting the worst that his brain was manufacturing phantom danger out of thin air. Issei was just having his lucky day. If Ren went running down there like a maniac, acting like a jealous, paranoid stalker, he would ruin the single best moment of his friend's life.

"Don't ruin it for him," Ren murmured softly to the empty night sky. "He earned his happy ending."

He turned his back on the bridge.

Ren walked to the edge of the roof, grabbed the iron railing of the fire escape, and swung his body over the side. He dropped smoothly to the platform below, his thin-soled sneakers making a quiet *tap* against the iron mesh. He dissolved the kinetic impact into a tight crouch, pivoted on his heels, and began his rapid, silent descent back down into the dark alleys of the city.

Issei had just stepped into his grand high school romance.

Ren, on the other hand, had a long jog home through the dark. He had bruised muscles to ice, basic martial arts stances to drill, and a positional chess game to play before sleep.

He didn't know that the girl on the bridge was Raynare. He didn't know that Issei's "happy ending" was actually a death sentence. All Ren knew, as he hit the cold asphalt of the alleyway and broke into a steady, rhythmic stride, was that the night was young—and he still had a long way to go before he was strong enough.

## Life 1: New Life

### Section 1: The Traitor of Kuoh

#### Sub Section 1: Girlfriend

The morning sun bled through the high windows of the 2-B classroom, throwing warm light across the rows of wooden desks. Before the homeroom bell could even chime, Matsuda and Motohama were already huddled around Ren’s desk, groaning over their shared homework assignment.

Ren leaned back in his chair, resting his hands behind his head. "Hey. Before Issei gets here, I have to report an anomaly from last night."

Motohama paused, his pen hovering over his notebook as he pushed up his taped glasses. "An anomaly? Did you spot a new security blind spot near the gym?"

"Worse," Ren said, dropping his voice to a conspiratorial whisper. "I was finishing my running route up on the ridge near the park bridge around dusk. I saw Issei walking home."

Matsuda wiped his nose with the back of his sleeve. "So? The guy walks like a confused duck. What's so weird about that?"

"He wasn't alone," Ren said, leaning forward. "A girl intercepted him right on the bridge. She was wearing an unfamiliar, high-class uniform—not Kuoh's. She approached him, bowed, and they stood there talking for ten minutes."

Silence fell over the desk.

Motohama’s eyes widened behind his thick lenses. "Nonsense. The baseline probability of a female initiating direct verbal contact with Hyoudou without a threat of physical violence is zero point zero zero three percent. Your spatial observation was corrupted by low lighting."

"I'm dead serious," Ren chuckled, enjoying their stunned expressions. "And then Issei did his ridiculous victory dance right there on the concrete."

"He... he didn't," Matsuda gasped, clutching his chest as if hit by a physical bullet. "Ren, tell me you're lying. Tell me he was getting mugged! Tell me it was a debt collector!"

"Good morning, losers!" a loud, booming voice echoed from the classroom doorway.

All three turned. Issei Hyoudou stepped into the room, and he was literally glowing. His stride was bouncing, his chest was puffed out like an overconfident peacock, and a wide, dopey, borderline-insane grin stretched across his face.

He didn't just walk to his desk—he glided. He threw his school bag onto his seat with a flourishes that belonged in a high-budget theater production.

"Look at him," Matsuda whimpered, his voice trembling with deep, existential terror. "Look at that face. Ren wasn't lying."

"Gentlemen," Issei said, crossing his arms and looking down his nose at them with supreme, obnoxious smugness. "Bask in the presence of a man who has officially exited the lower strata of human existence."

Motohama stood up so fast his chair screeched against the floorboards. "Hyoudou! Speak clearly! What happened on the bridge last night?!"

Issei closed his eyes, dramatically tossing his head back. "Ah, so the rumors have already reached your pathetic ears. Yes, it's true. Yesterday evening, an angel descended from the heavens. Her name is Yuma Amano. She looked at me with tears in her eyes, blushed like a blooming rose, and confessed her undying love."

"A CONFESSION?!" Matsuda shrieked, drawing the bewildered stares of three classmates near the window. He grabbed Issei by the lapels of his uniform, shaking him violently. "You traitor! You scum! You absolute villain! We had a pact! We were supposed to rot in the trenches of singlehood together!"

"Release me, peasant!" Issei laughed maniacally, swatting Matsuda's hands away. "My rom-com storyline has officially begun! She asked me out on a date this Sunday! We're meeting at the park! I am no longer just Issei the Pervert—I am Issei, the boyfriend!"

Motohama fell back into his chair, clutching his head in utter despair. "It's a statistical catastrophe! The balance of power in our group has completely collapsed! We are down to a trio of pariahs!"

"He crossed the DMZ without tactical authorization," Ren chimed in with a theatrical, disappointed shake of his head, joining the group outrage with an exaggerated sigh. "He violated the Non-Aggression Pact of the Four Perverts. I motion for an immediate tribunal."

"Seconded!" Matsuda howled, pretending to wipe tears of blood from his cheeks. "May your date be rained on! May your shoe lace untie! May you accidentally order the most expensive item on the menu and be forced to wash dishes!"

Issei just threw his head back and laughed, utterly immune to their curses. He was floating on a cloud of pure, unadulterated teenage bliss.

Ren joined in on the loud, chaotic bantering, slinging an arm around Matsuda’s shoulders to fake-restrain him from tackling Issei. He played his part perfectly, slinging jokes and hyping up the absurd drama of the situation.

Yet, as he watched Issei chatter on about how cute Yuma’s voice was and how soft her hair looked, Ren’s analytical mind briefly drifted back to the rooftop.

He remembered the sudden, suffocating chill that had spiked through his chest when he looked down at that bridge. He remembered the razor-sharp feeling of invisible crosshairs locking onto the scene.

Ren looked closely at Issei’s face. There was no deceit there. No hidden agenda. Issei was just a naive, clumsy, genuine teenage boy who thought the universe had finally handed him a break.

*I'm overthinking it,* Ren thought to himself, forcing a soft, internal exhale. *I spend too much time reading dark urban fantasy and drilling combat angles. It was just the autumn wind and my own paranoid imagination. He’s just happy.*

Ren stepped forward, breaking away from Matsuda's dramatic sobbing, and extended a closed fist toward Issei.

Issei blinked, looking at Ren's hand.

"If you ruin this by saying something completely unhinged on Sunday, Issei," Ren said, his warm, easy smile returning, "Matsuda and Motohama will personally bury you in the courtyard. But... congratulations, man. Don't mess it up."

Issei's goofy grin softened into something genuine. He grinned wide and slammed his fist against Ren's in a heavy, solid fist-bump. "Thanks, Ren. I won't. I swear, I'm gonna be a total gentleman!"

"LIES!" Matsuda screamed in the background. "HE WILL FAIL IN FIVE MINUTES!"

Just as the homeroom teacher slid open the front door to call the class to order, a tall, striking girl with long crimson hair passed by the hallway window. She was accompanied by a smaller, blonde girl carrying a stack of papers.

As Issei sat down in his chair, the blonde girl paused briefly by the classroom door, slipping a small, elegant paper flyer onto the corner of Issei's desk before walking away to keep up with the crimson-haired student.

Issei picked up the flyer, frowning in confusion. It was an oddly styled parchment featuring a strange, circular crest and a line of stylized text advertising the *Occult Research Club*.

"What's this?" Issei muttered, turning it over in his hands.

"Probably trash," Motohama sniffed, still wiping away his imaginary tears. "Focus on the lesson, traitor."

Ren glanced at the flyer in Issei's hand for a split second, noting the odd, symmetrical geometry of the crest. His brain filed it away as just another weird school club advertisement, entirely unaware that the wheel of fate had already begun to turn.

#### Sub Section 2: The Architecture of Aspiration

The sheer, absurd reality of Issei Hyoudou securing a date acted as a psychological accelerant for Ren.

If a guy who spent his afternoons meticulously cataloging the color of the girls' tennis team uniforms could stumble into a high-tier rom-com scenario, the underlying mechanics of the universe were clearly pliable. As Ren sat in his dark, quiet bedroom that Friday night, his mind began to map out a new, hyper-focused thesis. To his gamer-wired brain, Issei had somehow triggered a hidden event flag by pure chance. But Ren didn't want to rely on RNG—random number generation. He wanted to build his stats until the universe had no choice but to reward him.

He rationalized it through a flawless, two-pronged logical theorem: Path One dictated that maximizing his physical and tactical capabilities would inevitably elevate his social standing, inherently increasing the probability of drawing the attention of high-tier girls. Path Two provided the ultimate safety net—even if the girls never came, he would still be ascending toward peak human performance. It was a win-win scenario, fueled entirely by the raw, unadulterated envy and hope of a teenage boy.

That night, after completing a grueling two-hour parkour circuit across the commercial district rooftops, Ren didn't just go to sleep.

He stood under the freezing spray of his shower, deliberately turning off the hot water to shock his nervous system. Closing his eyes, he pushed his neuro-somatic conditioning deeper than he ever had before. He didn't just map the bruised tissue; he visualized his entire circulatory system. He focused on consciously dilating the blood vessels in his extremities, fighting the body's natural autonomic response to the freezing water. He commanded his heart rate to slow, forcing his brain into a state of hyper-oxygenated calm. The cold ceased to be a physical torment and became pure data, a sensory input he could manually mute.

Stepping out of the shower, his skin flushed red from the extreme temperature shift, he wrapped himself in a towel and booted up his PC. He spent the next hour silently studying grandmaster chess tournaments online. He watched the world's elite dissect the board, analyzing how they controlled the center, sacrificed pawns for positional dominance, and suffocated their opponents through suffocating spatial geometry.

Then, seamlessly shifting from high-level cognitive warfare to raw emotional fuel, he closed the chess client and opened his anime folder. He binged four episodes of a wildly popular harem anime. He watched the impossibly dense protagonist surround himself with beautiful, powerful heroines, absorbing the exaggerated tropes and dramatic pacing. He wasn't just watching for entertainment; he was crystallizing his motivation. He let the fantasy feed his drive, transforming basic teenage hormones into a burning, mechanical obsession to ascend his own mortal limitations.

The next morning, while Issei was undoubtedly agonizing over what to wear for his Sunday date, Ren hit the local community gym.

He didn't just wander over to the weight racks like a casual gym-goer. He arrived armed with a thick library book on human kinesiology and biomechanics. For three hours, he treated his own body like a dissecting table. He meticulously isolated every major muscle group—the latissimus dorsi, the obliques, the vastus lateralis, the intricate stabilizers of the rotator cuff. He tested fast-twitch versus slow-twitch muscle fibers, experimenting with explosive plyometric box jumps to measure his kinetic output, followed by agonizingly slow, time-under-tension isometric holds to map his muscular endurance. He wanted to understand the absolute mechanical limits of the human frame so he could eventually figure out how to break them.

By the afternoon, he transitioned his focus from raw strength to absolute elasticity.

Ren cleared the floor of his living room and initiated a brutal stretching regimen. He pushed his joints past their comfortable thresholds, performing static and dynamic splits, backbends, and contortionist bridges until his tendons screamed. He integrated advanced acrobatics, practicing back handsprings and aerial cartwheels in the tight, confined space of his house. Every time he failed, crashing heavily onto the hardwood floor, he instantly ran a mental diagnostic, adjusted his spatial trajectory by a fraction of an inch, and launched himself back into the air. He needed his body to act as a flawless, liquid shock absorber.

As the weekend progressed, Ren took his most ambitious step yet: attempting to synthesize his meditative mind with his violent body.

Until now, his neuro-somatic conditioning had been a static tool—something he used while standing still in the shower or lying flat on his bed to heal and recover. Now, he wanted to weaponize it.

He dropped into his orthodox fighting stance, bouncing lightly on the balls of his feet, and began throwing complex striking combinations at the empty air. A jab, a cross, a slipping pivot to the left, followed by a vicious roundhouse kick. But this time, he layered his breathing cycle directly over his kinetic output.

*Inhale on the slip. Hold on the pivot. Exhale entirely through the strike.*

It was agonizingly difficult. Moving at high speeds naturally spiked his adrenaline and shattered his focus, causing his heart rate to erratically spike. But Ren refused to quit. He visualized the oxygen routing directly to his fast-twitch fibers at the exact millisecond of impact. He forced his nervous system to stay icy and calm while his body executed violent, explosive torque. He wanted the cold, calculating mind of a 1600-Elo chess player operating inside the kinetic frenzy of a fighting game combo.

Sweat pooled on the floorboards beneath him. His lungs burned, and his muscles twitched with near-paralytic exhaustion. Yet, as Saturday night bled into the early hours of Sunday morning, a terrifyingly focused smile crept across Ren's face.

Issei was going to chase a girl at the park. Ren was going to build a god out of flesh and bone.

### Section 2: The Unseen Strike

#### Sub Section 1: The Art of the Shadow

Sunday afternoon in Kuoh’s shopping district was a dense, chaotic weave of civilian traffic. For Ren, it was the perfect laboratory.

He walked down the crowded pavement with his eyes cast downward, his vision deliberately restricted to the two feet of concrete immediately in front of his sneakers. He was running a real-time sensory isolation drill, forcing his brain to map his environment without relying on his primary optical input.

He tuned his hearing first, isolating the distinct acoustic signatures of the crowd. He separated the sharp, rhythmic *clack-clack* of a woman's high heels to his left from the heavy, scuffing drag of a tired salaryman to his right. He mapped the Doppler effect of passing cars, judging their speed and distance purely by the pitch of their tires against the asphalt.

Next, he focused on olfactory data. He inhaled through his nose, compartmentalizing the competing scents of the street: the heavy, greasy exhaust of a passing bus, the sharp ozone of the cooling autumn air, and the overwhelming wave of caramelized sugar from a nearby crepe stand. He even mapped his tactile feedback and spatial awareness—the subtle shift in air pressure on his right cheek when a cyclist zoomed past too closely, the metallic tang of city smog on his tongue, and the exact positional weight of his own limbs as he navigated the moving bodies without bumping into a single person.

It was an exhausting cognitive load, demanding maximum neuro-somatic focus to process so much raw data simultaneously.

Then, a sudden, familiar sensory input broke his concentration.

It wasn't a sound. It was a smell. Specifically, it was the suffocating, chemical assault of cheap, aerosol body spray applied in lethal quantities.

Ren stopped walking. He raised his head, fully opening his eyes.

Standing twenty yards ahead, staring nervously at his reflection in the tinted glass of a closed electronics store, was Issei Hyoudou. He was dressed in a casual jacket and jeans, frantically attempting to flatten a stubborn cowlick in his brown hair while checking his watch every five seconds. His posture was a frantic mess of nervous energy, shifting his weight back and forth like a coiled spring.

Ren crossed his arms, leaning casually against a brick pillar as a slow, amused smirk spread across his face.

Issei was actually on his date. The notorious pervert of Kuoh was standing in the middle of the shopping district, waiting for a girl.

Ren’s first instinct was to walk over, slap his friend on the back, and tease him about the toxic cloud of cologne hovering around him. But as he watched Issei pace back and forth, a different idea sparked in Ren’s tactical mind.

He had spent the entire weekend drilling his physical limits, studying blind spots, and mimicking the stealth mechanics of his favorite games. Tracking an active, moving target through an urban environment was the ultimate test of his spatial geometry and evasion training. Issei was loud, completely lacking in situational awareness, and entirely focused on his impending date—making him the perfect, unwitting training dummy. Furthermore, Ren’s natural curiosity demanded to see if this mysterious "Yuma Amano" was actually real or if Issei was about to get stood up in spectacular fashion.

Ren pulled his dark hood up over his head, shielding his profile.

He adjusted his posture, dropping his center of gravity slightly to mute the impact of his footsteps. He visualized the "cone of vision" extending from Issei’s frantic pacing. When Issei turned his head to the left, Ren slid smoothly into the slipstream of a large group of passing high school girls, using their collective mass as a visual barrier.

The game was on.

Issei finally checked his watch one last time, let out a massive, visible exhale, and began walking toward the central plaza.

Ren detached from the crowd, maintaining a precise thirty-yard gap. He mirrored Issei’s pace exactly, stepping only when ambient street noise—a passing truck, a laughing group of kids—masked the sound of his approach. He utilized the environment flawlessly, drifting behind bus stops, darting behind vending machines, and pausing in the shadows of store awnings whenever Issei randomly stopped to check his reflection.

To Issei, it was just a nerve-wracking Sunday stroll to meet his dream girl. To Ren, it was a high-stakes stealth mission playing out in real-time, completely devoid of his previous rooftop paranoia. He was just a gamer testing his urban mechanics in the real world, entirely unaware that he was quietly tracking his best friend straight toward a slaughter.

#### Sub Section 2: The Predator's Gait

The target acquired her mark at exactly 1:15 PM near the central fountain.

From his vantage point on the second-floor balcony of an outdoor cafe, partially obscured by a decorative trellis, Ren watched Yuma Amano arrive. She was objectively flawless—petite, dressed in a stylish autumnal coat, her dark hair framing a delicate, flushed face. Issei practically vibrated out of his shoes, bowing so deeply he nearly headbutted the pavement before nervously offering her a hand.

Ren took a sip of his black coffee, his eyes tracking their movements as they began to walk toward the boutique stores. The stealth game was holding his interest, but as he watched Yuma walk, his freshly acquired knowledge of biomechanics began to fire warning signals in the back of his mind.

There was something fundamentally wrong with the way the girl moved.

Ren leaned forward, resting his elbows on the railing, his eyes narrowing as he analyzed her gait. Normal human locomotion was a controlled forward fall—the heel strikes the ground, the weight rolls through the foot, and the calves and hamstrings absorb and distribute the kinetic shock.

Yuma didn't walk like that.

She stepped almost entirely on the balls of her feet, her spine aggressively straight, her shoulders pulled slightly back. She moved with an eerie, frictionless glide, as if her center of gravity was hovering a foot higher than it should be. To Ren’s analytical mind, her posture looked like a physical overcompensation. She walked exactly like someone who was subconsciously balancing a massive, unseen weight on their upper back.

*She moves like she’s used to being airborne,* Ren thought, tapping his index finger against his coffee cup. *Or like she’s hiding a fifty-pound backpack under her coat. It doesn't make sense.*

Despite the mechanical anomalies, the date proceeded like a standard teenage rom-com. For the next three hours, Ren tailed them flawlessly. He shadowed them through a clothing store by pretending to browse winter jackets, tracked them across an arcade by blending into a crowd of rowdy middle-schoolers, and maintained a forty-yard visual down the main promenade as the sun began to dip below the city skyline.

He utilized his neuro-somatic breathing to manage the physical toll of a three-hour stealth operation, keeping his heart rate at a flat, resting rhythm while his legs burned from maintaining a low, silent crouch.

By 5:30 PM, the sky bruised into deep shades of violet and orange. The weekend crowd thinned out, the temperature dropped, and Issei and Yuma turned away from the commercial district, heading down the familiar paved path toward the river park.

The fun of the stealth drill immediately evaporated.

As Ren slipped into the perimeter of the park, sticking to the deep shadows of the towering oak trees, a profound, suffocating silence settled over the area. The ambient noise of the city seemed to mute itself. There were no joggers. There were no children playing. It was a statistical dead zone.

Ren scaled a thick, knotted oak tree near the center of the park, using a silent, explosive muscle-up to hoist himself onto a sturdy branch fifteen feet above the ground. From here, he had a perfect tactical view of the central clearing and the dry, concrete fountain where Issei and Yuma had come to a halt.

Then, it hit him.

The same violent, icy dread he had felt on the rooftop two days ago slammed into his chest, but this time, it was magnified a hundredfold. Ren’s breath hitched. His nervous system screamed in primal panic, every hair on his arms standing on end. The air itself felt heavy, thick with a cold, metallic pressure that tasted like copper in the back of his throat.

Down in the clearing, the shy, blushing demeanor of Yuma Amano vanished.

She turned her back to Issei, her shoulders dropping the forced human posture. The temperature in the park seemed to plummet by ten degrees.

"Hey, Issei..." her voice echoed, carrying a bizarre, echoing distortion that sent a violent shiver down Ren’s spine. "Can I ask you a favor?"

"S-Sure! Anything!" Issei stammered, entirely oblivious to the lethal shift in the atmosphere.

Yuma turned around. Her eyes were no longer those of a shy high school girl. They were flat, predatory, and glowing with an unnatural, violent light.

"Would you die for me?"

Up in the tree, Ren’s heart stopped. The shadows around Yuma began to warp, and a sickeningly bright, purplish-black light began to coalesce in the palm of her hand, emitting a high-pitched, humming frequency that made Ren’s eardrums throb.

This wasn't a game anymore. The physics of reality were snapping right in front of him.

Ren didn't think. He didn't hesitate. He bypassed his conscious mind entirely, feeding every ounce of oxygen in his lungs into his fast-twitch muscle fibers, and shifted his weight forward on the branch to launch himself into the abyss.

#### Sub Section 3: The Fatal Inaccuracy

The fabric of reality did not just bend in the park clearing; it shattered.

As the sickening, high-frequency hum of the purplish-black light intensified, the very clothes on Yuma Amano's body disintegrated into a storm of black feathers. For a singular, disorienting microsecond, her human disguise fell away completely. The dissolution revealed the pale, unblemished curves of her naked form, granting Issei’s perverted, hormone-addled brain a sudden, vivid flash of her breasts and bare skin.

Issei’s eyes widened, a dopey, confused blush fighting its way through his confusion. But the adolescent fantasy died instantly.

From her back, a pair of massive, pitch-black wings violently erupted, tearing through the air with a sound like snapping sails. The purplish light in her hand solidified into a crackling, lethal javelin of pure, weaponized radiance. The blush drained from Issei’s face, replaced by a hollow, paralyzing terror. His knees locked. His jaw went slack. The primal, undeniable presence of a Fallen Angel standing over him short-circuited his nervous system entirely.

Raynare smiled, a cruel, mocking twist of her lips, and drew the spear of light back, aiming directly for Issei’s heart.

From the canopy above, a shadow detached itself from the oak tree.

Ren did not shout. He did not issue a heroic battle cry. He weaponized gravity, free-falling fifteen feet and converting the raw kinetic energy of his descent into a singular, devastating point of impact.

Just as Raynare drove her arm forward to release the spear, Ren’s flying kick slammed squarely into the side of her skull.

The kinetic transfer was massive. The sheer, unexpected physical force of a hundred-and-sixty-pound human dropping from the sky broke her posture entirely. Raynare shrieked, the light spear dissolving into sparks as she was violently thrown off her feet. She skidded across the dry, cracked concrete of the fountain base, her black wings flailing in a frantic, uncoordinated mess before she crashed into the stone rim.

Ren hit the ground rolling, dissolving the shock of the landing through his shoulders and springing immediately back to his feet. He dropped into a low, orthodox fighting stance, his hands raised, his eyes locked on the crumpled heap of black feathers.

"Ren?!" Issei screamed, his voice cracking into a high-pitched wheeze as he collapsed backward onto the grass. "What—how—what the hell is that?!"

"Get up, Issei!" Ren barked, his voice tight, his eyes never leaving the Fallen Angel. "On your feet! Move!"

Before Issei could even attempt to command his paralyzed legs, the pile of black feathers at the fountain exploded outward. Raynare rose, her neck cracking sickeningly as she snapped her head back into place. A thin line of blood trickled from the corner of her mouth. Her eyes, now glowing with a furious, unhinged crimson light, locked onto Ren.

"A human?" Raynare hissed, her voice vibrating with supernatural malice. She wiped the blood from her lip, her expression twisting into absolute disgust. "You filthy, mundane little insect. You dare touch me?"

"You telegraphed your throw," Ren said, his neuro-somatic breathing forcing his heart rate to remain steady despite the suffocating demonic pressure crushing the air out of his lungs. "Drop the wings and the light show, and your footwork is garbage."

Raynare snarled, the air around her warping as she lunged forward. She did not run; she moved with a sudden, localized burst of acceleration that defied human physics.

Ren engaged.

For the first four seconds, Ren’s obsessive, grueling weekend grinds validated themselves. Raynare was vastly stronger and impossibly fast, but she was arrogant, wild, and completely lacked formal combat discipline. She threw a sweeping, decapitating hook. Ren slipped under it, pivoting on his back foot, and drove a precise, neuro-somatically enhanced liver punch into her exposed side. He felt the impact reverberate up his forearm. She recoiled, throwing a backhand strike. Ren parried it away, shifting his center of gravity to deliver a snapping inside leg kick to her leading knee.

He was technically out-striking a Fallen Angel. His martial arts foundation was unequivocally superior to her brawling.

But Ren was not the anomaly he needed to be. In another timeline, in the lost annals of Route 0, there existed a human named Fajar. Even without supernatural enhancement, Fajar’s martial arts were so profoundly transcendent—so impossibly perfect in their geometric execution and spatial dominance—that Raynare and her entire squad of Fallen Angels could not lay a single finger on him. Fajar had dismantled them through pure, god-like technique.

Ren was not Fajar.

Ren was a teenager with a fragmented, self-taught fighting style, operating on mortal hardware that was already redlining.

The turning point happened in a fraction of a second. It was exactly like high-level chess: against a vastly superior engine, a single inaccuracy does not just lose an exchange; it forces a checkmate.

Raynare feinted a high strike. Ren calculated the parry window based on human muscle-twitch speeds. He shifted his weight to the right to slip the blow. But Raynare’s supernatural physiology ignored momentum. She canceled the strike mid-air, reversing her kinetic energy instantly, and drove a brutal, localized burst of speed directly into Ren’s guard.

It was a catastrophic miscalculation.

Her knee slammed into Ren’s midsection with the force of a speeding compact car.

The sound of three ribs splintering echoed sharply across the park. Ren’s eyes widened, the air exploding from his lungs in a mist of blood and saliva. His neuro-somatic pain suppression shattered instantly under the sheer, localized trauma. Before his brain could even process the agony, Raynare grabbed him by the throat, hoisting him completely off the ground.

"Technique," Raynare whispered, her eyes burning into his, "means nothing against a god."

She casually casually backhanded him across the chest. The impact lifted Ren off his feet entirely, launching him through the air like a discarded ragdoll. He flew twenty feet backward, crashing violently through the thick wooden slats of a park bench before tumbling to a halt in the dark, damp grass near the edge of the clearing.

Ren lay completely paralyzed. The world spun in a sickening vortex of agony.
