He tried to command his body to rise, to push up onto his hands, but his nervous system was flooded with critical failure signals. He could only turn his head, his vision swimming, tasting the hot, metallic tang of his own blood pooling in his mouth.

Raynare did not even look at him again. He was a broken toy. She turned her attention back to her actual target.

"Ren..." Issei whispered, crawling backward across the grass, tears streaming down his face as he looked from his broken friend to the approaching Fallen Angel. "Stay away! Just take me! Leave him alone!"

"Oh, don't worry. I'm leaving him to bleed out," Raynare purred, her footsteps completely silent against the concrete. A fresh spear of crackling light materialized in her hand, illuminating the clearing in a sickly purple glow. "But you, Issei... you have something inside you that my superiors want gone. It’s nothing personal."

"Wait, please—"

Raynare drove the spear downward.

The sound of the light blade piercing flesh and sinking deep into the earth was sickeningly wet and decisive. Issei’s back arched violently, a choked, gurgling gasp escaping his lips. His hands grabbed desperately at the shaft of light protruding from his chest before his strength gave out, his arms dropping limply to his sides. A massive pool of crimson blood began to rapidly expand beneath him, soaking into the dry soil.

Raynare pulled her hand away, letting the spear dissolve into harmless motes of light, leaving only the gaping, fatal wound behind.

She looked down at Issei’s lifeless eyes for a brief moment, brushing a speck of dirt from her shoulder. Without another word, she unfurled her massive black wings, kicking off the ground with a concussive shockwave that sent dead leaves spiraling into the air. In a matter of seconds, she was swallowed by the dark autumn sky, vanishing completely.

Silence rushed back into the park, heavy and absolute, save for the ragged, shallow wheezing of a broken tactician bleeding in the dark, forced to watch his friend die.

#### Sub Section 4: The Crimson Divide

Through the blurry, blood-stained lens of Ren’s fading vision, the world reduced itself to the agonizing sound of his own shallow breathing and the wet, rhythmic dripping of Issei’s blood onto the concrete.

Ten yards away, Issei lay on his back in the center of the growing crimson pool. His chest heaved in violent, erratic spasms. His hands twitched at his sides, lacking the strength to even clutch the gaping hole in his chest.

As the cold reality of death closed in around him, Issei’s mind began to unravel. He didn't think about his parents, or his grades, or the video games sitting unfinished on his desk. As his eyes locked onto the pool of his own blood reflecting the dim streetlights, a single, hyper-fixated memory floated to the surface of his dying brain.

He remembered the old school building.

Just a few days prior, after getting brutally chased off by the Kendo club, Issei had looked up at the decaying wooden windows of the Occult Research Club. He had seen a girl standing there. A beautiful, untouchable upperclassman with hair so vibrantly red it seemed to burn against the dreary backdrop of the school.

"Red..." Issei gurgled weakly, his eyes glazing over as he stared at the blood soaking his hands. "It’s so red... but it’s not... not as beautiful as her hair..."

Ren gritted his teeth, tasting copper as he tried to force his shattered ribs to expand. He needed to move. He needed to drag himself over there and apply pressure to the wound. But his nervous system was screaming, completely locking his limbs in a state of traumatic paralysis.

"If I'm gonna die..." Issei whimpered, a pathetic, tragic tear slipping down his cheek. "I don't want to die on the ground... If I have to go... I at least wanted to die with my face... buried in the soft breasts... of a beautiful girl..."

It was the most absurd, classically Issei Hyoudou final wish imaginable. Yet, as the words left his mouth, a shift occurred in the atmosphere.

The small, elegant paper flyer that the blonde girl had slipped onto Issei’s desk the previous morning had fallen from his jacket pocket during the attack. It lay directly in the center of the expanding pool of blood. As Issei’s lifeblood soaked the parchment, the strange, symmetrical crest printed on it began to emit a faint, pulsing light.

Then, the park exploded in crimson.

A massive, intricate magic circle, glowing with a deep, blood-red luminescence, violently etched itself into the air directly above Issei. The ambient temperature spiked. The sheer demonic pressure radiating from the crest was so heavy that Ren felt his lungs physically compress, his instincts screaming at him to flee a predator far older and vastly more terrifying than the Fallen Angel.

From the center of the crimson light, a figure materialized.

The first thing Ren saw was the hair—a cascading waterfall of profound, immaculate crimson that seemed to absorb the light around it. She stepped out of the magic circle, her tall, elegant frame wrapped in the Kuoh Academy uniform. Her blue-green eyes swept over the blood-soaked clearing with the calm, detached authority of a queen surveying her domain.

Rias Gremory had arrived.

She knelt gracefully beside Issei, seemingly oblivious to the blood soaking into her pristine skirt. She reached out, gently caressing Issei's pale cheek.

"You are the one who called me?" Rias’s voice was smooth, carrying a hypnotic, aristocratic cadence that echoed through the empty park.

Issei could only let out a weak, rattling breath in response.

Rias looked at his chest, her eyes narrowing slightly as she sensed the lingering divine magic of the light spear, and the faint, dormant pulse of the Sacred Gear hidden deep within Issei's soul.

"For a human to possess such an artifact... What a pity," Rias murmured softly, a small, sorrowful smile touching her lips. "But I cannot let you perish here. Since you are going to die anyway, I will take your life."

She leaned closer, pressing a hand over the fatal wound. A brilliant, crimson aura flared around her palm.

"From this moment on, you will live for me," Rias declared, her voice ringing with absolute, demonic sovereignty. "You will be my servant."

Twenty feet away, hidden in the pitch-black shadows of the shattered wooden bench, Ren lay completely immobilized. His throat was choked with blood, preventing him from calling out. He watched in agonizing silence as the school's most untouchable princess gathered Issei into her arms.

Rias didn't look toward the bench. She didn't scan the perimeter. To a high-class Devil responding to a direct summoning pact, the localized area was just an empty stage for her new Pawn. The presence of a mundane human bleeding out in the dark foliage didn't even register on her supernatural radar. Why would it? Ren had no magic. He had no Sacred Gear. He was just a baseline mortal whose biological signature was fading into the background noise of the city.

The crimson magic circle flared one final, blinding time.

When the light faded, Issei and Rias were gone. The park was entirely empty.

The silence that followed was deafening.

Ren lay in the cold, damp grass for a long time. The freezing autumn wind whipped across his face. Slowly, painfully, he forced his eyes open. He looked at the spot where his friend had died, and where a miracle had descended from the heavens to save him.

He didn't feel relief.

A dark, venomous heat began to spread from the pit of his stomach, burning away the icy shock of his injuries. It was a terrifying, suffocating wave of pure, unadulterated bitterness.

He had dedicated his entire weekend to studying anatomy, optimizing his kinetic output, and mastering the geometry of combat. He had tracked a Fallen Angel through the city. He had thrown his mortal body into the path of a divine weapon, breaking his bones and rupturing his organs just to buy his friend three seconds of time. He had fought, and bled, and pushed his human limitations to the absolute brink.

And for what?

He was left to die in the dirt, entirely unnoticed and utterly discarded. Meanwhile, Issei—who had done absolutely nothing but walk blindly into a trap and cry about breasts—had been rewarded with a magical resurrection and the embrace of a demonic princess.

*The universe doesn't reward effort,* Ren realized, his fingers digging into the muddy earth as a savage, toxic Envy finally took root in his soul. *It only rewards the chosen.*

Ren squeezed his eyes shut, engaging his neuro-somatic conditioning not for training, but for raw, spiteful survival. He mentally visualized the bleeding in his side, forcing his blood vessels to constrict. He blocked the nerve receptors screaming from his shattered ribs. He refused to die here as a forgotten extra.

With a ragged, bloody gasp, Ren rolled onto his stomach. He planted his elbows into the dirt, his knuckles white, and began the agonizing, solitary crawl back into the dark alleys of the city. The seed of darkness had been planted, and it was being watered by his own blood.

### Section 4: The Broken Pattern

#### Sub Section 1: The Phantom Limbs of Memory

Issei woke up screaming.

He vaulted upright in his bed, his hands frantically clawing at his chest, fully expecting to feel a gaping, blood-soaked hole where his heart should be. Instead, his fingers met the soft, unblemished cotton of his pajama shirt. He gasped for air, his chest heaving, sweat dripping down his forehead.

He tore his shirt open. His skin was perfectly smooth. There was no blood. There was no puncture wound. There was no Fallen Angel with black wings laughing at him.

"A dream..." Issei choked out, collapsing back onto his pillows, his heart hammering against his ribs. "It was just... a really messed up nightmare."

But as he swung his legs out of bed and stood up, a sudden, violent throb flared in his left arm. He grabbed his forearm, wincing. It felt like a phantom heartbeat trapped beneath his skin—a deep, unnatural heat radiating from his bones. He shook it off, attributing it to sleeping on his arm wrong, but the bizarre sensations didn't stop there.

Walking to school that morning, the world felt fundamentally out of focus. It wasn't dull; it was terrifyingly sharp. Issei could hear the individual rustle of oak leaves three streets away. He could hear the hum of the electrical wires above him. The morning sunlight seemed aggressively bright, forcing him to squint. As he walked through the front gates of Kuoh Academy, he even noticed a tiny, white-haired first-year girl sitting on a bench near the shoe lockers, quietly eating a melon bread. She paused mid-bite, her blank, golden eyes locking onto him for a fraction of a second before he looked away, thoroughly unnerved.

The moment he slid open the door to classroom 2-B, his disorientation was violently interrupted.

"Traitor!" Matsuda roared, instantly abandoning his desk and pointing an accusing finger.

Motohama was right behind him, pushing up his taped glasses with a menacing glare. "Report, Hyoudou. Detail every miserable second of your Sunday. Did she laugh at your outfit and walk away, or did she politely excuse herself to the restroom and never return?"

"Guys, you won't believe it," Issei stammered, dropping his bag onto his desk. He rubbed the back of his neck, laughing nervously. "I think the stress of the date gave me a literal fever. I had the craziest nightmare."

"A nightmare?" Matsuda snorted. "You mean realizing you were on a date with a 3D woman?"

"No, I mean she turned into a monster!" Issei blurted out, waving his hands defensively. "I'm serious! We were at the park, and suddenly she grew these giant black bird wings, and her clothes vanished, and she pulled out this glowing spear and stabbed me right through the chest! It felt so real. I thought I was actually dead."

A beat of silence hung over the desk.

Then, Matsuda and Motohama erupted into hysterical laughter.

"A monster!" Matsuda howled, clutching his stomach. "He got dumped so hard his brain had to invent a fantasy monster to protect his ego!"

"Fascinating psychological defense mechanism," Motohama chuckled, wiping a tear from his eye. "To avoid the reality of a girl rejecting him, his subconscious hallucinated an assassination attempt. Truly pathetic, Issei."

"Shut up! I'm telling the truth, it felt real!" Issei yelled, though his face flushed bright red. He looked around the classroom, desperate for some backup. "Hey, Ren, tell these guys I'm not—"

Issei stopped.

He blinked. The desk in the second row, right next to the window, was completely empty.

"Where's Ren?" Issei asked, his voice suddenly dropping its comedic pitch.

Matsuda shrugged, wiping his eyes. "Hasn't shown up. Homeroom is about to start. Pretty weird, right? Ren never misses a day."

A cold, paralyzing spike of ice drove itself straight into the base of Issei’s spine. The ambient noise of the classroom—the laughing, the scraping of chairs, the distant school bell—suddenly muted itself, replaced by a high-pitched ringing in his ears.

*Ren never misses a day.*

The memory hit him not as a hazy dream, but as a visceral, high-definition flashback.

He didn't just remember Yuma growing wings. He remembered a shadow dropping from the sky. He remembered Ren—Ren, who had supposedly just been walking home—flying out of the trees and kicking the monster in the face.

*CRACK.*

Issei gasped, stumbling backward and knocking his chair over with a loud clatter. The sound of Ren’s ribs shattering echoed in his skull. He could suddenly smell the copper tang of blood. He could see Ren’s broken body flying through the air and crashing through the wooden park bench, lying completely paralyzed in the dark grass, bleeding out while Issei was stabbed.

"Issei? Hey, man, you look like you're gonna puke," Matsuda said, his smile faltering.

"It wasn't a dream," Issei whispered, his eyes wide with absolute horror. He looked down at his left arm, which was suddenly pulsing with that same, strange heat. He looked at his unblemished chest.

If it wasn't a dream, and Issei was perfectly fine... then where was Ren?

Without another word to his bewildered friends, Issei grabbed his bag, spun on his heel, and bolted out of the classroom before the teacher even walked in.

#### Sub Section 2: The House of the Wounded

Issei’s lungs burned as he sprinted away from Kuoh Academy, his feet blindly carrying him back to the scene of the crime.

When he reached the river park, the morning sun was illuminating the central clearing in harsh, unforgiving light. In his memory, the date with Yuma Amano had been a dream come true, right up until the sky darkened and she asked him to die for her. Normally, he might have lingered by the fountain to mourn the brutal betrayal of his first love.

But his heightened eyes immediately caught something else.

The heavy wooden bench at the edge of the clearing was completely shattered. Thick wooden slats were splintered in half, and the iron armrest was bent at a sickening angle. Just beyond it, in the damp grass, Issei spotted dried, rust-colored stains coating the dirt.

It wasn't a nightmare. The physical evidence of Ren’s desperate, violent intervention was right there.

Panic completely overwrote his heartbreak. Issei spun around and sprinted toward the residential district, ignoring the throbbing ache in his left arm. He navigated the narrow, winding streets until he reached a worn, two-story apartment complex where Ren lived alone.

Issei took the metal stairs two at a time and hammered his fists against door number 204.

"Ren! Ren, are you in there?!" Issei yelled, his voice cracking. "Open up, please!"

For a long, agonizing minute, there was no response. Then, the deadbolt clicked.

The door opened just a few inches. Issei pushed his way inside, the words of apology tumbling out of his mouth before he even registered what he was looking at.

Ren was seated on the edge of a cheap futon, illuminated only by the pale light filtering through the drawn window blinds. He was a mess. His torso was heavily wrapped in thick white medical bandages, the fabric stained with faint, dark patches of dried blood over his left ribcage. His skin was pale, bathed in a cold sweat, and his breathing was agonizingly shallow, strictly regulated by his neuro-somatic focus to keep his shattered ribs from shifting.

"Close the door, Issei," Ren said. His voice was hoarse, stripped of its usual sarcastic warmth. "You're being loud."

Issei slammed the door shut, his legs giving out as he dropped to his knees on the tatami mat. "Ren... oh god. You're alive. The bench... I saw the bench at the park. Was it... was the nightmare real? Did she actually—"

"It wasn't a nightmare," Ren interrupted, his dark eyes locking onto Issei. "She didn't just attack you, Issei. She killed you. I watched her drive a light spear straight through your heart."

Issei choked on his breath. He clawed at his shirt, pulling it open to reveal his completely unblemished chest. "But look! I don't have a scratch! I woke up in my bed! How am I alive if she killed me?!"

"Because you hit the statistical jackpot," Ren replied, a cold, dark edge creeping into his tone. He leaned back slightly against the wall, wincing as a spasm of pain shot through his side. "After you bled out on the concrete, a massive, crimson magic circle appeared right over your body. Rias Gremory stepped out of it."

Issei’s eyes widened to the size of saucers. "The President of the Occult Research Club? Wait..." His mind flashed back to the blonde girl in the hallway, the strange flyer on his desk, and the final, fading memory of a beautiful girl with crimson hair standing over him in the dark. *I will take your life. You will live for me.*

"The flyer..." Issei whispered, staring at his hands. "I had that weird paper in my pocket. She told me I belonged to her now."

"Exactly," Ren said, his gaze analytical and unblinking. "Whatever that paper was, your blood activated it. She performed some kind of ritual, resurrected you, and teleported you both out of there. You got the ultimate magical bailout."

"And you?" Issei asked, tears welling up in his eyes as he looked at Ren’s bandaged, broken body. "Did she heal you too? Did she bring you back here?"

A heavy, suffocating silence filled the small apartment.

Ren looked away, staring at the dust motes dancing in the sliver of sunlight slipping through the blinds. The memory of the cold mud, the agonizing pain, and the absolute insignificance of his own existence flared in his chest, burning hotter than his physical wounds.

"No," Ren said softly, a bitter, venomous smile pulling at the corner of his mouth. "I got thrown twenty feet into a bench. I was outside the radius of your little miracle. She didn't even glance in my direction. I spent three hours crawling back to this apartment through the dirt, choking on my own blood, while you got carried off by a demonic princess."

"Ren... I'm so sorry," Issei sobbed, bowing his head to the floor. "You tried to save me. You broke your body for me, and I left you behind."

"You didn't leave me behind, Issei. The world did," Ren stated flatly.

As Issei continued to cry, Ren’s gamer-wired brain instinctively shifted into diagnostic mode, analyzing the boy weeping on his floor.

Issei looked the same, but he wasn't. Ren’s trained senses picked up the subtle, impossible anomalies. Issei’s resting body temperature was too high, radiating a faint, localized heat that hadn't been there on Friday. The micro-expressions of his musculature were different. The very presence he projected into the room felt dense, carrying a faint echo of the crimson magic that had saturated the park.

Issei wasn't human anymore. He had crossed the threshold, completely bypassing the grueling, agonizing grind of mortal training through sheer dumb luck and a hidden stat modifier.

Ren closed his eyes, inhaling slowly through his nose. He had survived the night, but the baseline reality of their friendship had irrevocably fractured. Issei was a supernatural being now, and Ren was just the collateral damage left in the dirt.

#### Sub Section 3: The Watchers and the Fedora

Issei wiped his eyes, bowing his head one last time before stepping out of the apartment. He needed air. He needed to process the impossible reality that his normal life was over, and the agonizing guilt that his survival had come at the cost of Ren’s broken body.

Ren sat alone in the dim room, listening to the heavy footsteps of his friend receding down the exterior metal walkway. He closed his eyes, managing his breathing. He didn't hate Issei. Hating Issei would be like hating a lottery winner for buying a lucky ticket. Issei was just a foolish, incredibly fortunate idiot. No, Ren’s bitterness was entirely reserved for the cold, mechanical unfairness of the world itself—a world that discarded the relentless grind of a mortal while handing divine miracles to the oblivious.

Outside, the late afternoon shadows were stretching across the concrete courtyard of the apartment complex. Issei barely made it to the bottom of the metal staircase when the temperature plummeted.

"I thought I sensed a lingering anomaly," a grating, arrogant voice echoed from the shadows of the alleyway.

Issei froze. He turned slowly, his newly heightened senses picking up the sound of leather shoes against asphalt.

A tall man stepped into the fading sunlight. He wore a sharp trench coat and a fedora pulled low over his eyes, a menacing smirk stretching across his face. But it wasn't his clothes that made Issei’s blood run cold—it was the massive, pitch-black wings sprouting from his back, identical to Yuma's.

"Dohnaseek," the man introduced himself, tipping his fedora. "I was sent to verify a glitch in our extermination report. It seems Raynare was sloppy. You're actually alive."

"No... not again," Issei whimpered, stumbling backward.

Dohnaseek didn't waste time with a theatrical monologue. He raised his hand, a crackling spear of yellow light coalescing in his palm, and hurled it with blinding speed.

The spear caught Issei squarely in the shoulder, pinning him against the metal railing of the staircase with a sickening crunch. Issei screamed, squeezing his eyes shut and waiting for the cold, absolute void of death to take him a second time.

But the void didn't come.

His shoulder burned with agonizing pain, and blood soaked his uniform, but his heart kept beating. His new Devil physiology, completely unknown to him, violently rejected the fatal shock. He was bleeding, but he wasn't dying.

Dohnaseek blinked, his smirk vanishing into a scowl. "Fascinating. Your physical density has fundamentally shifted. No matter. I'll just aim for the head." He began to manifest a second spear.

*CLANG. THWACK.*

Two heavy kitchen knives and a stainless-steel fork ricocheted violently off Dohnaseek’s raised forearm, the sudden kinetic impacts breaking his concentration. The Fallen Angel cursed, taking a half-step back as the silverware clattered onto the concrete.

"Get in here!"

Issei opened his eyes. Ren was leaning heavily against the doorframe of his second-floor apartment, his face deathly pale, his right hand still raised from the throw. Despite the agonizing pain radiating through his shattered ribs, Ren hadn't hesitated. The world might have left him behind, but he wasn't about to watch his idiot best friend get murdered twice in one weekend.

Ren grabbed the collar of Issei’s jacket and violently hauled him over the threshold, collapsing onto the floor with a wet, ragged cough as the exertion tore at his internal injuries.

"Ren!" Issei cried out, clutching his bleeding shoulder as he scrambled to help his friend.

"You're an idiot, Issei," Ren panted, a pained, breathless chuckle escaping his lips as he held his bandaged side. "If you can't fight... you have to learn how to run."

Down in the courtyard, Dohnaseek sneered, aiming his newly formed light spear directly at the open doorway of apartment 204. "A mundane human interfering again? How utterly pathetic. I'll vaporize you both."

Before the Fallen Angel could release the projectile, the air pressure in the courtyard violently inverted.

A massive, blood-red magic circle burst into existence on the concrete directly between Dohnaseek and the staircase. The sheer demonic aura radiating from it was so dense it physically rattled the windows of the apartment complex. Ren forced himself up onto one knee, his gamer instincts screaming as he recognized the same suffocating pressure from the park.

From the crimson light stepped Rias Gremory.

This time, she wasn't alone. Flanking her was the petite, white-haired girl Issei had seen at the shoe lockers, a tall, handsome blonde boy with a knight's posture, and a beautiful, raven-haired upperclassman who was smiling with terrifying, sadistic glee.

"This territory belongs to the House of Gremory," Rias announced, her voice echoing with sovereign authority. She glared at Dohnaseek, her crimson hair flaring with magical energy. "You have immense nerve, Fallen Angel, attacking my servant in my own domain."

Dohnaseek lowered his spear, his arrogant demeanor instantly dissolving into a tense, calculated grimace. He scanned the four Devils standing before him, doing the tactical math in his head.

"Rias Gremory," Dohnaseek spat, taking a slow step backward into the shadows. "So, you claimed the boy. How tedious. I have no interest in starting a war with the Underworld over a single human anomaly. Consider this a temporary retreat."

With a powerful flap of his black wings, Dohnaseek launched himself into the sky, vanishing into the twilight.

Rias sighed, dismissing her magical aura before turning her gaze up to the second-floor walkway. Her blue-green eyes locked onto Issei, who was clutching his bleeding shoulder, and then shifted, for the very first time, to the battered, blood-stained human kneeling beside him.

Ren stared back at the Crimson Ruin. He couldn't fight her. He couldn't even stand up. But as he looked at the flawless, magical beings who had effortlessly chased away a god, the seed of Envy in his chest finally sprouted, locking his resolve into something cold, permanent, and terrifyingly ambitious.

#### Sub Section 4: The Vanguard of Truth

Rias ascended the metal staircase with effortless grace, her peerage falling in seamlessly behind her. The blonde knight, Kiba, offered a polite, disarming smile, while the raven-haired Akeno covered her mouth with a delicate hand, her eyes dancing with quiet amusement. The small, white-haired girl—Koneko—remained entirely expressionless, her golden eyes locking onto Ren with the calculating gaze of a seasoned brawler.

Rias knelt beside Issei, pressing her glowing hand over his bleeding shoulder. A warm, crimson aura enveloped the wound, and within seconds, the torn flesh knitted itself back together without a trace of a scar.

"President..." Issei stammered, his face flushing crimson as he stared at the beautiful upperclassman saving his life for a second time.

"You are my cute servant now, Issei," Rias said gently, withdrawing her hand. "It is my duty to protect you." Her gaze then shifted to the pale, battered boy leaning heavily against the doorframe. "And you... you threw mortal steel at a Fallen Angel. That was incredibly foolish, but undeniably brave."

"He didn't just do it now!" Issei blurted out, scrambling to his feet. "President, this is my best friend, Ren. Yesterday at the park, when Yuma—I mean, Raynare—attacked me... Ren jumped out of a tree and kicked her in the face! He fought her to save me. That's why he's so hurt!"

Rias blinked, her aristocratic composure slipping for a fraction of a second. Akeno lowered her hand, her sadistic smile fading into genuine surprise, while Kiba and Koneko exchanged a swift, silent glance.

"You engaged a Fallen Angel in close combat?" Rias asked, her voice dropping a cautious octave. She stepped over the threshold into Ren’s apartment, her eyes scanning his blood-stained bandages. "As a completely ordinary human?"

"I observed her walking through the shopping district," Ren stated, his voice calm and entirely devoid of bravado. He didn't puff out his chest or downplay the danger; he simply delivered the raw data. "Her gait was mechanically unnatural—she was overcompensating for unseen mass on her back, which turned out to be the wings. I tailed you both to the park. When she initiated her attack sequence, I calculated a vertical drop from the canopy to disrupt her physical posture and interrupt her throw. It worked, temporarily."

Ren took a slow, shallow breath, wincing slightly. "I engaged her in standard striking range. My technique was cleaner, but I miscalculated her kinetic reversal speed. She ignored physical momentum, bypassed my guard, and shattered my ribs. I survived because she deemed me irrelevant."

Silence hung in the small apartment. Rias stared at Ren, her blue-green eyes wide. He wasn't crying. He wasn't hysterical. He was analyzing his near-death experience like a chess player dissecting a lost match.

Rias stepped closer and gently placed her hand against Ren’s bandaged side. "To possess such a clinical mind, and to act on it without magic... you are a rare anomaly, Ren."

A soft, emerald-green light radiated from her palm, soaking through the bandages and deep into Ren's torso. In an instant, the agonizing, grinding pressure of his fractured ribs vanished. The ruptured blood vessels sealed, the bone fragments fused, and the burning pain in his lungs evaporated. Ren took a deep, unrestricted breath for the first time in twenty-four hours. His neuro-somatic training had allowed him to endure the pain, but this—this was a total circumvention of biology. It was both a miraculous relief and a quiet, mocking reminder of the vast gulf between mortal effort and demonic power.

"Thank you," Ren said simply, offering a respectful nod.

Rias motioned for them to sit. For the next ten minutes, the President of the Occult Research Club laid out the hidden architecture of the world. She explained the eternal stalemate between the Three Factions: the Angels of Heaven, the Fallen Angels of the Grigori, and the Devils of the Underworld. She revealed her true identity as the heiress to the Gremory clan, and explained the Evil Pieces system.

"You were targeted, Issei, because you possess a Sacred Gear—an artifact created by God," Rias explained, her tone grave. "The Fallen Angels deemed it a threat. I resurrected you using a Pawn piece. You are no longer human; you are a Devil of my household."

Issei looked at his hands, overwhelmed, terrified, yet strangely captivated by the beautiful woman who had claimed him.

Rias then turned her attention back to Ren. "Normally, it is our policy to erase the memories of humans who inadvertently witness our world. The truth often brings severe trauma, paranoia, and unnecessary danger. If you wish, I can use my magic to wipe these events from your mind. You can wake up tomorrow believing Issei simply had a strange weekend, free of this burden."

Ren didn't even hesitate.

"I appreciate the offer, President, but I decline," Ren replied smoothly. "A memory wipe might be peaceful, but ignorance is a tactical blind spot. If there are Fallen Angels throwing light spears in my city, I prefer to know exactly where the threats are coming from. The truth doesn't bother me."

Akeno chuckled softly, her eyes gleaming with renewed interest. "My, my. What a pragmatic human."

"Very well," Rias said, respecting his agency with a solemn nod. "I will not erase your memory. But you must swear absolute secrecy, Ren. And understand that without a Sacred Gear, you are stepping into a crossfire completely unprotected."

"Understood," Ren said.

He stood up, rolling his newly healed shoulders, and walked over to Issei. He looked down at his best friend—the pervert, the slacker, the brand-new demonic Pawn—and smiled warmly. It wasn't a mask; it was genuine affection. The toxic envy was there, buried deep in the bedrock of his soul, but it was dormant for now. Issei hadn't asked for this, and he had nearly died twice.

Ren reached out and slapped Issei firmly on the shoulder.

"Well, look at you," Ren laughed softly, his tone shifting back to the familiar, grounded banter of high school boys. "You actually did it, Issei. You managed to get a beautiful upperclassman in your life, even if you had to die to sign the club registry. Congratulations, man."

Issei looked up, his eyes welling with tears again, and offered a weak, grateful grin. "Thanks, Ren. I guess... I guess my rom-com really did start."

#### Sub Section 5: The Mortal Arsenal

The door clicked shut, leaving Ren in the sudden, suffocating silence of his apartment.

Through the window, he watched Issei walk away, surrounded by the beautiful, terrifying elite of Kuoh Academy. They were a flock of immortals returning to their hidden world, casually leaving the human realm behind. Ren stood completely still in his living room, his hands resting on his fully healed ribs.

He was grateful to be alive. He was glad Issei was safe. But beneath that genuine relief, a cold, desperate pragmatism began to claw at his chest. The power scaling of his reality had just been shattered. He was a level-one human living in an endgame zone, and the grace of a Devil princess wasn't a sustainable survival strategy.

That night, Ren didn't sleep. He sat in the center of his dark bedroom, crossing his legs and closing his eyes, diving deeper into his neuro-somatic conditioning than he ever had before.

Rias’s healing magic had been a humiliating display of power, but it had also provided him with an unintended gift: a flawless, tactile blueprint. When the demonic energy had flooded his system, Ren’s hyper-observant brain had tracked exactly how it stimulated his cellular regeneration and muted his pain receptors. He used that memory as a map. He focused on his breathing, visualizing his nervous system not as a chaotic web of impulses, but as a clean, manageable circuit board. He isolated the residual phantom aches in his side and consciously dialed them down to zero. He routed oxygen to his healing tissues with smooth, frictionless precision. By sunrise, he had completed his neuro-somatic foundation. His mind and body were finally, truly synchronized.

The next morning, while Issei was undoubtedly getting acquainted with his new demonic duties, Ren began a brutal, analytical post-mortem of his failures.

He stood in front of his full-length mirror, replaying the park fight in his head frame by frame. *Inaccuracy number one,* he thought, shadowboxing the exact sequence against his reflection. *I treated Raynare like a human martial artist bound by gravity and momentum. I struck the liver, assuming a natural flinch response. I threw a leg kick, assuming her center of gravity was tethered to the earth. It wasn't.*

He adjusted his stance, dropping lower, visualizing an opponent capable of mid-air kinetic reversal. He integrated his newly perfected neuro-somatic breathing, forcing his muscles to snap through the combinations with zero wasted energy.

Then, he reviewed the Dohnaseek encounter. Throwing kitchen knives had been a successful distraction, but against a supernatural entity, distractions were just delayed executions. Bare fists and blunt-force trauma were mathematically useless against enemies with reinforced bone density and magical auras. If he was going to survive in Issei's shadow, he needed to weaponize.

Over the next few days, Ren completely overhauled his combat syllabus. He spent hours at his computer, studying the lethal, hyper-efficient knife CQC (Close Quarters Combat) depicted in military action webtoons. He dissected the brutal, reverse-grip slaughter tactics of *Teenage Mercenary* and *God of Blackfield*, and the terrifying, relentless edge-alignment found in *Return of the Bloodthirsty Police*. He bought a dull training karambit and a tactical folding knife, spending his nights in the apartment fluidly drilling parries, disarms, and fatal artery strikes against an imaginary Fallen Angel until his knuckles bled.

But a knife wasn't always available. He needed to be lethal in any environment.

Ren shifted his research to the ruthless, environmental pragmatism of Gray Yeon from *Weak Hero*. He started looking at mundane objects with a terrifying new perspective. A heavy winter coat wasn't clothing; it was a tool to blind an opponent's vision and trap their arms. A ballpoint pen was a makeshift spike for the carotid artery. A handful of dirt, a shattered coffee mug, the sharp edge of a desk—everything became a potential weapon to bridge the massive gap in physical stats.

To tie it all together, he booted up his old *Metal Gear Solid* files. He didn't play for fun; he studied the tactical espionage philosophy. He reviewed the mechanics of sightlines, the art of silencing footsteps, and the core tenets of CQC. He realized that as a mortal, stealth was his absolute greatest asset. He couldn't win a fair fight against a Devil or a Fallen Angel, which meant he must never, ever fight fair.

By the end of the week, Ren returned to the community gym.

He hit the weights with a desperation that bordered on manic. He deadlifted, squatted, and sprinted until his muscles screamed and his vision blurred with exhaustion. But when the dreaded wall of lactic acid buildup hit him—the physical limit that forces every human to stop—Ren simply closed his eyes.

He engaged his neuro-somatic control. He visualized the burning acid in his muscle fibers and manually commanded his circulatory system to flush it out. He slowed his heart rate, flooded his body with hyper-oxygenated blood, and forced his pain receptors to shut down.

When he opened his eyes thirty seconds later, the crippling fatigue was gone. He walked back to the squat rack, added another twenty pounds to the bar, and kept grinding.

He was still just a human. But he was building a monster in the dark.

### Section 5: The New Paradigm

#### Sub Section 1: The Death of a Pervert

The rumor mill of Kuoh Academy was a highly efficient machine, but on this particular Monday morning, it was operating at terminal velocity. By 8:15 AM, the whispered reports had reached critical mass, rippling through the hallways and crashing into classroom 2-B like a tidal wave.

Issei Hyoudou, the undisputed king of the perverts, had been spotted walking to school in the company of Rias Gremory.

When Issei finally slid the classroom door open, he looked like a man who had stared into the abyss and found the abyss naked in his bed. He was exhausted, twitchy, and visibly sweating.

He didn't even make it three steps into the room before the execution squad descended.

"Traitor!" Matsuda shrieked, launching himself across the room and grabbing Issei by the collar of his uniform. "Explain yourself, Hyoudou! The entire student body is saying you arrived with President Gremory! Confess your sins to the court!"

Motohama flanked him instantly, adjusting his taped glasses so the lenses caught the morning light in a blinding, sinister glare. "This is a violation of the sacred pact, Issei. We endure the absolute bottom of the social hierarchy together. If you have somehow bypassed the system and interacted with a top-tier idol, the penalty is death by a thousand cuts."

"Guys, keep your voices down!" Issei panicked, waving his hands frantically as the girls in the classroom shot him glares of utter bewilderment. "It’s not what you think! She just... we just bumped into each other at the gates! It was a coincidence!"

"Lies! Deception!" Matsuda wailed, actual tears of bloodshot jealousy forming in his eyes. He looked over his shoulder, desperate for reinforcements. "Ren! Get over here! Hold his legs while we administer justice!"

Ren did not get up.

He remained seated at his desk near the window, his chin resting casually on his hand. He watched the chaotic, cartoonish display of his friends with a calm, unbothered expression. He knew exactly why Issei was with Rias. He knew about the bloody ritual in the park, the demonic reincarnation, and the fact that Issei was now functionally her property. There was absolutely nothing to be jealous of.

"Let the guy breathe, Matsuda," Ren said, his voice entirely void of its usual degenerate enthusiasm. "He had a really rough weekend. If he says it was a coincidence, let it go."

Matsuda froze. Motohama turned his head slowly, his jaw dropping in genuine shock. Issei just stared at Ren with profound, silent gratitude.

"Ren..." Motohama whispered, pointing a trembling finger at him. "Where is your fire? Where is your righteous fury? A member of the Perverted Trio has made contact with the Crimson Ruin, and you are sitting there reading... what is that? *Gray’s Anatomy*?"

Ren smoothly closed the thick, heavy medical textbook he had borrowed from the local library and placed it in his desk. "I'm just tired, Motohama. And honestly, it’s too early in the morning to be screaming about girls."

A strange, unprecedented silence fell over their corner of the classroom.

It wasn't just Matsuda and Motohama who noticed the shift. Several girls sitting a few rows away exchanged genuinely puzzled looks. For the past two years, Ren Nocturnus Veritas had been lumped into the same radioactive category as Issei and the others—a slouched, leering degenerate who spent his afternoons trying to peek into the Kendo club locker room.

But looking at him now, the "pervert aura" was completely gone.

His posture was entirely different. He wasn't slouching; he sat with a straight, grounded spine, his shoulders relaxed but perfectly balanced. His eyes, which usually darted around looking for uniform malfunctions, were focused, sharp, and chillingly calm. The grueling neuro-somatic conditioning and the sheer, traumatic reality of surviving a Fallen Angel had burned the trivial teenage degeneracy right out of his system. He still offered them a friendly, familiar smile, but the energy beneath it was cold, calculating, and fundamentally dangerous.

"Did... did Ren hit his head?" Matsuda whispered loudly to Motohama. "He isn't even looking at the girls' tennis practice out the window."

"A psychological reset," Motohama deduced, adjusting his glasses nervously. "Perhaps the rejection he suffered last week was so catastrophic it shattered his libido entirely. He has ascended to a state of hollow enlightenment."

"I can hear you both," Ren sighed, resting his boots on the crossbar of his desk. "I'm not enlightened. I just have different priorities right now. Sit down before the teacher gets here."

Issei collapsed into his chair, letting out a massive sigh of relief as Matsuda and Motohama begrudgingly backed off. He shot Ren a look that conveyed a million words of thanks. Ren just offered a subtle, reassuring nod. The trio was still intact on the surface, but the underlying reality was undeniable: Ren had quietly, cleanly resigned from being a human joke.

#### Sub Section 2: The Prince’s Escort

The final bell of the day rang, but the usual stampede for the door was abruptly halted by a blinding, blonde anomaly standing at the entrance of classroom 2-B.

Kiba Yuto, the undisputed "Prince" of Kuoh Academy, stood in the doorway with a flawless, disarming smile. The effect was instantaneous. A collective, high-pitched gasp rippled through the female population of the room. Girls stopped packing their bags, blushing furiously as they tried to make eye contact with the handsome knight.

Kiba politely stepped past a group of swooning third-year girls and walked directly toward the back row.

"Hyoudou-kun," Kiba said gently, his voice smooth and perfectly modulated. "The President sent me to collect you. It’s time for club activities."

The romantic tension in the room instantly shattered, replaced by a deafening, collective silence of utter confusion. The girls stared at Issei, their faces contorting in horror as their brains tried to process why the school's most elegant prince was personally escorting the school's most notorious pervert.

Matsuda and Motohama, however, did not experience confusion. They experienced absolute, violent despair.

As Issei nervously slung his bag over his shoulder and followed Kiba out into the hallway, Matsuda fell to his knees, gripping his hair. "It’s real! The rumors are real! He wasn't just walking with her this morning; he’s actually been drafted into the Occult Research Club!"

Motohama slammed his fists onto his desk, his glasses fogging up with righteous fury. "Do you understand what this means, Matsuda?! He is going to be locked in a private room every afternoon with President Gremory, Akeno Himejima, and the school's mascot, Koneko Toujou! He has bypassed the mortal realm and entered the promised land of harems!"

Ren slung his own backpack over his shoulder, looking down at his weeping friends with a mix of pity and analytical curiosity.

"I don't understand," Ren said smoothly, leaning against his desk. "If you guys are so intensely jealous that he gets to hang out in the club room, why don't you just go to the faculty office, fill out a standard club application, and join?"

Motohama stopped crying. He looked up at Ren as if he had just suggested they try to breathe underwater.

"Apply?" Motohama scoffed, pushing his taped glasses up his nose with a dramatic, trembling finger. "Ren, your mind truly has deteriorated. One does not simply *apply* to the Occult Research Club. It is an impenetrable fortress of the elite."

Matsuda nodded vigorously from the floor. "Yeah! Guys have tried it before! If you go up there with an application, President Gremory just smiles at you, says the club is at capacity, and closes the door. And if you try to be stubborn about it, you just... forget why you even wanted to join. It’s like her aura is so intimidating it actually erases your willpower. The whole school knows it's an invite-only society for the absolute top-tiers. We don't even have clearance to touch the doorknob!"

Ren hummed softly, his dark eyes drifting toward the window, looking out across the campus toward the silhouette of the Old School Building.

To the rest of Kuoh Academy, the ORC's exclusivity was just a social phenomenon—a club for beautiful, untouchable idols. But to Ren’s newly calibrated tactical mind, the reality was stark and brutally efficient.

*It’s not a social barrier,* Ren realized, perfectly connecting the dots. *It’s territory management. They reject everyone and use demonic suggestion to wipe the interest from persistent applicants. It ensures no normal humans ever stumble into their base of operations or accidentally witness a magic circle. It's the perfect civilian filter.*

"Well," Ren said, adjusting the strap of his bag and stepping over Matsuda's legs. "I guess Issei just got lucky. See you guys tomorrow."

He left his two friends mourning their ruined high school lives on the classroom floor and walked out into the hallway. The comedic jealousy of the Perverted Trio was a comfortable, familiar noise, but Ren was completely detached from it now. The game had changed, and he had entirely different objectives to pursue.

#### Sub Section 3: Separate Paths

The setting sun bled across the horizon, painting the stone courtyard of Kuoh Academy in deep shades of gold and amber.

Issei Hyoudou walked alongside Kiba Yuto, his steps hesitant as the pair crossed the open grounds toward the shadowed silhouette of the Old School Building. Kiba moved with the effortless grace of a noble, his golden hair catching the dying light, while Issei glanced nervously around, terrified that a Fallen Angel might drop from the sky or that Rias Gremory might suddenly appear to demand another blood oath. He was headed toward his destiny—toward the grand wooden doors of the Occult Research Club, where a steaming shower, a naked Devil princess, and the official start of his supernatural life awaited him.

From the second-floor windows, dozens of eyes watched their departure. The school grounds were alive with a chaotic storm of rumors, spreading like wildfire from the classrooms to the clubhouses.

"Look at Hyoudou... Kiba-sama is actually taking him to the Old Building."
"Is it true? Did President Gremory really invite that pervert into her private club?"
"Gross! What could someone like her possibly see in a creep like him?"

The envy directed at Issei was loud, explosive, and ridiculous. But as the students whispered about the sudden ascent of Kuoh’s luckiest idiot, another rumor was quietly taking root in the hallways—one spoken not with outrage, but with a strange, uncomfortable sense of unease.

"Hey... have you noticed Nocturnus lately?"
"Ren? Yeah... he’s completely different."
"He used to stare at the girls' track team with those other two creeps, but today he just walked past them without even turning his head."
"He looks... dangerous. Did you see his eyes when he talked to Matsuda? It felt like looking at a stranger."

To the students of Kuoh Academy, Ren’s transformation was a mystery—a sudden, trauma-induced personality shift caused by a bad weekend or a secret heartbreak. They believed the slouched, leering pervert had broken under the weight of high school social pressure, casting aside his degenerate persona to become a cold, aloof recluse.

They could not have been more wrong.

Ren was not changing. Ren was returning to what he had always been.

Beyond the narrow boundaries of the High School DxD timeline, far beyond the conceptual framework of Heaven, the Underworld, and the Grigori, there had once existed a legend lost to the void. Long ago, in a era outside the cosmic tapestry, a collective known as the *Fiction to Reality* community sought to shatter their mortal limits. They sought to forge their own paths through the multiversal expanse, seeking out unformed, empty realms to claim and cultivate them into sovereign domains—much like the Daedric Lords of ancient elder lore.

The ambition was grand, but the multiversal cosmos was unforgiving. Powerful, primordial forces descended upon the community, unleashing a slaughter that reduced their grand vision to ash. Of all who fought in that forgotten war, only one survived through sheer, terrifying strength: a warrior named Fajar.

Fajar, possessed of power that defied the architecture of reality, tore a rift through the raw expanse of space and time. Single-handedly, he built his own realm, crowned himself its deity, and walked the endless corridors of existence as an absolute force. But Fajar never forgot the comrades who had bled alongside him.

During his infinite travels across the void, Fajar eventually stumbled upon the *Harrowing Interstice*—a dark, suffocating abyss where the raw negativity, forgotten grievances, and shattered fragments of dead souls from every universe accumulated, floating like discarded debris in an endless sea of darkness.

There, buried deep in the toxic currents of the void, Fajar found the broken remnants of his fallen comrade: Ren.

Ren’s soul was fractured, eroded by time and trauma, reduced to a flickering spark of instinct. But Fajar remembered who his friend had been. In his past life, before the war, Ren had been a mortal captivated by stories of fantasy and power—most notably the chaotic, supernatural world of *High School DxD*, blended with the dark, heartless mythos of *Kingdom Hearts*. He had loved the idea of the grand harem, the absurdity of teenage desire, and the intoxicating pursuit of strength.

Fajar took that shattered spark of a soul and placed it directly into the fabric of this timeline. It was a gesture of divine brotherhood—a living sandbox where Ren could reconstruct his identity, heal his broken spirit, and live out the precise fantasies he had once held in his mortal heart. Fajar wanted to entertain his old friend, allowing him to navigate the dilemma between the foolish, innocent desire for a harem and the ruthless pursuit of supernatural power.

Before his soul had been shattered in the ancient war, Ren had spoken a core truth to Fajar—a philosophy that defined his very essence:

*"If I am strong enough, I want to stand in the light. I want to be part of the chosen, to laugh, to fight, and to claim a harem of my own. But if the world denies me that light... if I am just a baseline mortal standing in the shadow of born gods and lucky heroes... then I will abandon the dream. I will embrace the dark, become the monster of the unchosen, and forge my power through absolute, uncompromising strength."*

That was the truth resting in the depths of Ren Nocturnus Veritas.

His time spent as a crude, perverted high schooler with Matsuda and Motohama had been the subconscious healing phase—a peaceful, foolish disguise used by a recovering soul to experience normal mortal life. But the moment Raynare’s light spear shattered the park bench, the moment Issei was chosen by a Devil while Ren was left to bleed out in the mud, the peaceful dream ended.

The shock of near-death, combined with the lingering essence of Rias's healing magic, had acted as a lightning bolt to his core. The slouched pervert was dying away, and the ancient, battle-tested warrior who had once stood alongside Fajar in a war against cosmic forces was beginning to awaken.

Ren walked through the heavy iron gates of Kuoh Academy, his backpack slung over one shoulder. The autumn wind whipped past his face, carrying the faint scent of fallen leaves and distant rain. He didn't look back at the Old School Building. He didn't look back at Kiba and Issei.

Up in the high tower of the clubroom, Issei was currently tumbling into a world of magic, demonic peerages, and beautiful women. He was the chosen one, handed a miracle on a silver platter, setting off on his grand, heroic rom-com.

Ren took a deep breath, engaging his neuro-somatic breathing. His heartbeat dropped to a slow, methodical thud. His senses expanded, taking in the rustle of the wind, the texture of the pavement beneath his boots, and the subtle, dark pulse resting dormant at the bottom of his own spirit.

Issei had chosen the path of the Devil, wrapped in the light of the chosen.

Ren turned down the quiet, shadow-draped alleyway leading toward his apartment, stepping away from the sunset and into the gathering twilight. He was a mortal with no magical blood, no divine artifact, and no demon princess to save him. But as his dark eyes reflected the fading light, a small, cold, and profoundly dangerous smile touched his lips.

He was going home to train. He was going home to bleed. And if the universe refused to give him a seat at the table, he would simply tear the table down.

## Life 2: I Start as a Devil

### Section 1: The First Awakening

Ren vaulted over the rusted chain-link fence, using his left hand as a pivot point to launch his body into the alleyway below. He wore a twenty-pound weighted vest strapped tightly across his chest, but his descent was completely silent.

Mid-air, he reached into his tactical belt, drew three weighted steel ball bearings, and snapped his wrist. The projectiles struck a line of empty aluminum cans sitting on a brick wall ten yards away with sequential, metallic clacks. Before his boots even touched the asphalt, Ren engaged his neuro-somatic breathing. He commanded his leg muscles to relax and compress at the exact moment of impact, absorbing the kinetic shock that should have rattled his knees, instantly converting the downward momentum into a smooth, forward roll.

He popped up into a low sprint, his heart rate steady at a calm ninety beats per minute despite an hour of high-speed urban parkour. The city was his testing ground. He was mapping the rooftops and alleyways, forcing his body to navigate complex environments while simultaneously deploying weapons.

As Ren rounded the corner of a residential street, he was forced to brake hard.

A rusty bicycle squealed to a halt right in front of him. Issei Hyoudou sat on the seat, hunched over the handlebars and panting like a dying dog. His school uniform was soaked in sweat, and a canvas bag filled with papers hung heavily from his shoulder.

"Issei?" Ren asked, straightening up and discreetly sliding a dull training karambit back into his pocket. "What are you doing? It’s past sunset."

"Devil... chores," Issei gasped, leaning his head against the handlebars. "President Rias gave me a stack of summoning flyers to hand out to people who might want to make contracts. But I don't have enough demonic energy to use the magic teleportation circles yet. So... I have to bike everywhere."

Ren looked at his friend’s exhausted state. He noted the heavy breathing, but beneath the fatigue, his analytical eyes picked up on the subtle changes. Even exhausted, Issei’s muscle density was slowly increasing. The ambient demonic energy in his system was passively reinforcing his biology while he pedaled a bicycle.

Before Ren could offer him a bottle of water, the streetlights above them flickered and violently shattered.

The ambient temperature plummeted. The hairs on the back of Ren’s neck stood on end as a suffocating, unnatural pressure flooded the street. Above them, hovering in the twilight sky, was a woman with long, vibrant blue hair, dressed in a trench coat that barely concealed her revealing outfit. Sprouting from her back were a pair of massive, pitch-black wings.

"Kalawarner," the Fallen Angel introduced herself, her lips curling into a sadistic sneer. "Raynare reported a lingering anomaly. It seems she was right. A low-class Devil rat scurrying around the city."

Issei dropped his bicycle with a clatter, stumbling backward in absolute terror. "Another one?! Give me a break!"

Ren didn't panic. His neuro-somatic conditioning locked his heart rate in place. He stepped to the side, maintaining a clear sightline, and smoothly drew a tactical folding knife from his belt. He didn't charge her—he knew the physics of a mid-air target against a grounded combatant.

"Issei," Ren said, his voice terrifyingly calm, cutting through his friend's panic. "Show me you can fight this time. You're a Devil now. Act like it."

Kalawarner laughed, raising her hand. A massive, crackling spear of blue light coalesced in her palm. "Fight? Don't be absurd. You're both going to be vaporized."

Issei squeezed his eyes shut, raising his arms to shield his face. In the darkness of his panic, a single memory flashed in his mind: Rias Gremory, standing in the clubroom, telling him that the power of a Devil and a Sacred Gear was entirely dependent on *imagination* and pure desire.

*I don't want to die!* Issei thought violently. *I want to live! I want a harem! I want power!*

A deafening, mechanical roar echoed through the street.

*BOOST!*

A shockwave of dense, crimson energy erupted from Issei’s body, cracking the pavement beneath his feet. Kalawarner recoiled in mid-air, the sudden pressure pushing against her wings.

From Issei’s left arm, a massive, crimson gauntlet materialized, armored in thick, draconian plates with a glowing green jewel embedded in the back of the hand. At the exact same moment, the skin on Issei’s back tore, and a pair of small, leathery, bat-like Devil wings unfurled into the night air.

Kalawarner stared at the red gauntlet, her arrogant sneer vanishing into a look of absolute horror. "That aura... that Sacred Gear. The Boosted Gear?! You possess the soul of the Red Dragon Emperor?!"

She looked at Issei, who was staring at his own armored arm in shock, and then at the dense, terrifying energy radiating from it. Doing the tactical math instantly, Kalawarner realized she was entirely unprepared to fight a Longinus-class artifact.

"This changes the parameters," Kalawarner grit her teeth. She extinguished her light spear, wrapped her black wings around herself, and shot straight up into the night sky, retreating as fast as she could fly.

The heavy, suffocating pressure vanished. The street was quiet again.

Issei stood frozen, panting heavily as the crimson aura slowly dissipated. He looked at his demonic wings, then at the massive dragon gauntlet, completely bewildered by his own existence.

"Woah," a voice said beside him.

Ren walked up, the folding knife completely gone from his hands. He grabbed Issei’s left arm, pulling the Boosted Gear closer to inspect it. Ren’s eyes were wide with genuine, enthusiastic curiosity as he tapped the thick crimson armor plating. It felt like impenetrable, ancient steel.

"You actually have a built-in weapon system," Ren grinned, circling Issei to look at the leathery wings protruding from his uniform. "And aerial mobility. This is insane, Issei. You just scared off a Fallen Angel by flexing."

"I... I did?" Issei stammered, the adrenaline finally wearing off. He looked at Ren, seeing his best friend completely unfazed and genuinely hyped for him.

"You did," Ren laughed, slapping Issei on the right shoulder. "I told you to fight, and you pulled out dragon armor. Not bad at all, man. Not bad at all."

Ren meant every word of it. His envy wasn't directed at Issei's success or his shiny new armor. Issei was his friend, and Ren was deeply relieved he wasn't defenseless anymore. The true darkness waiting in Ren’s soul wasn't about hating the chosen hero—it was about the agonizing realization that no matter how much he trained, the universe would never grant him a gauntlet of his own.

### Section 2: The Tactical Guess

Lunchtime at Kuoh Academy was usually a chaotic affair, but the corner of Classroom 2-B felt like an interrogation room under totalitarian rule.

Issei sat at his desk, defensively holding a half-eaten yakisoba pan like a shield, while Matsuda and Motohama loomed over him, casting long, menacing shadows across his lunchbox.

"Start talking, Hyoudou," Matsuda demanded, his voice dropping an octave into a low, sinister rasp. "It’s been three days since you were drafted into the Occult Research Club. Give us the detailed daily itinerary. And don't leave out a single millimeter of what happens inside that Old School Building."

"I told you guys a thousand times, it’s not what you think!" Issei groaned, throwing his hands up in exasperation. "It’s pure, unadulterated manual labor! My legs feel like lead every single afternoon! I'm pedaling a rusted bicycle across three different municipal districts handing out promotional flyers! Do you have any idea how exhausting it is to bike up the steep hill near the station four times in one night?!"

Motohama pushed his taped glasses up his nose, the light reflecting off the lenses in a stark, skeptical glare. "Flyers? A bicycle? You expect two refined scholars of culture to believe that Rias Gremory—the single most untouchable, elegant woman in this school—brought you into her private sanctuary just to make you a glorified paperboy? Your cover story is weak, Issei."

"It’s not a cover story! She literally makes me track my delivery routes!" Issei yelled, his face flushing red. "I'm working my butt off while you two sit around talking about 2D girls!"

A few feet away, Ren sat quietly at his desk, flipping through a dense textbook on human neurological pathways. He took a slow, measured sip from his thermos, completely unbothered by the shouting match. His neuro-somatic training had made his hearing razor-sharp, meaning he didn't miss a single beat of their ridiculous exchange.

Ren closed his book with a soft, precise *thud*, resting his chin on his hand as he looked over at the trio. A faint, mischievous glint flickered in his dark eyes.

"You know, Issei," Ren spoke up, his calm, level voice smoothly cutting through the tension. "If you want them to believe you, you should be totally transparent about the rest of your routine."

Issei blinked, looking at Ren with sudden relief. "See?! Ren gets it! Tell them, Ren!"

"I mean," Ren continued, his expression completely deadpan, "it’s only natural, right? Let me take a wild guess at how your schedule actually goes. After all those busy, exhausting delivery runs make your muscles completely sore, President Gremory probably feels bad for you. So, naturally, she drags you into a private bath or shower right there in the clubroom to 'wash away your fatigue' personally."

Issei’s relief instantly dissolved. His jaw went slack.

"And not only that," Ren added, stoking the flames with terrifyingly accurate precision, "given how attentive she seems, she probably drops by your house completely unannounced late at night or early in the morning. She sneaks into your bathroom while you’re washing up just to 'check on your physical condition,' and ends up hopping into the shower with you as if it’s the most normal thing in the world."

Silence descended upon the classroom.

It was a total, suffocating, absolute silence.

Ren had meant it as a bizarre, hyperbolic joke to tease his friend—a ridiculous scenario that sounded like a cheap light novel plot. But Issei didn't laugh. He didn't yell. He didn't call Ren crazy.

Issei simply froze.

His eyes widened to the size of dinner plates. A single, massive bead of cold sweat formed on his temple and rolled slowly down his cheek. His mouth opened slightly, but no sound came out. For five agonizing, uninterrupted seconds, Issei sat completely paralyzed, his mind flashing back to Rias randomly popping up naked in his bed, straddling him in the morning, and casually walking into his home bathroom to evaluate his demonic healing while he was covered in soap.

He didn't say a word. The pause went on for so long it felt like time itself had stopped moving.

Matsuda’s eyes slowly shifted from Ren to Issei. Motohama’s glasses slid half an inch down his nose.

"He..." Matsuda whispered, his voice trembling with a horror so profound it bordered on existential. "He didn't deny it."

"The latency period of his response was three point four seconds," Motohama choked out, his hands shaking violently as he pulled out a notepad. "A standard denial occurs within zero point five seconds. A pause of that duration indicates... visual processing. Memory retrieval!"

"HE DIDN'T DENY IT!" Matsuda roared, exploding into a hysterical, blood-curdling scream that rattled the classroom windows. "IT'S ALL TRUE! HE'S TAKING SHOWERS WITH THE CRIMSON RUIN!"

"TRAITOR! DEMON! EXECUTE THE HERETIC!" Motohama wailed, tearing off his glasses and launching himself over the desk.

In a flash of pure, chaotic madness, Matsuda and Motohama tackled Issei out of his chair. Matsuda locked Issei in a brutal, bone-crushing headlock while Motohama furiously attempted to rub a dusty chalkboard eraser directly into his face, both of them weeping actual tears of unadulterated, envious rage.

"GAHHH! GET OFF ME! REN, YOU BASTARD, WHY WOULD YOU SAY THAT?!" Issei shrieked, frantically trying to kick his legs free as he was swarmed by his hysterical friends. "IT WAS A MISUNDERSTANDING! SHE WAS JUST CHECKING MY DEMONIC RECOVERY RATE! IT WAS MEDICAL!"

"MEDICAL SHOWERING?!" Matsuda shrieked louder, pulling the headlock tighter. "I'LL SHOW YOU MEDICAL!"

Ren leaned back in his chair, taking another calm, slow sip from his thermos. He watched the three of them wrestling violently on the linoleum floor, a rare, genuine smile tugging at the corner of his lips.

In his previous life—or in the foolish, perverted disguise he had worn just a week ago—he might have joined the dogpile, screaming about unfairness and demanding details. But that superficial desire had been burned away in the fires of the park. He didn't feel a single drop of jealousy toward Issei's absurd, anime-trope lifestyle. He didn't want a magical girl to wash his back; he wanted the strength to survive a spear of light through his heart.

Still, watching Issei get pummeled by chalkboard erasers because Ren had accidentally guessed the literal truth with 100% accuracy was undeniably hilarious.

"Keep your guard up, Issei," Ren called out over the sound of screaming and squeaking sneakers, his tone entirely relaxed. "Your footwork on the floor is terrible. Motohama is about to take your back."

"NOT HELPING, REN!" Issei howled from the bottom of the pile.

### Section 3: The Golden Girl

The sensory overload of Kuoh’s central shopping district was entirely intentional.

Ren walked slowly down the crowded pedestrian avenue, his hands tucked casually into his jacket pockets. To anyone passing by, he looked like a relaxed high school student enjoying the late afternoon. Internally, his mind was operating at maximum processing capacity.

He was running a live neuro-somatic diagnostic on his five senses. By actively regulating his breathing, he forced his brain to filter out the useless white noise of the city. He isolated the sound of rubber soles squeaking on the pavement. He tracked the overlapping conversations of a group of girls thirty yards away. He separated the smell of roasted chestnuts from the sharp exhaust fumes of a passing delivery truck. He was teaching his body to read the environment the way a sonar array reads the ocean.

Suddenly, a distinct anomaly pinged his internal radar.

It was a dense, localized pocket of heat, radiating a heavy, almost magnetic pressure. *Issei,* Ren deduced instantly. His friend's new demonic aura was impossible to miss once you knew what to look for. But what made Ren stop walking was the second anomaly right next to it.

It was a stark, jarring contrast. Where Issei’s presence felt like a heavy, burning coal, this second presence felt like a soft, weightless breeze. It was warm, impossibly pure, and completely devoid of malice.

Ren opened his eyes fully and navigated through the crowd. Near a large fountain at the center of the plaza, he found Issei frantically trying to communicate with a girl in a nun's habit. She had long, brilliant blonde hair, emerald green eyes, and a frantic, innocent expression as she tried to read a heavily creased city map.

"No, no, the train station is that way, but if you want the church, you have to go back up the hill," Issei was saying, pointing in two different directions and clearly confusing her more.

"Oh, dear," the nun murmured, bowing apologetically. "I am so sorry for taking up your time. I am still not used to Japan."

"Need a navigator, Issei?"

Issei spun around, letting out a breath of relief as Ren stepped out of the crowd. "Ren! Thank god. She's looking for the old church on the outskirts of town, but my sense of direction is terrible."

"Hello," the nun said, offering Ren a polite, deeply respectful bow. "My name is Asia Argento. I have recently been assigned to the church here."

"Ren Nocturnus," he replied, giving a brief nod. His eyes scanned her quickly. No concealed weapons, no tactical posture, perfectly soft hands. She was a complete civilian. "The old church is about a twenty-minute walk from here. We can walk you there if you'd like."

"Oh, I couldn't possibly impose!" Asia protested, her face flushing with gratitude.

Before Ren could insist, a loud wail interrupted them. A small boy, no older than five, had tripped over a paving stone near the fountain, skinning his knee raw on the concrete. His mother rushed over, looking panicked as the boy sobbed loudly.

To Ren and Issei’s absolute shock, Asia didn't hesitate. She ran straight over to the boy and knelt on the dirty pavement.

"It's alright, little one. God’s grace is with you," Asia whispered softly.

She held her hands hovering just an inch over the bleeding knee. Ren narrowed his eyes, his heightened senses flaring to maximum reception. The air around Asia’s hands rippled, and a brilliant, warm, golden-green light blossomed from her palms.

It wasn't like Rias's magic. When Rias had healed Ren’s ribs, it had felt like a dense, forceful overwrite of his biology—a demonic command forcing his cells to obey. Asia’s light was entirely different. It felt soothing, natural, and profoundly holy. Ren could literally feel the gentle oscillation of the aura washing over the air, completely knitting the boy’s skin back together in a matter of seconds.

The mother gasped in awe, thanking Asia profusely, but Asia simply smiled and walked back to them as if she had just handed the boy a band-aid.

Issei was staring at her with his jaw dropped. "You... you have magic?"

"It is my Sacred Gear," Asia smiled humbly, holding her hands together. "Twilight Healing. It is a gift from the Lord to help those in pain."

Ren logged the data instantly. *A ranged, area-of-effect healing artifact. High output, low cast time. Extreme tactical value.*

"That's a beautiful gift, Asia," Ren said smoothly, breaking Issei out of his trance. "Come on. Let's get you to your church before it gets dark."

The walk to the edge of town was pleasant. Asia was incredibly naive, marveling at the sights of Japan while Issei happily acted as her tour guide. Ren walked slightly behind them, playing the silent guardian, his eyes scanning the rooftops and alleyways out of sheer habit.

As they left the bustling city center and entered the heavily wooded hills on the outskirts, the atmosphere began to shift. The temperature dropped. The chirping of cicadas faded into an unnatural, suffocating silence.

When the abandoned church finally came into view through the trees, Issei smiled. "Well, here we are! It looks a little run down, but—"

Ren stopped walking.

His neuro-somatic conditioning spiked. His heart rate, previously sitting at a calm resting pace, hammered a sudden warning rhythm against his ribs. To Issei and Asia, the church was just an old, decrepit wooden building with broken stained glass and overgrown ivy.

But to Ren’s hyper-tuned senses, the building was screaming at him.

The air around the church tasted like copper and ash. The ambient pressure was sickeningly dense, carrying the exact same hostile, predatory signature he had felt radiating off Raynare in the park and Kalawarner in the alley. The holy aura that should have surrounded a church was completely absent, hollowed out and replaced by a suffocating nest of corrupted malice.

*Fallen Angels,* Ren realized, his hand instinctively brushing against the pocket where he kept his karambit. *Multiple hostiles. High density.*

"Thank you both so much," Asia said, turning to them with a radiant, innocent smile. "You have been so kind to me. May God bless you."

"Yeah, no problem, Asia!" Issei grinned, completely oblivious to the spiritual tripwire they were standing on. "See you around!"

Asia waved happily and turned, walking up the cracked stone steps and pushing open the heavy wooden doors. The darkness of the church swallowed her, and the doors shut with a heavy, final thud.

Ren stood perfectly still in the lengthening shadows of the trees. He didn't pull out his knife. He didn't say a word to Issei. Warning him now would only cause Issei to blindly charge into an enemy stronghold with zero tactical preparation, and both of them would be slaughtered.

"Man, she was really cute, huh?" Issei sighed dreamily, turning to head back down the path. "Maybe my luck is finally turning around."

Ren turned and followed his friend down the hill, his expression carved from ice. He mentally mapped the coordinates, the sightlines, and the entry points of the building. His luck wasn't turning around. Issei had just walked a defenseless, high-value healer directly into a den of monsters, and Ren knew with absolute certainty that this peace wasn't going to last the week.

### Section 4: Monsters in the Dark

The industrial district of Kuoh was a graveyard of rusted warehouses and defunct manufacturing plants, making it the perfect arena for a ghost.

At 1:00 AM, Ren was suspended upside down from a steel I-beam, twenty feet above the concrete floor of an abandoned factory. He wasn't moving. He had engaged his neuro-somatic regulation to its absolute limit, manually dropping his heart rate to forty beats per minute and shallowing his breathing until his chest barely moved. He was practicing absolute concealment—the art of erasing his biological presence from a hostile environment.

A sudden, violent spike in atmospheric pressure nearly broke his concentration.

It wasn't the cold, predatory malice of a Fallen Angel. It was feral, chaotic, and sickeningly dense, reeking of blood and raw ozone. Seconds later, four distinct, highly controlled demonic auras entered the perimeter, hunting the chaotic one.

Ren smoothly unhooked his legs from the I-beam, dropping to a lower gantry and melting into the deep shadows just beneath the factory’s rusted catwalks. He drew his dull training karambit purely out of instinct, his eyes adjusting to the darkness below.

The heavy metal doors of the factory blew off their hinges, crashing onto the concrete.

Rias Gremory stepped into the moonlight filtering through the broken skylights, radiating absolute, sovereign authority. Behind her walked Kiba, Koneko, Akeno, and a visibly terrified Issei, who was clutching his glowing red gauntlet.

"Viser," Rias’s voice echoed through the cavernous space, cold and commanding. "You have betrayed your master, succumbed to your base desires, and become a Stray. In the name of the House of Gremory, I am here to execute you."

From the shadows at the far end of the warehouse, a horrific screech vibrated against the corrugated metal walls. The Stray Devil emerged. From the waist up, it was a beautiful, albeit deranged woman. From the waist down, it was a grotesque, bulbous monstrosity of flesh, venomous stingers, and thrashing limbs.

Up in the rafters, Ren’s analytical mind immediately began running combat simulations. *Target possesses massive weight advantage, unpredictable attack vectors, and heavily reinforced chitin. A frontal assault is suicide. Standard blade penetration is zero.*

Down below, Rias looked at Issei. "Watch closely, Issei. You are a Pawn. Tonight, you will see the full potential of your peers."

Kiba stepped forward first. He didn't draw a stance; he simply rested his hand on the hilt of his sword.

*Knight,* Rias commanded.

Kiba vanished. Ren’s eyes, trained to track high-speed projectiles, barely caught the blur of motion. It was subsonic speed. Kiba materialized directly behind the Stray, his blade already returning to its sheath. A second later, three of Viser’s massive, thrashing limbs severed perfectly at the joints, spraying purple blood across the floor.

*Rook,* Rias called out.

Koneko stepped forward. The petite, white-haired girl—who couldn't have weighed more than ninety pounds—casually walked up to a reinforced concrete pillar that had collapsed from the ceiling. Without grunting, she dug her fingers into the stone, lifted the three-ton block of concrete over her head, and hurled it like a baseball. It slammed into Viser’s torso with the force of an artillery shell, shattering the Stray’s chitinous armor and pinning it to the floor.

*Queen,* Rias finished, a cruel smile touching her lips.

Akeno stepped forward, her sadistic giggle echoing through the factory. She raised her hands, and the air pressure inverted. A blinding pillar of demonic lightning tore through the roof of the factory, striking the pinned Stray Devil with apocalyptic force. The sheer heat of the blast singed the air in Ren's lungs twenty feet above.

When the light faded, there was nothing left of Viser but a scorch mark on the concrete.

Ren stared at the smoldering crater. His grip on the training karambit was so tight his knuckles were white.
