*This is the reality,* Ren thought, his heart rate finally spiking as adrenaline flooded his system. *If I fight them like a human, I die. If I try to match Kiba’s speed or Koneko’s strength, I die. Mortal physics cannot compete with demonic output.*

He looked at the karambit in his hand, then back down at the crater.

*But... durability is just matter. Matter has weak points.*

A fleeting, ancient instinct flared in the back of his mind—a remnant of the shattered warrior who had fought alongside Fajar. He remembered a martial arts philosophy that defied the boundaries of physical limits. *Kinetic condensing.*

If Koneko used three tons of dispersed force to crush the armor, Ren couldn't replicate that. But what if he used one hundred pounds of force, synchronized perfectly through neuro-somatic breathing, and condensed it entirely into the surface area of a needle’s point? What if he didn't strike the armor, but drove the kinetic shockwave perfectly through the gaps in the joints, transferring the lethal momentum directly into the internal organs before the aura could react?

He didn't need to be faster than Kiba. He just needed to be in the exact right place, at the exact right millisecond, striking with zero wasted momentum. The margin for error wasn't small—it was zero. If he missed by a fraction of an inch, his bones would shatter.

A cold, terrifying smile spread across Ren's face in the darkness of the rafters.

It was mathematically impossible for a normal human. But Ren wasn't planning on staying a normal human for long.

Down on the floor, Akeno suddenly paused, her violet eyes snapping upward toward the steel gantries. "President... I thought I sensed something in the rafters just now. A slight fluctuation in the ambient heat."

Rias frowned, manifesting a small sphere of crimson magic in her palm to illuminate the upper levels of the factory. "A Stray familiar, perhaps? Or a Fallen Angel spy?"

The red light swept across the rusted I-beams, illuminating the dust motes dancing in the air, the tangled ventilation pipes, and the broken skylights.

There was nothing there.

Ren was already two blocks away, sprinting silently across the rooftops under the moonlight. His mind was ablaze with kinetic equations, anatomical weak points, and a desperate, beautiful hunger. He finally had the blueprint. He knew exactly what he needed to build.

The scent of rust and damp concrete hung heavy inside the derelict five-story factory on the industrial fringe of Kuoh.

Ren clung to a high steel I-beam thirty feet above the cracked floorboards, his body flattened into the deep shadows near the ceiling. He wore a dark hoodie, reinforced gloves, and lightweight boots with soft rubber soles designed for sound dampening. His neuro-somatic conditioning was dialed to complete stealth mode: his heart rate hovered at a quiet forty-two beats per minute, his breathing was completely silent through his nose, and his body heat was tightly regulated to prevent excess sweat.

He hadn't followed Issei here out of simple curiosity. Ren had been conducting a midnight urban scouting route when he picked up the familiar, dense pressure of Rias Gremory’s magic moving toward the industrial zone. Following them had been a live-fire exercise in stealth.

Down on the ground floor, five figures stood around a trembling Issei Hyoudou. Rias, Akeno, Kiba, and Koneko were gathered near the center of the vast, hollow chamber.

"Tonight is your first official lesson, Issei," Rias’s voice echoed softly off the concrete walls. "A wild Devil—a Stray—has taken root in this facility. It has abandoned its master and given into its primal, monstrous urges. We are here to exterminate it."

A guttural, wet screech ripped through the silence of the warehouse.

From the rusted elevator shaft at the far end, a grotesque creature dragged itself into the moonlight. It was Viser—a Stray Devil whose lower half had mutated into a massive, multi-legged arachnid form made of chiseled, chitinous hide, while its upper torso retained a twisted, monstrous humanoid shape. Acids oozed from its maw, sizzling as they dripped onto the concrete floor.

"It's... a monster!" Issei shrieked, taking three quick steps backward.

"Observe carefully, Issei," Rias commanded smoothly, stepping back beside him. "Every member of a peerage is assigned a role based on the chess pieces used to reincarnate them. Watch how my house executes its duties."

From his perch in the rafters, Ren narrowed his eyes, his neural mapping firing at full capacity.

*Kiba Yuto. Knight.*

Kiba drew a sleek silver longsword. In a fraction of a millisecond, the blonde boy vanished. There was no windup, no friction, no telegraphing of movement. He appeared directly behind the Stray Devil, a blur of silver light trailing his blade.

*CRACK.*

Four of the creature's armored spider legs were cleanly severed before the beast even registered Kiba had moved. *Velocity,* Ren noted, analyzing the biomechanics. *He doesn't accelerate—he instantly hits top kinetic speed. Unnatural stride length.*

Viser howled in rage, swinging a massive, clawed arm toward Kiba.

*Koneko Toujou. Rook.*

The tiny white-haired girl stepped casually into the path of the claw. She didn't dodge. She didn't block. She simply caught the massive, monster-sized fist in her tiny palm, stopping several tons of force dead in its tracks. With a cold, expressionless glare, Koneko rotated her hip, grabbed the Stray's wrist, and slammed the entire multi-ton beast into the concrete floor, shattering the foundation into dust.

*Density,* Ren cataloged. *Her mass and structural alignment briefly become immovable. Incredible bone density.*

Viser scrambled back, screeching in agony, only for black clouds to roll across the ceiling right beneath Ren’s beam.

*Akeno Himejima. Queen.*

Akeno hovered in mid-air, a sadistic, radiant smile playing on her lips as holy lightning crackled along her fingertips. "Shall I tenderize it, President?"

With a flick of her wrist, a blinding pillar of yellow thunder erupted from the sky, slamming into the Stray Devil and frying its hide until the flesh burned black.

Finally, Rias stepped forward, raising her hand. A sphere of crimson, anti-matter magic condensed above her palm. "And I am the King. Destruction is my domain."

She launched the orb. The moment the red magic touched the Stray Devil, the monster didn't just burn or bleed—it was atomized, converted into raw dust that dissolved into nothingness.

Issei stood frozen, staring at the empty space where a terrifying monster had existed just seconds prior.

"And you, Issei," Rias said, turning to him with a gentle smile as she reached into her pocket, "are my Pawn. In fact, your potential is so vast that it took all eight of my Pawn pieces to reincarnate you. You hold the ultimate adaptability."

Up in the shadows of the rafters, Ren pressed his back flatter against the iron beam, his chest rising and falling in slow, controlled cycles.

The demonstration was over. Rias’s peerage was laughing, chatting casually as if they had just finished an afternoon chore, completely unaware that a mortal observer was sitting twenty feet above their heads.

Ren stared at the cracked, cratered concrete floor below.

A normal human watching that fight would have felt absolute despair. How could fists, knives, or martial arts ever hope to match a girl who could lift a building, a knight who moved faster than sound, or a demon who could vaporize matter with a thought? A human soul would conclude that mortal effort was a joke—that without a Sacred Gear or a Devil piece, a mortal was just a bug waiting to be crushed.

Ren closed his eyes, engaging his neuro-somatic focus. He felt his pulse, his steady breathing, and the iron grip of his gloved fingers on the cold metal beam.

*They're wrong,* Ren thought, a cold, sharp clarity settling over his mind. *CQC and knives aren't useless. They're just incomplete.*

He had watched the Stray Devil's hide repel Kiba's first strike until Kiba adjusted his wrist angle. The monster's chitinous armor was thick, but its neck joint, the soft membrane behind its eyes, and the gaps between its sternum plates were unarmored. Physics still governed this world. Force still equaled Mass times Acceleration.

If a supernatural entity relied on reinforced skin and dense magic to survive, then a mortal couldn't rely on brute strength. A mortal had to strike with absolute, terrifying precision. Every strike had to target the tiny, unprotected anatomical seams—the carotid plexus, the spinal cord junction, the tear ducts—with hyper-condensed kinetic force delivered faster than the enemy's brain could trigger its magical defense.

He didn't need to punch through a mountain like Koneko. He just needed to drive a two-inch blade into the exact millimeter of space where a monster’s nerve center connected to its spine, before it knew he was there.

*Faster. Cleaner. More precise.*

Down below, Rias opened a crimson magic circle, and the Occult Research Club stepped through, vanishing into thin air and leaving the ruined factory in total silence.

Ren waited three full minutes until the ambient magical pressure completely dissipated. Then, with smooth, frictionless agility, he dropped down from the thirty-foot rafter, landing in a low, silent crouch on the concrete.

He stood up, looking at the spot where the Stray Devil had been erased.

Issei was a Pawn. He had eight pieces of ultimate potential, a dragon gauntlet, and a team of god-like Devils to protect him.

Ren pulled his hood down, his dark eyes reflecting the pale moonlight filtering through the broken roof. He was just a human standing in an empty warehouse. But as he adjusted his tactical gloves and turned toward the exit, the foundation of his mortal arsenal was no longer a desperate defense. It was becoming a lethal, calculated science.

**Section 3: Monsters in the Dark**

The scent of rust and damp concrete hung heavy inside the derelict five-story factory on the industrial fringe of Kuoh.

Ren clung to a high steel I-beam thirty feet above the cracked floorboards, his body flattened into the deep shadows near the ceiling. He wore a dark hoodie, reinforced gloves, and lightweight boots with soft rubber soles designed for sound dampening. His neuro-somatic conditioning was dialed to complete stealth mode: his heart rate hovered at a quiet forty-two beats per minute, his breathing was completely silent through his nose, and his body heat was tightly regulated to prevent excess sweat.

He hadn't followed Issei here out of simple curiosity. Ren had been conducting a midnight urban scouting route when he picked up the familiar, dense pressure of Rias Gremory’s magic moving toward the industrial zone. Following them had been a live-fire exercise in stealth.

Down on the ground floor, five figures stood around a trembling Issei Hyoudou. Rias, Akeno, Kiba, and Koneko were gathered near the center of the vast, hollow chamber.

"Tonight is your first official lesson, Issei," Rias’s voice echoed softly off the concrete walls. "A wild Devil—a Stray—has taken root in this facility. It has abandoned its master and given into its primal, monstrous urges. We are here to exterminate it."

A guttural, wet screech ripped through the silence of the warehouse.

From the rusted elevator shaft at the far end, a grotesque creature dragged itself into the moonlight. It was Viser—a Stray Devil whose lower half had mutated into a massive, multi-legged arachnid form made of chiseled, chitinous hide, while its upper torso retained a twisted, monstrous humanoid shape. Acids oozed from its maw, sizzling as they dripped onto the concrete floor.

"It's... a monster!" Issei shrieked, taking three quick steps backward.

"Observe carefully, Issei," Rias commanded smoothly, stepping back beside him. "Every member of a peerage is assigned a role based on the chess pieces used to reincarnate them. Watch how my house executes its duties."

From his perch in the rafters, Ren narrowed his eyes, his neural mapping firing at full capacity.

*Kiba Yuto. Knight.*

Kiba drew a sleek silver longsword. In a fraction of a millisecond, the blonde boy vanished. There was no windup, no friction, no telegraphing of movement. He appeared directly behind the Stray Devil, a blur of silver light trailing his blade.

*CRACK.*

Four of the creature's armored spider legs were cleanly severed before the beast even registered Kiba had moved. *Velocity,* Ren noted, analyzing the biomechanics. *He doesn't accelerate—he instantly hits top kinetic speed. Unnatural stride length.*

Viser howled in rage, swinging a massive, clawed arm toward Kiba.

*Koneko Toujou. Rook.*

The tiny white-haired girl stepped casually into the path of the claw. She didn't dodge. She didn't block. She simply caught the massive, monster-sized fist in her tiny palm, stopping several tons of force dead in its tracks. With a cold, expressionless glare, Koneko rotated her hip, grabbed the Stray's wrist, and slammed the entire multi-ton beast into the concrete floor, shattering the foundation into dust.

*Density,* Ren cataloged. *Her mass and structural alignment briefly become immovable. Incredible bone density.*

Viser scrambled back, screeching in agony, only for black clouds to roll across the ceiling right beneath Ren’s beam.

*Akeno Himejima. Queen.*

Akeno hovered in mid-air, a sadistic, radiant smile playing on her lips as holy lightning crackled along her fingertips. "Shall I tenderize it, President?"

With a flick of her wrist, a blinding pillar of yellow thunder erupted from the sky, slamming into the Stray Devil and frying its hide until the flesh burned black.

Finally, Rias stepped forward, raising her hand. A sphere of crimson, anti-matter magic condensed above her palm. "And I am the King. Destruction is my domain."

She launched the orb. The moment the red magic touched the Stray Devil, the monster didn't just burn or bleed—it was atomized, converted into raw dust that dissolved into nothingness.

Issei stood frozen, staring at the empty space where a terrifying monster had existed just seconds prior.

"And you, Issei," Rias said, turning to him with a gentle smile as she reached into her pocket, "are my Pawn. In fact, your potential is so vast that it took all eight of my Pawn pieces to reincarnate you. You hold the ultimate adaptability."

Up in the shadows of the rafters, Ren pressed his back flatter against the iron beam, his chest rising and falling in slow, controlled cycles.

The demonstration was over. Rias’s peerage was laughing, chatting casually as if they had just finished an afternoon chore, completely unaware that a mortal observer was sitting twenty feet above their heads.

Ren stared at the cracked, cratered concrete floor below.

A normal human watching that fight would have felt absolute despair. How could fists, knives, or martial arts ever hope to match a girl who could lift a building, a knight who moved faster than sound, or a demon who could vaporize matter with a thought? A human soul would conclude that mortal effort was a joke—that without a Sacred Gear or a Devil piece, a mortal was just a bug waiting to be crushed.

Ren closed his eyes, engaging his neuro-somatic focus. He felt his pulse, his steady breathing, and the iron grip of his gloved fingers on the cold metal beam.

*They're wrong,* Ren thought, a cold, sharp clarity settling over his mind. *CQC and knives aren't useless. They're just incomplete.*

He had watched the Stray Devil's hide repel Kiba's first strike until Kiba adjusted his wrist angle. The monster's chitinous armor was thick, but its neck joint, the soft membrane behind its eyes, and the gaps between its sternum plates were unarmored. Physics still governed this world. Force still equaled Mass times Acceleration.

If a supernatural entity relied on reinforced skin and dense magic to survive, then a mortal couldn't rely on brute strength. A mortal had to strike with absolute, terrifying precision. Every strike had to target the tiny, unprotected anatomical seams—the carotid plexus, the spinal cord junction, the tear ducts—with hyper-condensed kinetic force delivered faster than the enemy's brain could trigger its magical defense.

He didn't need to punch through a mountain like Koneko. He just needed to drive a two-inch blade into the exact millimeter of space where a monster’s nerve center connected to its spine, before it knew he was there.

*Faster. Cleaner. More precise.*

Down below, Rias opened a crimson magic circle, and the Occult Research Club stepped through, vanishing into thin air and leaving the ruined factory in total silence.

Ren waited three full minutes until the ambient magical pressure completely dissipated. Then, with smooth, frictionless agility, he dropped down from the thirty-foot rafter, landing in a low, silent crouch on the concrete.

He stood up, looking at the spot where the Stray Devil had been erased.

Issei was a Pawn. He had eight pieces of ultimate potential, a dragon gauntlet, and a team of god-like Devils to protect him.

Ren pulled his hood down, his dark eyes reflecting the pale moonlight filtering through the broken roof. He was just a human standing in an empty warehouse. But as he adjusted his tactical gloves and turned toward the exit, the foundation of his mortal arsenal was no longer a desperate defense. It was becoming a lethal, calculated science.

### Section 5: The Unseen Threshold

#### Sub Section 1: The Blind Vanguard

Survival against the supernatural was not a matter of blocking. If Ren attempted to block a punch from a Knight like Kiba or a Rook like Koneko, his forearms would shatter into dust, and the residual kinetic force would liquefy his internal organs. To survive in the crossfire of gods and monsters, the prime directive was absolute: do not get hit. And to ensure he never got hit, he needed the ability to foresee an attack before the enemy's muscles even twitched.

His physical conditioning and neuro-somatic integration had already pushed his evasion to the absolute limit of human biomechanics. He could dodge, pivot, and roll with zero wasted momentum. But physical speed meant nothing if his reaction time was tethered to visual processing. Light hit the retina, the optic nerve signaled the brain, the brain commanded the muscles—that biological latency, no matter how optimized, was too slow to dodge a spear of light.

Sitting cross-legged in the center of his dark apartment, Ren held a thick strip of black cotton fabric in his hands.

If visual processing was a crutch that limited his reaction time, then the most logical step was to break the crutch entirely.

Ren raised the cloth, wrapped it securely over his eyes, and tied it tight at the back of his head. The world vanished into absolute, suffocating darkness. Instantly, his brain panicked, a primal spike of vertigo hitting his system as his primary method of spatial orientation was severed.

He didn't remove the blindfold. Instead, he engaged his neuro-somatic breathing. He commanded his heart rate to slow, manually overriding his brain's panic response, and forced his sensory hierarchy to re-calibrate.

Slowly, the darkness stopped feeling empty.

He heard the low, rhythmic hum of the refrigerator in the kitchen. He felt the subtle draft of cool air seeping in through the crack under the front door. He smelled the faint, metallic scent of the oil he used to maintain his training knives.

Ren stood up in the pitch-black room. He drew his dull training karambit from his belt.

For the next four hours, he ran through his brutal CQC and knife-fighting drills completely blind. At first, it was clumsy. He bumped his knee on the coffee table; he misjudged the distance to the wall and scraped his knuckles. But as he forced his neuro-somatic focus deeper into his peripheral nervous system, something incredible began to happen.

He stopped trying to *see* his imaginary opponent and started trying to *feel* them. When he slashed the air, he paid absolute attention to the micro-currents of wind wrapping around the blade. He mapped the entire apartment purely through echolocation, using the soft thud of his own footwork bouncing off the drywall to gauge his exact coordinates. He practiced slipping, weaving, and pivoting against invisible strikes, relying entirely on his spatial memory and the shifting pressure of the air around him.

But confining this training to a controlled environment wasn't enough. The real world was chaotic, and he needed his newly developing senses forged in that chaos.

The next morning, Ren did not take off the blindfold.

He navigated his morning routine entirely in the dark. He cooked his breakfast by listening to the sizzle of the eggs and smelling the exact moment the proteins began to brown. He dressed himself by tactile memory.

When it was time to train outside, he pulled the hood of his jacket up, put on a pair of dark sunglasses over the thin black blindfold to hide it from civilians, and walked out the front door.

Stepping into the bustling commercial district of Kuoh blindfolded was like walking into a hurricane of sensory overload. The sheer volume of overlapping data—hundreds of footsteps, car engines, overlapping conversations, the heat of the sun, the wind channeling between buildings—threatened to overwhelm him. He stood frozen on the corner for a full minute, his neuro-somatic circuits burning as he processed the noise.

Then, he began to walk.

He didn't bump into anyone. He felt the displacement of air as a businessman walked past his left shoulder. He felt the radiant heat of a food stall ten yards to his right. He stopped at crosswalks not because he saw the light change, but because he listened to the shifting idle of the car engines and the collective, forward momentum of the crowd around him.

Only three times during his four-mile trek across town did he slide his thumb under the blindfold, opening his eyes for a fraction of a second just to confirm his heading and correct his internal compass.

As the hours passed and the sun began to set, Ren realized that his neuro-somatic integration was unlocking something buried deep within his soul's architecture—something that went beyond the basic five senses.

Standing in the middle of a crowded plaza, immersed in total darkness, he wasn't just hearing or feeling the physical world anymore. He was beginning to sense *intent*. He could feel the slight, invisible spike in atmospheric pressure when a rushing pedestrian was about to abruptly change direction. He could sense the heavy, sluggish aura of a stray dog sleeping under a bench.

He was starting to feel the subtle, spiritual weight of the world around him. It was a faint, primitive version of the "foreseeing" he desperately needed. If he could sense the intent of a normal human's movement before their foot even hit the pavement, then eventually, he would be able to feel the murderous intent of a Fallen Angel a split second before they threw a spear.

Ren adjusted his hood, turned toward the direction of his apartment, and walked smoothly through the crowded street. He was entirely blind, yet he had never seen the world more clearly.

#### Sub Section 2: The Ascetic Vanguard

Mastering the ability to sense hostile intent—to feel the invisible, psychic weight of a pending strike before the physical muscles even contract—was a threshold that usually required decades of desperate, monk-like devotion. For a baseline human, achieving that level of extrasensory perception meant stripping the mind to its absolute barest foundations, enduring years of sensory deprivation, and bleeding through thousands of life-or-death encounters.

Yet, Ren had begun to grasp the fringes of this impossible skill in a matter of days.

This terrifyingly accelerated growth was not merely the result of his neuro-somatic conditioning, nor was it simple biological genius. It was the resonance of the dormant architecture within him. Two distinct, unquantifiable forces were acting as a catalyst for his evolution.

The first was the ancient, dormant echo of his past life. He possessed the shattered, reincarnated soul of a warrior who had once stood shoulder-to-shoulder with Fajar in a multiversal war against primordial entities. His mortal brain was learning these tactical espionage skills for the first time, but his *soul* was simply remembering. When Ren closed his eyes and reached out to feel the atmospheric shifts of intent, he wasn't building a new instinct; he was clearing the dust off a cosmic weapon he had wielded eons ago.

The second factor was the seed of darkness festering at the core of his being. The absolute, pragmatic acceptance of his role as the "unchosen"—the cold, dense gravity of his dormant envy—acted as a highly conductive medium. Light magic required faith and imagination, but the darkness within Ren was grounded in raw, unfiltered reality. It allowed him to feel the negative space in the world, picking up on malice, aggression, and intent like a spider sitting at the center of a perfectly tuned web.

He didn't just practice this in the isolation of his apartment or the anonymity of the city streets. If he was going to weaponize his blindness, he had to normalize it in the most chaotic, densely populated environment available to him.

The next morning, Ren walked through the front gates of Kuoh Academy wearing a sleek, folded strip of black cotton tied securely over his eyes.

The reaction of the student body was instantaneous and profoundly loud.

As Ren navigated the bustling courtyard, sidestepping a group of first-years and fluidly adjusting his path to avoid a swinging gate, a wave of stunned silence rippled outward, immediately followed by furious, overlapping whispers.

"Is he... is he blindfolded?"
"He just walked right around the fountain without touching his cane. He doesn't even *have* a cane!"
"What is Nocturnus doing?"

Inside Classroom 2-B, the sensory labyrinth was even more complex. Ren stepped through the sliding door, his neuro-somatic focus automatically mapping the room. He could smell the sharp tang of the dry-erase markers, hear the exact frequency of the fluorescent lights buzzing overhead, and feel the localized heat signatures of his thirty classmates. He walked smoothly down the aisle, his hip clearing the edges of the desks by a margin of mere millimeters, and sat down in his chair near the window.

Within five seconds, a heavy shadow fell over his desk.

"Ren," Issei said, his voice laced with a mixture of genuine concern and utter bewilderment. "Buddy. What is on your face?"

Matsuda and Motohama flanked Issei, staring at Ren as if he had just sprouted a second head.

"It's a blindfold, Issei," Ren replied, his voice calm and perfectly leveled. He didn't bother turning his head, instead facing the window where he could feel the morning sun warming the glass.

"We can see that it's a blindfold!" Matsuda hissed, leaning in close. "The question is *why* are you wearing a blindfold in homeroom?! Did you lose a bet? Is this some kind of new, highly advanced perverted technique we don't know about? Are you trying to heighten your imagination?!"

"My eyes are strained," Ren answered smoothly, delivering the alibi he had prepared with deadpan sincerity. "I've been staring at too many screens lately, reading anatomical and medical texts until three in the morning. The blue light gave me severe ocular fatigue. I decided to give my optic nerves a rest. Plus, I was curious about how the body compensates. I'm training my other senses to pick up the slack."

Motohama adjusted his glasses, peering closely at the black fabric. "You navigated the entire second-floor hallway, bypassed a crowd of girls from the tennis club, and found your exact desk without tripping over a single backpack. That level of spatial awareness transcends simple curiosity."

Motohama paused, his eyes narrowing as he analyzed the perfect, relaxed posture Ren was holding. "The way you move... your center of gravity is completely locked. You didn't just stumble in here. Ren... do you practice martial arts?"

"It’s been a long time," Ren admitted casually, leaning back in his chair and crossing his arms. It was a half-truth that perfectly covered his tracks. "I stopped talking about it because I only train when I'm alone. It’s just a hobby to stay in shape. Navigating the dark is just a new drill I'm trying out."

The trio exchanged wide-eyed, completely baffled looks. Issei stared at his friend, a sudden wave of realization hitting him. Ren wasn't just acting weird; he was genuinely disciplined. The physical changes, the calm demeanor, the sudden lack of interest in their degenerate antics—it was all starting to form a cohesive, terrifying picture.

And they weren't the only ones putting the pieces together.

The girls sitting a few rows away had been eavesdropping on the entire exchange. The whispers began immediately, spreading through the female population of the classroom like a shockwave.

"Did you hear that? He's a martial artist."
"That explains why his shoulders look so broad lately. He's been hiding it!"
"But wait... if he's wearing a blindfold to rest his eyes... that means he literally can't look at us."
"Oh my god. You're right. He can't peek in the locker rooms or stare at our skirts if he's completely blind!"

The logic was bulletproof. To the student body of Kuoh Academy, the pervert persona was intrinsically tied to the act of looking. By voluntarily blinding himself, Ren had publicly and undeniably severed his ties to his degenerate past.

As the homeroom teacher walked in and demanded everyone take their seats, the rumors solidified into a new reality. Ren Nocturnus Veritas was no longer a creep. He was an eccentric, stoic ascetic. A mysterious, physically capable student who trained in secret and possessed the bizarre, almost intimidating discipline to navigate a high school entirely in the dark.

Ren sat quietly as the teacher began the roll call. He could feel the shifting weight of the classroom's attention, the subtle reduction in hostility from the girls, and the lingering confusion of Issei and the others. It was the perfect cover. While Kuoh Academy debated the nature of his newfound stoicism, Ren simply slowed his breathing, sinking deeper into the dark, listening for the invisible currents of intent flowing through the room.

#### Sub Section 3: The Lethal Blueprint

The silence of Ren's apartment was absolute, save for the slow, rhythmic intake of his neuro-somatic breathing. Seated in the center of the dark living room with the black cotton blindfold securely tied over his eyes, he was not resting. He was constructing a digital battleground in his mind, running high-fidelity simulations of the monsters he had witnessed in the factory.

He conjured Viser first. The Stray Devil shrieked in his mind, firing a volley of acidic venom. Ren visualized the exact trajectory, dropping into a low slide to let the acid melt the wall behind him. Viser lunged. Ren didn't engage; he visualized utilizing the factory environment, parkouring up a rusted staircase, slashing a pressurized steam pipe to blind the beast’s multi-eyed vision, and escaping through a fractured skylight.

Then, he simulated the Occult Research Club.

*Kiba Yuto.* The Knight vanished. Ren’s mind calculated the air pressure. Kiba wouldn't attack from the front. Ren visualized stepping backward the exact millisecond the silver longsword swung at his neck, ducking into the blade's blind spot. He couldn't fight Kiba. Escape was the only victory. Ren imagined throwing a handful of steel ball bearings onto the concrete to disrupt the Knight's unnatural frictionless footwork, detonating a smoke grenade, and diving into a narrow ventilation shaft where a longsword couldn't swing.

*Koneko Toujou.* The Rook charged. A punch capable of shattering a building aimed squarely at his chest. Ren visualized perfectly pivoting his hips, letting the small girl’s fist breeze past his jacket and slam into a load-bearing pillar. As the roof began to cave in from the structural damage, Ren would use the falling debris to mask his retreat, letting Koneko’s own catastrophic strength bury her momentarily.

*Akeno and Rias.* The magic-users. Ren’s tactical simulation for them was brutally short. Against Akeno’s lightning, the environment was his only shield; he would have to ground himself behind dense, insulated concrete, breaking line of sight before the thunderbolt fell. Against Rias’s orb of pure destruction, there was no block, no parry, and no cover. The simulation dictated total, immediate evacuation. If the Crimson Ruin targeted him, his only strategy was to never be in the room in the first place.

Opening his eyes to the darkness of his blindfold, Ren realized mental simulations were no longer enough. His mind understood the geometry of survival, but his body needed to be baptized in physical chaos.

At 2:00 AM, Ren stood in an empty, automated batting cage on the outskirts of Kuoh. The pitching machine was dialed to its maximum velocity—ninety-five miles per hour.

Ren stood directly in the batter's box without a bat, perfectly still, entirely blindfolded.

*Clunk. Thwack.*

The machine fired. Ren heard the mechanical release, felt the displacement of air, and slightly tilted his head. The baseball slammed into the chain-link fence behind him, missing his ear by two inches. For an hour, he danced in the cage, letting the high-speed projectiles whistle past his shoulders, ribs, and face. He then moved to the alley behind the cages, where he had bolted a modified, spinning Wing Chun dummy to a brick wall. He engaged it blind, using his heightened tactile feedback to parry and redirect the rapidly spinning wooden arms, his neuro-somatic focus allowing him to deflect the heavy impacts with soft, circular motions.

But baseballs and wooden arms were predictable. They possessed mechanics, but they lacked malice. He needed a training ground that simulated the chaotic, lethal unpredictability of a supernatural battlefield.

Since he couldn't spar with a Devil and there were no wild beasts roaming the city limits, Ren built his own hazard gauntlet.

He broke into a condemned, structurally unstable warehouse near the docks. Over the course of three nights, he rigged the ceiling and the rusted catwalks with a terrifying array of traps. He hoisted massive, heavy cinder blocks onto frayed ropes, rigged rusted iron pipes to tripwires, and suspended heavy sandbags attached to unpredictable pendulums.

On the fourth night, Ren stood in the center of the kill zone, blindfolded. He pulled the master release lever.

The warehouse erupted into a symphony of falling death. Ropes snapped. Chains groaned. Ren engaged his neuro-somatic breathing, expanding his senses outward. He felt the heavy, crushing intent of a cinder block plummeting toward his skull. He side-stepped, feeling the wind shear as the concrete shattered against the floor. A rusted pipe swung at his ribs from the dark; he felt the air compress against his side and backflipped, letting the pipe smash into a sandbag. He navigated the collapsing, chaotic environment entirely by sound, air pressure, and raw spatial instinct. If he made a single mistake, a falling cinder block would shatter his spine. He survived purely by refusing to be where the danger was.

When the traps finally settled and the dust cleared, Ren was untouched, though his chest heaved as he manually flushed the lactic acid from his system.

Now came the final, most difficult phase of the curriculum.

Ren reached up and untied the blindfold.

The moonlight filtering through the shattered warehouse windows flooded his retinas. Instantly, his brain tried to revert to its default state. The visual data—the dust motes floating in the air, the jagged shadows of the debris—screamed for his attention, trying to aggressively overwrite the delicate, hyper-tuned senses of hearing and air-pressure he had just forged.

He couldn't let his eyes make him weak again.

Ren reset the traps. He walked back to the center of the room. He kept his eyes wide open, but he violently commanded his neuro-somatic circuits to treat his vision merely as secondary data. He focused his primary attention on the flow of the wind, the subtle groans of the ropes, and the ambient temperature of the room.

He pulled the lever.

As the hazard gauntlet rained down on him a second time, Ren watched the cinder blocks falling, but he didn't dodge based on what he saw. He dodged based on what he *felt*. He synchronized the visual tracking with the invisible pressure of the air, fusing them into a singular, flawless reaction loop. He slipped between a swinging iron pipe and a falling sandbag with terrifying, frictionless grace, his dark eyes entirely calm, reading the physics of the room perfectly.

The threshold was crossed. His eyes were open, but his awareness was boundless.

## Life 3: I Saved a Nun!
### Section 1: The Mad Dog and the King's Politics
#### Sub Section 1: Other Senses

The mechanics of sensing intent were not a new concept in the realm of fiction. As Ren sat in his apartment, recovering from the brutal hazard gauntlet he had constructed the night before, his mind drifted back to the vast archives of media he had consumed in his previous life. Specifically, his thoughts locked onto the wild, untamed jungles of Rusukaina from *One Piece*, and the grueling training regimen between Silvers Rayleigh and Monkey D. Luffy.

Rayleigh’s lessons on Observation Haki—Kenbunshoku—perfectly mirrored the threshold Ren was currently trying to master. He remembered the specific sequences vividly. Luffy, blindfolded and armed with only a stick, repeatedly failing to dodge Rayleigh’s strikes because his baseline human senses were too easily hijacked. Luffy would react to the audible snap of a twig under Rayleigh’s sandal, completely missing the actual physical strike coming from another angle. He would lose his focus the moment the scent of roasted meat drifted through the jungle air. Rayleigh had relentlessly beaten the boy to teach him a fundamental truth: sight and sound are easily manipulated, but the underlying intent of a living creature cannot be faked.

Ren realized that while he had successfully forced his body to react to intent during his blindfolded warehouse drill, he was still inherently vulnerable to cognitive hijacking. His past self—the deeply entrenched otaku—had a fatal flaw: the tendency to experience absolute tunnel vision when consuming entertainment. If a screen was in front of his face, the rest of the world ceased to exist. In a battlefield populated by supernatural predators, that kind of singular focus was a death sentence.

He needed to weaponize his own distractions.

Ren grabbed his smartphone and a pair of noise-canceling over-ear headphones. He downloaded the specific episodes of Luffy’s training arc, ensuring the video quality was high and the audio was crystal clear. Then, he put his jacket on, secured the headphones over his ears, hit play, and walked out into the bustling evening streets of Kuoh’s commercial district.

This was not a leisurely stroll; it was an advanced, high-stress multitasking protocol.

The volume in his headset was turned up loud enough to completely drown out the ambient noise of the city. The vibrant, flashing animation on the bright screen dominated his direct line of sight. To any casual observer, Ren looked like a typical, hopelessly distracted teenager glued to his phone, completely oblivious to the world around him.

Internally, however, Ren’s neuro-somatic circuitry was burning at maximum capacity, actively splitting his brain’s processing power into two distinct, parallel tracks.

Track one was entirely dedicated to the screen. He watched Rayleigh dodge a massive elephant with a casual tilt of his head, analyzing the animated biomechanics and absorbing the fictional lore of sensing invisible presence. He allowed the loud anime soundtrack and the dramatic voice acting to flood his auditory cortex. He deliberately let his otaku instincts engage, drawing his conscious attention deep into the media.

Track two was pure, unfiltered survival instinct. Because his primary visual and auditory channels were effectively blinded by the device, he violently forced his peripheral nervous system to take over spatial navigation.

He walked smoothly down the crowded sidewalk, his eyes completely locked on the glowing screen. A man carrying a stack of heavy boxes rushed out of a storefront to Ren’s left; Ren didn't look up, but he felt the sudden, localized shift in air pressure and the heavy vibration radiating through the concrete. Without missing a beat, he smoothly pivoted his hips, letting the man rush past his shoulder with an inch to spare.

He crossed a busy intersection. The noise-canceling headphones blocked the sound of the idling engines and the pedestrian crossing signals. Ren navigated the crosswalk entirely by feeling the collective kinetic momentum of the crowd moving around him and sensing the thermal heat radiating from the grills of the stopped cars. When a bicyclist swerved unexpectedly to avoid a pothole, slicing directly into Ren's path, Ren simply decelerated his stride by a fraction of a second. The bicycle’s handlebars whipped past his chest, ruffling his jacket, but his eyes never once left the animated fight on his phone.

It was a beautiful, terrifying synthesis. He was training his subconscious to maintain an impregnable, 360-degree radar of intent and physical displacement, even when his conscious mind was completely disengaged. If he could navigate the chaotic unpredictability of a dense urban center while actively distracting himself, no supernatural ambush would ever catch him off guard.

The sun dipped completely below the horizon, bathing the streets in the harsh, neon glow of storefronts and streetlamps. The commercial district slowly transitioned into the quieter, more residential arteries of the city. Ren kept walking, the glow of his phone illuminating his calm, stoic face as Luffy finally managed to dodge Rayleigh’s club on screen.

Suddenly, the passive, rhythmic data stream of the city was shattered by a massive, jagged spike of raw emotional static.

Ren stopped walking. He didn't even need to focus to feel it; the sensation was so dense and violently erratic that it felt like a physical weight pressing against his chest. It was a suffocating aura of despair, terror, and absolute, crushing defeat.

He paused the video. He pulled the noise-canceling headphones down around his neck, letting the quiet hum of the evening street rush back into his ears, and finally looked up from the screen.

Standing under the flickering light of a broken streetlamp, leaning heavily against a brick wall, was Issei Hyoudou.

The boy looked like he had just been dragged through a warzone. His school uniform was torn and stained with what looked unmistakably like dark, drying blood. His posture, usually so animated and full of obnoxious teenage energy, was completely shattered. Issei was trembling violently, his eyes hollow, wide, and staring blankly at the pavement. His knuckles were bruised, and his breathing was shallow, ragged, and hitched with the unmistakable rhythm of someone trying desperately not to hyperventilate.

Ren’s tactical mind instantly processed the visual data. The blood on Issei’s clothes didn't match the location of his minor physical abrasions. *It’s not his blood,* Ren deduced, the cold logic taking over. *He didn't just get into a fight. He witnessed a slaughter.*

Ren slipped his phone into his pocket, his posture shifting from relaxed distraction into absolute, grounded focus as he stepped out of the shadows and approached his broken friend.

#### Sub Section 2: The Blood of a Pawn

Ren closed the distance between them with measured, silent steps. Issei didn't even register his approach. The boy was trapped in a violently looping memory, his breathing shallow and rapid, his eyes locked onto the dark pavement as if he could still see the viscera spilling across it.

"Issei."

The word was spoken quietly, but it carried the firm, grounded density of a command. Issei flinched violently, his head snapping up. When he saw Ren standing under the streetlight, the dam broke. Issei’s legs gave out, and he slid down the brick wall, burying his face in his trembling hands.

"Ren... Ren, it was a massacre," Issei choked out, his voice cracking into a ragged sob. "There was so much blood. The guy just wanted a contract... he just wanted a Devil to help him... and that psycho chopped him to pieces. He was laughing, Ren. He was just laughing."

Ren knelt on the pavement in front of him. He didn't offer a hollow platitude like *it’s going to be okay*. He grabbed Issei’s wrists, pulling his hands away from his face, and looked directly into his terrified eyes.

"Breathe, Issei," Ren ordered, his tone devoid of panic, acting as an emotional anchor. "Four seconds in. Four seconds out. Do it."

Issei stuttered, gasping for air, but the absolute, unshakable calm radiating from Ren forced his lungs to comply. He took a jagged breath, then another, the violent trembling in his shoulders slowly reducing to a dull shake.

"Report," Ren said softly, using the exact, clinical tone a field medic would use on a shell-shocked soldier. "Whose blood is on your uniform? You aren't wounded."

"It’s the client's," Issei whispered, his eyes widening as the memory replayed. "I went to a house to fulfill a summon. But there was a priest waiting. His name was Freed Sellzen. He had a gun that shot light, and a sword made of light. He just... slaughtered the guy. And he was going to kill me, too."

Ren’s mind processed the data with cold, terrifying efficiency.

*A human priest wielding anti-supernatural, light-based weaponry. The Church has active exorcists operating in Kuoh. The Fallen Angels aren't the only hostile faction in the city.*

"How did you survive?" Ren asked, keeping his grip on Issei’s wrists to keep him grounded.

"Asia," Issei said, the name tearing out of his throat like a jagged piece of glass. "She was there. The nun we helped in the plaza. She’s... she’s traveling with Freed. When he went to execute me, she jumped in the way. She healed me. Freed backhanded her... he bashed her across the room just for helping me."

Ren’s eyes narrowed slightly. *The girl with the Twilight Healing is embedded with a psychotic exorcist. She isn't a combatant; she’s a captive asset being utilized for field triage.*

"Then President Rias arrived," Issei continued, tears finally spilling over his bruised cheeks. "The whole club teleported in. They stopped Freed. But... but we didn't stay. Rias said we couldn't risk a full-scale war with the Fallen Angels right now. She opened a magic circle and pulled us out." Issei looked down at his own hands, his voice dropping to a whisper of absolute self-loathing. "We left her, Ren. She saved my life, and I just left her on the floor with that monster. I'm a coward."

Ren let go of Issei’s wrists and sat back on his heels, analyzing the sheer, brutal reality of the situation.

Issei was viewing this through the lens of a high schooler. He thought he was the protagonist of a heroic story, where the good guys always save the innocent girl. But Ren saw the exact, pragmatic logic of Rias Gremory’s decision.

Rias was not a superhero. She was a sovereign commander managing enemy territory. She had assessed the battlefield, recognized that her Pawn was safe, and calculated that engaging an armed exorcist backed by Fallen Angels over a human nun was a massive strategic liability. Asia Argento was not part of the Gremory peerage; therefore, she was expendable. Rias had made a ruthless, tactically sound choice to protect her own family.

Ren respected the logic. It was exactly the kind of cold calculus he would expect from a veteran leader. But it also cemented a chilling truth in his heart: to the high-tier entities of this world, mortal lives were nothing but numbers on a spreadsheet.

Ren stood up. He looked down at Issei, who was still drowning in his own guilt.

"Rias made a commander's call, Issei," Ren said, his voice flat, cutting through the emotional static. "She protected her unit. You can't hate her for that. But you can hate yourself for being too weak to override her order."

Issei looked up, stunned by the bluntness of the statement.

"You have a dragon’s gauntlet inside your arm," Ren continued, his dark eyes reflecting the flickering amber light of the streetlamp. "You survived an execution. Asia paid for that survival in blood. If you want to repay that debt, sitting in an alleyway crying about how unfair the world is won't accomplish anything. Are you a Devil, or are you just a kid playing dress-up?"

The words were harsh, entirely stripped of typical friendly comfort, but they were exactly what Issei needed to hear. The panic in Issei’s eyes slowly began to recede, replaced by a flickering, desperate spark of anger. Not anger at Ren, but anger at his own powerlessness.

Issei slowly pushed himself off the pavement, his legs unsteady but holding his weight. He wiped his face with the back of his bloodstained sleeve, his jaw tightening.

"I have to see her," Issei muttered, staring at the ground, his fists clenching at his sides. "I'm going to find her tomorrow. I have to know if she's okay."

"Then go home, wash the blood off your uniform, and get your head straight," Ren replied smoothly, slipping his headphones back over his ears. "Panic gets you killed. Resolve gets you answers."

As Issei gave a stiff, determined nod and began to limp down the street toward his house, Ren watched him go. The naive, perverted high schooler was finally starting to crack, forced to confront the violent reality of the supernatural world.

Ren tapped the screen of his phone, resuming the animated training sequence of Silvers Rayleigh. As he turned and walked in the opposite direction, descending back into the dark, his own resolve crystallized into absolute certainty. Faction war was brewing in Kuoh, and the time for blindfolded drills was over.

### Section 2: The Innocent's Walk

The mid-day sun beat down on the crowded shopping district of Kuoh, casting harsh shadows across the pavement. The weekend foot traffic was a chaotic, unpredictable ocean of moving bodies.

Ren walked directly through the center of it, his eyes glued to the glowing screen of his smartphone, his ears sealed beneath heavy, noise-canceling headphones. He was currently watching a high-speed tactical breakdown of CQC weapon disarms, letting the rapid-fire visual data and the instructor's loud voice dominate his conscious brain. Yet, his body moved with the fluid, frictionless grace of water flowing through rocks. He sidestepped a running child, pivoted his shoulders to avoid a woman carrying grocery bags, and instinctively adjusted his stride to match the shifting density of the crowd. His open-eye sensory training was holding perfectly.

Then, two massive, contrasting blips of intent painted themselves across his internal radar.

One was a heavy, dense furnace of demonic energy—a localized pocket of heat that felt like a coiled spring. Right beside it was a soft, weightless aura of pure, golden warmth, vibrating with an innocent, almost tragic lack of malice.

Ren didn't even look up from his screen as he adjusted his trajectory, seamlessly merging into their path. He stopped walking exactly two feet in front of them, sliding his phone into his pocket and pulling his headphones down around his neck in one smooth motion.

Issei nearly dropped the two strawberry crepes he was holding, letting out a startled yelp. Beside him, Asia Argento blinked in surprise, wearing her nun's habit and a bright, innocent smile.

"Jesus, Ren!" Issei gasped, clutching his chest. "Where did you even come from? You move like a ghost!"

"I was just taking a walk," Ren said, his dark eyes scanning the perimeter before settling on his friend. He looked Issei up and down, noting the clean casual clothes and the completely relaxed posture. "I have to admit, Issei, your turnaround time is impressive. Last night you were covered in blood, having a panic attack against a brick wall because she was trapped with a psychotic priest. Today, you're buying her crepes. Walk me through the tactical pivot. Are we on a rescue mission, or is this a date?"

Issei flushed a violent shade of red, frantically waving a crepe in the air. "It's not—we just ran into each other! I was looking for her, and she was out running an errand, so I decided to show her around town! She's never been to a Japanese arcade before."

"It is wonderful to see you again, Ren-san," Asia bowed deeply, her emerald eyes shining with genuine warmth. "Issei-san has been so kind to me today. He even won me a stuffed bear."

Ren looked at the small plush bear clutched in Asia's hands, then back at Issei. The stark contrast between the brutal reality of the supernatural world and the naive innocence of this afternoon was jarring. Ren gestured toward a nearby cluster of park benches sitting beneath the shade of a large oak tree.

"Let's sit," Ren suggested smoothly. "Eating while walking ruins the digestion anyway."

They moved to the bench. As Issei handed Asia her crepe and excitedly explained the mechanics of a UFO catcher, Ren kept his posture relaxed, resting his elbows on his knees. But his eyes never stopped moving. He casually scanned the rooftops, the alleyways, and the faces of the crowd. He was acting as their invisible sentry.

"So, Asia," Ren asked, taking a sip from his water bottle. "You mentioned yesterday that you were assigned to the old church. But you're walking around town with a Devil, and you live with Fallen Angels. The ecclesiastical politics there seem a bit tangled. If you don't mind me asking, why are you really here?"

Asia’s bright smile faltered. She lowered her crepe, her fingers tightening around the paper wrapper. Issei shot Ren a look of panic, silently begging him not to pry, but Ren ignored him. Information was survival.

"I... I am no longer a part of the Church," Asia said softly, her voice carrying the heavy weight of a profound tragedy. She looked down at her lap. "I possessed the Twilight Healing since I was a child. I was raised in the Church, praised as a Holy Maiden. My only purpose was to heal the sick and the wounded in the name of the Lord. But one day, a Devil was brought before me, gravely injured. He was in so much pain."

She looked up, a single tear pooling in the corner of her eye. "I healed him. I didn't think about his faction or his race. I just saw someone suffering, and I used my gift. But the Church leadership discovered what I had done. They branded me a witch. They said my light was corrupted for aiding a demon. I was excommunicated and cast out."

Issei looked absolutely furious, his fists trembling on his knees. "It's so messed up, Ren. She did the right thing, and they threw her away! That's why she's with the Fallen Angels now. They took her in when she had nowhere else to go."

Ren did not express outrage. He didn't offer a warm, comforting smile. His mind was an absolute void of emotion, rapidly assembling the tactical data into a terrifying, cohesive picture.

*A high-value, area-of-effect healer. Excommunicated, meaning the Church will not send reinforcements to retrieve her. Taken in by Fallen Angels, a faction actively operating in enemy Devil territory.*

"They didn't take you in out of charity, Asia," Ren stated, his voice flat, cutting through Issei's emotional defense like a scalpel.

Both Issei and Asia looked at him, startled by the coldness in his tone.

"Ren, what are you talking about?" Issei frowned, defensive of the fragile peace they were enjoying.

"Look at the board, Issei," Ren said, leaning back and crossing his arms. He lowered his voice, ensuring only they could hear him over the ambient noise of the plaza. "The Fallen Angels are operating covertly in a city controlled by the Gremory clan. They employ a psychotic exorcist who executes contractors in cold blood. Does that sound like a charity organization that takes in stray nuns out of the goodness of their hearts?"

Asia shrank back slightly, hugging her plush bear tighter.

"They don't care about your faith, Asia," Ren continued brutally, because lying to them would get them killed. "They care about your Sacred Gear. You are an unaligned, top-tier medical asset. They aren't your saviors; they are your wardens. They are keeping you close because a healer of your caliber shifts the tactical balance of any faction war."

Issei’s eyes widened as the logic clicked into place. The realization hit him like a physical blow. "Wait... if they're just using her for her gear... then why did they let her wander around town by herself today?"

"They didn't," Ren said softly.

Ren’s neuro-somatic radar had been tracking the environment since the moment he sat down. For the last ten minutes, he had felt a heavy, suffocating pressure hovering just at the edge of his sensory range. It was a familiar, predatory coldness.

"They let her off the leash just to see where she would go," Ren warned, his eyes locking onto Issei with absolute, grave intensity. "And to see who she was talking to. You are a Gremory piece, Issei. You represent enemy territory. They are not going to let a high-value asset just walk away and play arcade games with a Devil."

Ren stood up, slipping his headphones back over his ears but leaving the audio off.

"Keep your guard up, Issei," Ren ordered quietly. "The date is over. Get her out of the open, and do not trust the shadows."

Before Issei could ask another question, Ren turned and blended seamlessly back into the crowded street, leaving the two of them sitting on the bench. Ren didn't go far. He simply circled the perimeter, melting into the blind spots of the plaza, waiting for the inevitable ambush to fall.

### Section 3: The Pragmatist’s Restraint

Ren did not stay in the shadows for long. Less than three minutes after he had melted into the crowd, he re-emerged from an alleyway, his expression carved from granite. He walked briskly back to the bench where Issei and Asia were still sitting in tense silence.

"Get up," Ren ordered quietly. "We’re boxed in. I mapped the perimeter. They’ve locked down the immediate sightlines and cleared the civilian foot traffic from the eastern plaza. It’s impossible to avoid them now."

Issei jumped to his feet, panic flaring in his eyes, but Ren kept his focus entirely on the nun.

"Asia," Ren said, dropping his voice to a low, tactical whisper. "You are a woman of the cloth. Do you have any holy water on you?"

Asia blinked, startled by the strange urgency of the request, but she reached into the deep pockets of her habit. "I... yes. A small vial for personal prayer. Why?"

"Give it to me," Ren commanded, holding out his hand. Asia placed the tiny glass vial into his palm. Ren instantly popped the cork, drew his training karambit, and carefully poured the blessed liquid over the curved steel blade. The water clung to the metal, shimmering with a faint, imperceptible light.

Before Issei could ask what Ren was doing, the temperature in the plaza plummeted. The warm afternoon sun was suddenly eclipsed by a localized shadow, and a suffocating, dense malice flooded the air.

"Such a touching display of solidarity," a sadistic, melodic voice echoed from above.

Raynare descended from the sky, her massive black wings absorbing the light around her. She landed on the cobblestone with a soft click of her heels, wearing her revealing dominatrix attire and a smile of absolute, predatory arrogance.

Asia stood up, stepping out from behind Issei. Her hands were trembling, but her emerald eyes carried a heartbreaking resignation.

"Ren-san was right," Asia said, her voice wavering as she looked at Issei. "They do not care about my faith. Lady Raynare told me this morning that she would grant me one final day to spend freely with you, Issei-san. A farewell, before I return to the church to fulfill my purpose."

She turned slightly, offering Ren a sad, fragile smile. "I am glad you found us, Ren-san. When I first met Issei, you were there beside him. I am happy that I was able to spend my last moments of freedom with both of you."

"Nobody is saying farewell!" Issei roared, his terror entirely vaporized by righteous, explosive fury. He stepped firmly in front of Asia, shielding her with his body.

A mechanical, draconic pulse shattered the air.

*BOOST!*

A shockwave of crimson energy blasted outward. The heavy red plates of the Boosted Gear materialized over Issei’s left arm, the green jewel glowing with raw heat. The fabric of his shirt tore as two leathery Devil wings ripped from his back, casting a demonic shadow over the pavement.

Ren flipped the holy-water-coated karambit into a reverse grip, lowering his center of gravity. "I hope you can actually fight this time, Issei," he said, his neuro-somatic breathing locking his heart rate into a cold, rhythmic thud. "Because running isn't an option."

"I'm going to smash her!" Issei yelled, recklessly charging forward, reeling his heavy gauntlet back for a devastating right hook.

Raynare laughed, summoning a spear of crackling blue light into her hand. "A Pawn playing hero? Die."

She sidestepped Issei’s clumsy, telegraphed punch with zero effort, raising her spear to impale him through the ribs. But Issei was exactly what Ren needed: a loud, high-threat distraction.

Ren engaged. He didn't run; he slid.

Exploiting the blind spot created by Raynare’s focus on Issei, Ren slipped completely under her guard. His neuro-somatic conditioning allowed him to move with terrifying, frictionless velocity. He didn't aim for a fatal blow against her reinforced collarbone or sternum. He aimed for the anatomical seams.

*Slash.*

The holy-water-coated blade dragged across the back of Raynare’s knee joint. The moment the blessed steel touched her skin, the holy water violently reacted with her dark aura.

Raynare shrieked in absolute agony, her knee buckling as the holy magic burned through her flesh like battery acid. She swung her light spear wildly down at Ren, but Ren was already gone. He pivoted on his heel, letting the spear crash into the pavement where he had just been standing. As Raynare overextended, Ren drove the karambit into her lateral obliques, twisting the blade and pulling it out before she could even register the secondary pain.

"GAAAH! You miserable little worm!" Raynare screamed, her arrogant composure shattering as she bled from two seared, burning wounds.

She lunged at Ren, abandoning Issei entirely. But Ren was in his element. His open-eye sensory training was flawless. He watched her shoulder drop, felt the shift in air pressure, and effortlessly slipped past her thrusts. Every time she missed, Ren countered with surgical, terrifying precision—a nick to the wrist, a shallow cut across her tricep. The holy water was bypassing her supernatural durability entirely.

Ren was dismantling a Fallen Angel with a two-inch blade.

"I'll kill you! I'll skin you alive!" Raynare shrieked, backing away as she realized she was losing to a baseline human. "Kalawarner! Dohnaseek! Kill them!"

The air above the plaza rippled. Ren’s internal radar spiked violently.

Two more heavy, predatory auras crashed into the battlefield. Kalawarner dropped from the sky, her blue hair whipping behind her, alongside a trench-coated Fallen Angel in a fedora. All three of them instantly summoned massive spears of light, illuminating the plaza in a blinding, lethal glow.

*The tactical math just inverted,* Ren realized, his mind operating at a million miles an hour.

"Die!" Dohnaseek roared, hurling a light spear directly at Ren's chest.

Ren didn't blink. The hazard gauntlet he had built in the warehouse had prepared him for exactly this. He felt the murderous intent radiate from the spear before it even left the Fallen Angel's hand. Ren dropped into a flawless backward roll, the spear scorching the air a millimeter above his nose and detonating against the bench behind him.

A barrage of light rained down. Ren moved like a phantom. He sidestepped Kalawarner’s thrust, vaulted over a shattered concrete planter to avoid an explosive blast from Raynare, and ducked under a horizontal sweep. His neuro-somatic focus reached its absolute zenith; he felt the wind, the heat, and the intent of every strike, dodging a three-on-one assault from supernatural executioners without taking a single scratch.

But Issei did not have neuro-somatic training.

"GAAAH!" Issei screamed as a light spear grazed his shoulder, burning his skin black. He stumbled, swinging his gauntlet blindly, only for Dohnaseek to kick him brutally in the chest. Issei crashed onto the cobblestone, coughing up blood.

Ren smoothly backflipped away from another explosion, landing in a crouch. He looked over and saw Kalawarner raise a spear directly over Issei’s throat. Ren could dodge all day, but he didn't have the offensive output to stop three Fallen Angels at once, and Issei was out of time.

"Stop!"

Asia’s voice cracked over the sound of crackling light. She ran forward, throwing herself between the Fallen Angels and Issei’s battered body. Tears streamed down her face, but she looked up at Raynare with desperate resolve.

"I will go with you," Asia sobbed, her voice trembling. "I won't fight. I won't run. Just please... leave Issei-san alone! Don't hurt them anymore!"

"Asia, no!" Issei choked out, reaching for her habit with a bloodied hand. "Don't do it!"

Raynare smiled, a cruel, triumphant sneer returning to her face as she lowered her spear. "See? Was that so difficult, little nun? You always belonged to us."

Raynare grabbed Asia roughly by the arm. Kalawarner and Dohnaseek sneered at Issei's pathetic state on the ground, entirely ignoring the human who had just danced through their crossfire. With a rush of displacing wind, a black magic circle enveloped the Fallen Angels and the weeping nun.

In a blink of an eye, the plaza was empty.

Ren stood slowly, his grip loosening on his karambit. He hadn't taken a single hit. His breathing was steady. But as he looked down at Issei, who was sobbing into the cracked pavement, Ren felt the bitter, icy reality of his existence settle into his bones. He could dodge the wrath of gods, but dodging wasn't enough to save anyone.

## Life 4: The Rescue of the Holy Maiden

### Section 1: The Pawn’s Gambit

#### Sub Section 1: The Gathering in the Clubroom

The journey across town to the Old School Building was quiet. Ren had expected the headquarters of a High-Class Devil to feel like an impenetrable fortress—guarded by magical tripwires, lethal wards, and stoic sentries. Instead, Issei simply pushed open the heavy oak doors of the Occult Research Club, and Ren walked right in behind him.

The scene waiting for them inside shattered every intimidating rumor Ren had ever heard about the Gremory clan.

Rias Gremory stood near her desk in the middle of changing out of her school uniform. She was dressed in nothing more than a pair of crimson lace panties and a matching brassiere, holding a crisp white blouse in her hands. Her long, scarlet hair draped over her bare shoulders like liquid silk.

Issei slammed the door open, ready to scream his heart out. "PRESIDENT! YOU HAVE TO HELP ME SAVE ASIA—"

He froze dead in his tracks.

Issei’s eyes locked onto Rias’s nearly bare form. His jaw dropped, his face flushed a violent, incandescent shade of red, and within half a second, twin geysers of blood erupted violently from his nostrils, splattering across the wooden floorboards.

"Issei?!" Rias gasped, quickly holding the blouse over her chest, though she didn't look particularly angry—just thoroughly flustered. "What are you doing bursting in here without knocking?!"

"P-President!" Issei yelled, holding his hands over his face to catch the streaming nosebleed while frantically trying to maintain his moral outrage. "I don't care if you're in your underwear! Asia is in danger! The Fallen Angels took her back to the church, and they're going to kill her! We have to go right now!"

"We are doing no such thing!" Rias snapped back, stepping behind a folding screen to finish buttoning her shirt. Her tone was sharp, but beneath the political authority, her voice carried a clear, undeniable wave of genuine worry. "Do you have any idea how dangerous that church is right now, Issei? You barely survived a single encounter! I am responsible for your life. As your master, I cannot and will not allow you to throw yourself into a suicide mission!"

"She saved my life!" Issei shouted back, his nose bleeding so hard it was dripping through his fingers. "I don't care if I die! I'm going!"

Beside the sofa, Akeno giggled softly into her palm, clearly enjoying the chaos, while Kiba smirked, leaning against the wall with his arms crossed.

Koneko sat on the couch, calmly nibbling on a chocolate bar. She glanced at Issei’s bloody face and deadpanned, "Your sudden burst of heroic righteousness is heavily undermined by the fact that you are currently hemorrhaging from sexual excitement."

Ren stood near the doorway, resting his hand casually in his jacket pocket as he took in the entire absurd scene.

*So this is the terrifying Gremory peerage,* Ren thought, a faint, amused smile tugging at the corner of his lips.

He had mentally prepped himself for a dark cabal of ruthless demon lords. Instead, he was standing in a cozy, antique lounge watching a teenage boy argue about international faction politics while bleeding out of his nose because his boss was changing her clothes. The terrifying rumors of the Occult Research Club as an unfeeling, untouchable fortress were wildly exaggerated. They weren't a cold military unit—they were just an oddly warm, deeply dysfunctional family. And the fact that they had casually allowed a baseline human like Ren to walk directly into the King's dressing room proved they were far more laid-back than their terrifying aura suggested.

Before the argument could escalate any further, Akeno stepped forward, holding a glowing crimson parchment.

"President," Akeno interrupted smoothly, her playful smile dimming into a professional expression. "An urgent summons just arrived from the Underworld. Lady Sona and the Sitri clan are waiting for us at the territorial boundary."

Rias stepped out from behind the screen, fully dressed in her school uniform. She sighed, looking at Issei with a mixture of stern authority and soft concern.

"We have to leave immediately," Rias said, walking toward the large magic circle forming on the floor. She paused beside Issei, looking him dead in the eye. "Listen to me, Issei. You think you are weak because you are a Pawn. But you are wrong. In the game of Devil chess, a Pawn carries a special rule. When a Pawn reaches the deepest rank of enemy territory... it Promotes. It gains the power of any piece it chooses."

Kiba chimed in smoothly, "The abandoned church is controlled by Fallen Angels. It is, by definition, deep enemy territory. If you make it inside, your Evil Piece will allow you to promote."

Ren, who had been quietly observing from the sidelines, finally stepped into the conversation.

"I don't know much about Devil chess," Ren said, his calm, level voice drawing everyone's attention. "But in standard human chess, Pawns aren't just fodder. They are the entire structural spine of the board. You use pawn structures to lock down the center, pawn storms to shatter the enemy King's defense, and pawn marches to force your opponent into terrible trades. A Pawn isn't a sacrifice—it's a slow, unstoppable executioner waiting for the eighth rank."

Rias stopped right at the edge of the magic circle, turning her head to look at Ren with genuine surprise. Her blue-green eyes narrowed slightly, inspecting the mortal boy with newfound intrigue.

"You understand strategic board theory?" Rias asked, a subtle spark of respect in her voice. "In this entire academy, Sona Sitri is the only person who speaks about chess with that level of analytical depth."

Rias shook her head, a faint smile appearing on her face before the crimson light engulfed her and Akeno. "We must go. Kiba, I leave the clubroom in your hands."

With a flash of red light, the two high-class devils vanished.

The room fell quiet. Kiba straightened up from the wall, walking over to Issei.

"So," Kiba asked, his tone remarkably light. "Are you really going to the church, Issei?"

"It's obvious!" Issei snapped, wiping his nose. "I'm going whether the President likes it or not!"

"You know," Kiba chuckled softly, "if you go alone, you're just going to die before you even step foot in the building. And then how are you going to save her?" Kiba reached down and drew his sleek silver sword half an inch from its scabbard, the metal gleaming in the sunlight. "Which is why I'm coming with you."

Issei blinked in shock. "Kiba... but what about the President's orders?"

"If the President *truly* wanted to forbid you from going," Kiba smiled mischievously, "she wouldn't have just scolded you. She would have bound you in leather restraints, locked you in the basement, and guarded the door herself."

Issei froze. A dazed, incredibly perverted expression washed over his face as his brain processed the image. "Wait... leather bondage restraints? Man... now I kind of wish she *did* forbid me..."

"Gross," Koneko's flat voice cut through his fantasy.

The tiny white-haired girl stood up from the couch, wiping the chocolate from her fingers. She cracked her knuckles with a sound like snapping wood. "I'll go too. For Issei's sake. He'll die in five seconds if there's no one around to hit things for him."

Issei’s eyes welled up with massive, dramatic tears of absolute gratitude. "KONEKO-CHAN!"

Driven by pure, unadulterated emotion, Issei lunged forward and wrapped his arms around her in a suffocating bear hug.

Koneko’s expression didn't change, but her eyes narrowed into sharp slits. "Too tight."

*BAM!*

With a terrifying display of casual Rook strength, Koneko grabbed Issei by the face and launched him across the room. Issei slammed violently into the leather couch, flipping over the back of it.

"THANK YOU SO MUCH!" Issei popped right back up, tears streaming down his face, and charged forward again. "YOU GUYS ARE THE BEST!"

"Too loud," Koneko said.

*BAM!*

She sidestepped his lunge, grabbed him by his collar, and threw him headfirst into the wall, cracking the plaster.

Issei bounced off the wall, completely unfazed by the brain damage, and lunged in for a third hug. "KONEKO-CHAAAN!"

*BAM!*

Kiba stood next to Ren, watching Issei get repeatedly pulverized and hurled across the clubroom like a ragdoll. Kiba rubbed the back of his neck, chuckling sheepishly as he looked at Ren.

"You know..." Kiba muttered softly, feeling a little left out of the emotional reunion. "I offered to help first. He didn't even try to hug me."

Ren looked over at Kiba, taking a slow, measured breath of air to keep from laughing out loud.

"Give it up, Kiba," Ren said dryly, patting the handsome Knight on the shoulder. "You're a great guy, but you don't have breasts or cat ears. His brain is physically incapable of registering your emotional availability."

#### Sub Section 2: The Pawn's Infiltration

The treeline surrounding the abandoned church was thick with damp mist, casting jagged, unnatural shadows across the overgrown perimeter.

Under the cover of a dense oak canopy three hundred yards from the boundary fence, Kiba unrolled a worn architectural schematic across a flat stone. He weighed down the corners with four heavy pebbles, pointing his finger at the center of the parchment.

"I managed to pull the original construction blueprints from the municipal registry," Kiba whispered, his voice hushed and professional. "The sanctuary sits at the core of the floor plan. If they're holding Asia for a ritual, it's almost certainly happening on the primary altar directly beneath the central nave. The front courtyard is wide open—zero cover."

"Then we smash through the double doors, clear the nave, and take her back!" Issei growled, slamming his fist into his palm, his Boosted Gear flickering with impatient crimson embers. "Koneko can break the gates, Kiba clears the hallway, and I'll go straight for Raynare!"

Koneko gave a small, stoic nod, adjusting her fingerless gloves. "I can tear the main doors off their hinges."

As the three of them prepared to break cover and charge into the courtyard, a hand reached out from the darkness, catching Issei firmly by the back of his collar and yanking him back into the brush.

"Wait," Ren commanded softly.

Issei stumbled backward, glaring at his friend. "Ren! What are you doing?! Every second we waste standing around here, Asia is in danger!"

Ren didn't look flustered. He remained crouched in the undergrowth, his dark eyes fixed on the illuminated stained-glass windows of the church. His neuro-somatic focus was fully engaged; he could feel the heavy, oppressive malice radiating off the building, along with the distinct thermal signatures of armed men patrolling the perimeter walls.

"We talked about chess in the clubroom," Ren said, his voice flat and dangerously calm. "There’s a famous rule laid down by Emanuel Lasker, one of the greatest grandmasters in history: *When you see a good move, always look for a better one.*"

Kiba paused, looking at Ren with mild curiosity. "A better move?"

"Kicking the front door down is a move," Ren explained, pointing a tactical finger toward the map. "It’s aggressive, direct, and predictable. But what is our actual win condition here? Is our objective to kill every exorcist in the city, or is it to secure Asia Argento?"

"To save Asia, obviously!" Issei snapped.

"Exactly," Ren continued. "Asia is the objective. In tactical terms, she is the high-value piece currently sitting on the enemy's side of the board. If you charge through the front gate, you kick off an alarm. You draw fifty armed priests into a choke point, force ourselves into a prolonged attrition fight, and give Raynare a ten-minute warning to either execute Asia or finish whatever ritual she’s performing before we even cross the threshold."

Ren looked at Kiba, then at Koneko. "A real chess player doesn't sacrifice momentum to pick off random pawns if he can slip behind enemy lines and threaten the objective directly. We don't need a siege. We need a stealth extraction."

Kiba stared at the map, then looked up at Ren with a slow, approving grin. "You want to bypass the front line entirely."

"Look at the blueprints," Ren said, tapping the rear section of the building. "There’s an old drainage maintenance shaft running under the eastern wing, leading straight into the upper choir balcony overlooking the main altar. The roof ventilation above the sanctuary is structurally degraded. We infiltrate silently, locate Asia, drop in directly on top of her, and secure her before Raynare realizes the perimeter was breached."

Koneko looked at Ren, her expression softening into a rare look of genuine tactical appreciation. "Smart," she murmured.

"Alright," Issei gritted his teeth, swallowing his reckless urge to scream. "We do it your way. But we move *fast*."

Using Ren’s heightened sensory radar as their compass, the four-man squad moved like ghosts through the shadows.
