Ren led the vanguard, his open-eye training mapping the patrols before they even rounded a corner. He felt the shifting weight of the armed exorcists on the gravel, guiding Kiba and Koneko around the blind spots of the searchlights.

They bypassed the front gates entirely, scaling the rusted iron drainage pipe along the church's rear flank and slipping through a broken stained-glass rose window thirty feet above the ground.

Within two minutes, they were crouching silently behind the marble balustrade of the high choir balcony, hidden in the deep shadows near the vaulted ceiling.

Ren dropped to one knee, peering down through the decorative stone pillars.

The main sanctuary below was a nightmare carved from dark magic and blood.

In the center of the nave, floating three feet above a massive, glowing purple magic circle, was Asia Argento. She was bound in dark, ethereal chains, her face pale as snow, her eyes closed in absolute agony as green, luminescent strands of pure energy were slowly being dragged from her chest. Standing directly beneath her was Raynare, her black wings unfurled, her eyes burning with sadistic ecstasy as she absorbed the floating light into a pair of green, crystalline rings hovering in her hands.

Surrounding the altar were at least thirty robed exorcists, wielding heavy silver crucifixes and automatic pistols loaded with light-blessed rounds, chanting a low, guttural incantation in Latin.

*She's extracting the Sacred Gear,* Ren cataloged instantly, his neuro-somatic mind running the cold math. *The ritual is fifty percent complete. Asia's life force is tethered to the Twilight Healing. If that extraction finishes, she dies.*

Ren reached into his tactical vest, quietly drawing two smoke grenades and sliding his karambit into a reverse grip. He turned his head to look at Kiba, Koneko, and Issei, keeping his voice to a imperceptible whisper.

"Alright," Ren began, his analytical mind executing the final sequence. "Here’s the extraction protocol. Koneko, you drop first to break their central formation and draw their fire. Kiba, you use your speed to cut Asia’s chains the second the smoke pops. I’ll provide suppressive throwing blades to blind their primary shooters. Issei, the moment she falls, you grab her and—"

Ren stopped mid-sentence.

Beside him, Issei was no longer listening. He was staring down at Asia’s agonizing face, his breath coming in ragged, violent gasps. The green jewel on his crimson gauntlet flared with an uncontrollable, blinding dragon fire.

*BOOST! BOOST! BOOST!*

The deafening, mechanical roar of the Sacred Gear shattered the silence of the cathedral like a bomb, echoing violently off the vaulted ceiling.

Ren’s eyes widened in sheer, utter disbelief. "Issei, wait—"

"ASIAAAAAAAAAA!"

Issei screamed at the absolute top of his lungs, completely abandoning the balcony, the smoke grenades, the stealth protocol, and the entire concept of tactical planning. Driven by pure, unadulterated, suicidal rage, Issei vaulted straight over the marble railing, launching himself thirty feet through the air directly into the middle of thirty armed exorcists and a High-Class Fallen Angel.

Ren froze on the balcony, his hand still holding a smoke grenade, his dark eyes staring at the empty space where his best friend had been sitting a microsecond ago.

Slowly, Ren reached up with his free hand and pinched the bridge of his nose, taking a long, deep, excruciatingly slow neuro-somatic breath to keep his brain from short-circuiting.

"I literally just explained chess to him," Ren muttered under his breath, his voice dripping with pure, exhausted exasperation.

### Section 2: The Cathedral of Blood**

#### Sub Section 1: The Mortal Vanguard

Issei crashed into the center of the nave like a crimson meteor, shattering the stone tiles and sending a shockwave that threw the closest exorcists off their feet.

Up in the balcony, Ren didn't waste another second on exasperation. His neuro-somatic radar instantly expanded, mapping the shifting battlefield. He picked up the thirty human priests reacting to Issei, but more alarmingly, he felt three massive, predatory spikes of cold malice descending rapidly from the church's belfry.

"Rias and Akeno aren't here yet. We bypassed the perimeter too fast," Ren said, his voice a rapid-fire tactical feed as he armed both smoke grenades. He looked at Kiba and Koneko. "Three more Fallen Angels are dropping through the ceiling in about five seconds. I can't pierce high-tier supernatural armor, but human bones break just fine. You two keep the Angels off Issei. I’ll clear the exorcists."

Kiba drew his silver sword, his eyes gleaming with lethal focus. "Leave the wings to us. Don't die, Ren."

Kiba and Koneko vaulted over the railing, plummeting into the fray just as the stained-glass skylights above the altar shattered. Kalawarner, Dohnaseek, and a third Fallen Angel in a gothic dress, Mittelt, descended with light spears blazing.

Ren pulled the pins on both grenades and tossed them over the edge.

*FSHHHHH.*

Thick, blinding gray smoke immediately flooded the rear of the sanctuary, swallowing the back line of chanting priests.

Ren dropped from the balcony, landing silently in the dense fog. He closed his eyes. The visual input was useless here, but his open-eye sensory training had prepared him perfectly for this exact scenario. He felt the panicked, erratic heartbeats of the exorcists, heard the panicked clicks of their gun safeties, and sensed the displaced air as they aimed blindly into the smoke.

A priest fired a wild shot. Ren felt the muzzle flash’s thermal bloom and stepped cleanly to the left, letting the bullet whistle past his ear. He flowed forward, entirely empty-handed. He gripped the priest's extended wrist, pivoted, and snapped the joint with a brutal, rotational lock. As the man dropped, Ren spun, delivering a precise, kinetic elbow strike to the temporal bone of a second priest, shutting his brain off instantly.

He was a phantom in the fog. He didn't rely on magic; he relied on perfect, frictionless biomechanics.

A larger exorcist lunged through the smoke, swinging a heavy, steel-capped ceremonial staff. Ren didn't back away. He stepped *into* the swing, passing the lethal arc of the weapon, grabbed the wood near the man's grip, and drove his knee into the priest's solar plexus. With a sharp twist of his hips, Ren stripped the staff from the man's hands.

Now armed with a six-foot reach, Ren became a localized natural disaster. He swept the legs out from under two priests, cracked the staff against the jaw of a third, and vaulted off a wooden pew to deliver a crushing downward strike to a fourth. His neuro-somatic breathing kept his heart rate perfectly stable as he systematically dismantled the human army.

"HA! A little rat playing in the fog!"

A maniacal, grating laugh ripped through the smoke. Freed Sellzen charged into the fray, dual-wielding a smoking exorcist pistol and a blazing blade of pure light. His movements were frantic, enhanced by church magic to be vastly faster and stronger than a baseline human.

Freed fired three shots, instantly closing the distance to slice Ren in half.

But Freed possessed no martial discipline—only chaotic, murderous intent. Ren felt the massive, telegraphed spike of aggression before Freed even pulled the trigger. Ren tilted his head, letting the bullets graze the collar of his jacket, and dropped his center of gravity.

As Freed swung the light sword in a lethal horizontal arc, Ren didn't try to block the unblockable blade. Instead, he thrust the blunt end of his wooden staff directly into Freed’s leading foot, pinning it to the stone floor. The sudden loss of momentum threw Freed’s violent swing completely off balance. Ren smoothly ducked under the light blade, used the staff as a fulcrum, and delivered a devastating, spinning palm strike directly to Freed’s throat.

Freed choked, his eyes bulging as his windpipe compressed. Before the psychotic priest could recover, Ren swept his staff behind Freed’s remaining leg and slammed him face-first into the cold stone floor, instantly dislocating Freed's shoulder with a brutal joint lock. The "mad dog" of the church had been flawlessly outmaneuvered by pure physics.

Meanwhile, at the front of the altar, the supernatural war raged.

Kiba was a blur of silver, parrying a storm of light spears from Dohnaseek and Kalawarner. The shockwaves of their clashes cracked the marble pillars. Koneko picked up entire wooden pews and hurled them like missiles at Mittelt, keeping the sky-bound Angel frantically dodging.

Issei charged straight through the chaos, ignoring the burns on his skin as he boosted his gauntlet to maximum capacity. "RAYNARE!" he roared, leaping onto the altar, drawing back a fist radiating enough draconian power to shatter a mountain.

Raynare didn't even look at him. Her eyes were locked onto the glowing purple magic circle above her.

"Too late, little Devil," Raynare whispered in absolute ecstasy.

With a sickening, ethereal tearing sound, the two glowing green rings of the Twilight Healing were violently ripped from Asia’s chest. The light surrounding the nun instantly died.

Issei’s punch connected with the magical barrier surrounding the altar, shattering it into a million pieces of glass, but the momentum threw him off balance. He crashed onto the marble steps.

Raynare absorbed the green rings into her own chest, letting out a euphoric gasp as the holy power fused with her dark aura.

Above the altar, the chains vanished. Asia Argento’s lifeless body plummeted toward the cold stone floor. Issei scrambled forward, catching her in his arms, but as he looked down at her pale, still face, the horrific reality set in. The extraction was complete.

#### Sub Section 2: Dying Nun

A deafening *BANG* shattered the gloom as Freed yanked a flashbang from his belt and detonated it at his own feet. White fire and a concussive shockwave ripped through the fog.

Ren didn't blink. His neuro-somatic radar had picked up the metallic pin-pull a fraction of a second before the detonation. He simply closed his eyes and adjusted his breathing, letting his body absorb the air displacement without flinching. When the smoke cleared, Freed was already scrambling through a shattered side window into the dark courtyard, clutching his broken shoulder. Ren watched him vanish, making no move to pursue. Chasing a manic, fleeing mercenary while the primary objective hung in the balance was an irrelevant distraction.

Across the nave, Issei screamed in pure, unadulterated grief. "RAYNARE!"

Ignoring his broken ribs, Issei lunged at the Fallen Angel, raising his Boosted Gear for a desperate swing. But Raynare, newly empowered by the stolen Sacred Gear glowing within her chest, merely sneered. She swatted Issei aside with a casual flick of her black wing. Issei crashed violently onto the stone floor, skidding across the altar steps, his blood staining the white marble.

Kiba and Koneko tried to push through to help him, but Kalawarner, Dohnaseek, and Mittelt locked them in a brutal stalemate, raining down a barrage of light spears that kept the Knight and Rook completely pinned.

Ren was already shifting his weight to intervene when the air inside the cathedral turned heavy and scorching hot. A massive, crimson magic circle erupted across the center floor, illuminating the high vaulted ceilings in blood-red light.

Rias Gremory and Akeno Himejima emerged from the light, their demonic aura radiating with suffocating authority.

"Rias..." Ren muttered, his dark eyes instantly spotting the opening.

With the High-Class Devils entering the fray, the battlefield shifted instantly. Ren didn't hesitate. Exploiting the momentary pause as the Fallen Angels recoiled from Rias's overwhelming presence, Ren sprinted onto the altar platform. He scooped Asia’s frail, cold body into his arms, leaped off the dais, and brought her straight to Issei.

"She's slipping, Issei," Ren said softly, gently transferring the dying nun into Issei's shaking arms. "Talk to her."

Above them, Rias's eyes burned with cold rage. "Akeno. Kiba. Koneko. Eradicate the intruders."

"With pleasure, President," Akeno smiled, her lightning crackling wildly across the rafters as she, Kiba, and Koneko swarmed Kalawarner, Dohnaseek, and Mittelt, driving them back into the shadows of the nave.

Raynare, realizing the situation had inverted, spread her wings to take flight through the shattered glass dome above. "This isn't over!"

*TAK.*

Ren vaulted off a marble pillar, using his wooden staff to propel himself high into the air. He landed directly on the railing of the choir loft above Raynare, cutting off her vertical escape path.

Raynare halted mid-air, hovering fifteen feet above the ground, glaring at the human boy with bitter contempt. "Get out of my way, baseline trash! You don't have your little holy water trick anymore. You can't even scratch me with that pathetic piece of wood!"

Ren rested the wooden pole across his shoulders, his expression entirely unreadable. "Let's test that theory."

Raynare thrust a spear of light directly at his chest. Ren tilted his head half an inch, letting the energy beam scorch the fabric of his collar, and stepped into her space.

He didn't need holy water to break physics.

Ren drove the tip of the wooden staff directly into the nerve cluster behind her shoulder joint. The kinetic force, amplified by his precise posture, bypassed her external magical barrier and transferred straight into her skeletal structure. Raynare gasped as her arm went numb, her light spear disintegrating.

Before she could recover, Ren spun the staff in a fluid, relentless spiral. *Thwack! Thwack! Thwack!*

The wood struck her elbows, her floating ribs, and the lateral tendons of her knees. He wasn't trying to slice her; he was systematically dismantling her balance, disrupting her center of gravity, and draining her stamina with flawless, unbroken martial pressure. Raynare shrieked in frustration, forced backward step by step, her wings flapping erratically as she tried and failed to land a single strike on the phantom-like human.

"I am entirely confident I can beat you to death right here, one-on-one," Ren said, his voice terrifyingly calm as he delivered a spinning strike to her jaw that sent her crashing onto the lower altar steps. "But you aren't my opponent tonight."

Ren lowered his staff and stepped back into the shadows of the altar.

On the floor below, Issei held Asia tightly against his chest, tears streaming down his face as her skin grew paler by the second.

Asia’s eyelids fluttered open, her faint emerald eyes looking up into Issei’s crying face. A small, heartbreakingly beautiful smile touched her lips.

"Issei-san..." Asia whispered, her voice barely a breath. "You came for me... You really came..."

"Don't talk, Asia!" Issei sobbed, holding her hand against his cheek. "Save your strength! The President is here! She can fix this!"

"I am... so happy," Asia murmured softly, reaching up with trembling fingers to wipe a tear from his eye. "I was always so lonely in the church... no one ever talked to me like a real person. But today... I got to walk in the city with you. I met Ren-san... and the others. I made real friends..."

She swallowed hard, her chest barely rising. "Issei-san... if... if I were to be reborn... would you... would you be my friend again? Would you... spend time with me...?"

"What are you saying?!" Issei roared through his tears, clutching her tighter. "Don't talk like you're dying! You're going to live! We're going to hang out every day! I'll take you back to the arcade! I'll introduce you to my friends, Matsuda and Motohama... sure, they're total perverts, but they're good guys, I swear! You're going to have so many friends, Asia!"

Asia’s smile widened, tears spilling over her pale cheeks. "You're crying for me..." she whispered softly. "Someone is crying... just for me... That was... all I ever wanted..."

Her hand slowly slipped from his cheek, falling limply onto the cold marble floor. Her eyes closed softly, the final faint breath escaping her lips.

"Asia...?" Issei whispered. "Asia... Hey! Wake up! ASIA!"

Silence answered him.

Issei lowered his head onto her chest, his shoulders shaking uncontrollably as a howl of pure, agonizing grief echoed through the shattered cathedral. "All she wanted... was a friend... She didn't care about factions... she just wanted to live..."

He looked up at Raynare, who was slowly pushing herself off the ground, clutching her bruised jaw. Issei's eyes were no longer those of a scared high school boy. They were burning with an ancient, terrifying dragon fire.

"Give her back..." Issei growled, his voice dropping into a guttural, terrifying register. "GIVE HER BACK TO ME!"

*BOOST! OVERBOOST!*

The green jewel on the Boosted Gear erupted in a blinding pillar of crimson fire that shattered the remaining stained-glass windows of the church. The raw, unleashed energy of the Welsh Dragon flooded Issei’s body, answering his desperate, agonizing cry for power.

Raynare gasped, backing away in terror. "No... no! Stay back!"

Issei didn't run. He vanished.

In a fraction of a second, Issei closed the distance, his boosted gauntlet roaring like a unleashed beast. He drove a massive, catastrophic right hook straight into Raynare's chest.

*BOOM!*

The shockwave tore through the nave. Raynare coughed up blood as the stolen Twilight Healing was violently purged from her body, the green rings of light bursting free from her chest and hovering in the air. Raynare was launched backward, crashing through the heavy stone pillars of the altar and collapsing into the rubble, broken and utterly defeated.

Issei stood amidst the settling dust, his breathing heavy, his crimson gauntlet smoking. He reached out his hand, and the two glowing rings of the Twilight Healing floated gently down into his palm.

He didn't celebrate. He turned around, walked slowly back to where Asia lay on the marble floor, and fell to his knees beside her, holding the Sacred Gear against his chest as the cathedral fell dead silent.


### Section 3: Rebirth and Residuals

#### Sub Section 1: The Forgotten Vanguard

The deafening roars of dragon fire and crackling lightning slowly faded, leaving the ruined cathedral in a heavy, reverent quiet.

Issei remained on his knees in the center of the shattered altar, trembling as he clutched the two glowing green rings of the Twilight Healing against his chest. Beside him, Asia lay completely still, her pale face bathed in the dim moonlight filtering through the shattered stained-glass dome.

Heavy, measured footsteps echoed across the stone floor. Rias Gremory walked up the altar steps, her crimson hair framing a gentle, sorrowful expression. Behind her, Akeno, Kiba, and Koneko gathered in a quiet semicircle, looking down at the fallen nun.

Rias lowered herself to one knee beside Issei. She reached out, her soft hand resting gently on Issei’s trembling shoulder.

"I heard her final words, Issei," Rias said softly, her tone carrying a profound, maternal warmth. "She wished with all her heart to be reborn... so that she could stay by your side and be your friend."

Issei looked up through bloodshot, tear-stained eyes, his voice choked with despair. "President..."

Rias reached into her blazer pocket and produced a glowing, intricately carved chess piece carved from polished ruby—a Bishop. It pulsated with a soft, dense demonic mana.

"It is a wish I have the power to grant," Rias continued, looking at Issei with absolute sincerity. "Her soul has not yet crossed the boundary. I can tether her back to this world. But... I must apologize to both of you. If I bring her back, she can no longer be a holy maiden of the Church, nor can she be human. She will be reborn as a Devil... as my piece, and your fellow comrade."

Issei didn't hesitate for even a fraction of a second. A wave of immense, overwhelming relief washed over his battered face, wiping away the dark shadow of grief. Tears spilled from his eyes anew, but this time born from pure, ecstatic hope.

"I don't care if she's a Devil, a demon, or anything else!" Issei cried out, nodding frantically. "Just save her, President! Please, bring Asia back!"

Rias offered a warm, reassuring smile. "Very well."

She placed the crimson Bishop piece gently onto Asia’s sternum. Rias raised her hand, reciting the ancient incantation of the High-Class Devil house of Gremory. A brilliant circle of crimson crests erupted beneath Asia’s body, radiating a soothing, life-giving heat. The ruby chess piece slowly sank through her clothes, dissolving directly into her chest cavity.

"Now, Issei," Rias instructed softly. "Give her back what belongs to her."

Issei leaned forward, his hands shaking as he gently pressed the two glowing rings of the Twilight Healing onto Asia's chest. The holy artifact resonated with her newly reborn Devil core, dissolving into particles of brilliant emerald light that flowed back into her bloodstream, sealing her internal wounds and rekindling the spark of life within her heart.

Asia’s chest rose with a sudden, deep gasp of air.

The pale, ghostly color drained from her skin, replaced by a soft, healthy flush. Her long eyelashes fluttered, and slowly, her deep emerald eyes opened, squinting against the dim light.

"Issei... san...?" Asia whispered faintly.

"ASIA!"

Issei burst into loud, unrestrained bawling, throwing his arms around her and pulling her into a fierce, desperate embrace. He buried his face in her shoulder, sobbing like a child. "You're alive! You're really alive! Don't you ever scare me like that again, you idiot!"

Asia blinked in utter confusion, but as she felt the warm, frantic beating of Issei’s heart and the wet tears soaking into her collar, a breathtakingly pure smile broke across her face. She raised her slender arms, wrapping them tightly around his back.

"Issei-san..." Asia wept softly, her own tears of joy flowing freely. "Thank you... thank you so much..."

Akeno giggled softly, resting a hand on her cheek, while Kiba let out a relieved, handsome chuckle. Even Koneko’s stoic face softened into a faint, approving nod.

Issei carefully slid his arms beneath Asia’s back and knees, scooping her up into a gentle bridal carry. He held her close against his chest as if she were made of delicate glass. Asia rested her head against his shoulder, clutching his shirt, completely safe.

"Let's go home," Rias said, her voice filled with pride as she turned toward the shattered main entrance of the church. "We have a lot of welcoming celebrations to prepare for our newest member."

"Right!" Issei beamed, wiping his nose with his shoulder as he carried Asia down the altar steps.

Rias, Akeno, Kiba, and Koneko fell into rank around Issei and Asia, forming a tight, protective honor guard. Laughing, chatting, and completely wrapped up in the emotional warmth of their miraculous victory, the Gremory peerage walked out through the archway, disappearing into the cool night air toward the Occult Research Club.

The heavy silence of the ruined cathedral settled back over the stone floor.

Dust motes drifted peacefully through the silver beams of moonlight streaming through the shattered ceiling. The wind whistled through the broken stained-glass windows, rustling the torn cloth of the abandoned pews.

Standing on the far side of the shadowy altar, resting a splintered six-foot wooden staff across his shoulder, was Ren.

He stood completely still, his dark eyes staring at the empty double doors where his best friend and a squad of high-class demons had just walked out. Not a single person had looked back. Not a single person had called his name.

Ren slowly reached up with his free hand, pinched the bridge of his nose, and let out a long, quiet, neuro-somatic exhale.

He looked down at his scuffed school shoes, then at the blood-stained wooden pole in his hand, and finally at the absolute wreckage of the empty church.

"Right," Ren said dryly to the empty room, his voice echoing off the cold stone walls. "Great team bonding, guys. I'll just walk."

#### Sub Section 2: Day After Flash Back

The autumn morning sun streamed through the stained-glass windows of the Occult Research Club, casting warm golden light across the lounge. Asia was happily drinking tea while Rias walked her through the basics of Devil society, and Koneko was quietly sharing a box of pastries with her.

Yet, sitting on the leather sofa, Issei couldn't concentrate on his tea. A heavy, nagging weight sat in the pit of his stomach.

*Man... I really screwed up yesterday,* Issei thought, staring down at his floating tea leaves as his mind drifted back to the previous night's return from the abandoned church.

*(Issei's Flashback)*

They had been walking back to the school grounds in high spirits. Asia was safe in Issei’s arms, Rias and Akeno were discussing the logistics of Asia’s official transfer into the Gremory peerage, and Koneko was nibbling on a congratulatory chocolate bar.

Suddenly, Kiba came to a dead halt on the sidewalk, tapping his chin with a thoughtful look.

"Wait..." Kiba muttered, looking around at the empty street. "I feel like... we forgot something really important."

Rias paused, turning her head. "Did we leave traces of demonic magic near the altar, Kiba?"

"Did I forget to collect the broken light spears?" Akeno tapped her cheek innocently.

Issei blinked, looking to his left, then to his right. His eyes widened in absolute, horrifying realization. "REN!" Issei shrieked, clutching his head in panic. "WE LEFT REN IN THE CATHEDRAL!"

Silence fell over the Gremory peerage.

Akeno giggled nervously into her sleeve. "Oh my... he was so quiet during the battle, I completely forgot he wasn't part of our telepathic network."

"He systematically dismantled thirty armed exorcists with a wooden pole," Koneko deadpanned, taking a slow bite of her chocolate. "And we left him alone in a dark, creepy ruin."

Rias’s face flushed a deep crimson—not from anger, but from sheer, unadulterated embarrassment. "Ah... he... he was very helpful, wasn't he? We really should have brought him back with us..."

"I'll go get him!" Kiba offered immediately, drawing upon his Knight speed. "I'm the fastest. Stay here!"

In a flash of silver light, Kiba bolted back toward the outskirts of town. Within two minutes, he was standing in the ruined nave of the abandoned church. The dust had settled, the moonlight shone through the broken roof, and the quiet whistling of the wind echoed through the empty hall.

Kiba walked up to the altar, looking around carefully. "Ren? Are you still here?"

Only silence answered him. The wooden staff Ren had used lay shattered on the marble floor, but the boy himself was completely gone.

When Kiba returned to the group and shook his head, Issei had felt a cold sweat break out across his back.

*(End of Issei's Flashback)*

Miles away, in a small, meticulously kept apartment, the morning sun was blocked out by heavy velvet curtains.

Ren sat cross-legged in the center of his bed, his eyes closed, his breathing measured and rhythmic. His neuro-somatic focus was tuned entirely inward, observing the turbulent currents flowing through his chest.

*They didn't mean anything bad by it,* Ren narrated silently to himself, opening his memory bank to yesterday’s midnight silence inside the cathedral.

*(Ren's Flashback)*

Standing alone on the ruined altar after the Gremory peerage walked away, Ren had felt a cold, quiet reality settle over him.

*Maybe I really am just an outsider,* Ren had thought, staring at the empty double doors. *I'm not part of their little family. That's just how the world works.*

He took a slow breath, shaking his head to clear the thought. *Stop being overly sensitive, Ren. You aren't a tragic protagonist in a melodrama. Just jog after them and catch up.*

He adjusted his jacket and turned to leave. But before his foot could touch the lower altar step, a sudden, violent vibration erupted from the base of his spine.

It wasn't physical. It was a cold, suffocating surge of primordial shadow echoing directly inside his mind, accompanied by a raspy, unnatural whisper that rasped against his soul:

*Rennnnnn......*

Ren froze mid-stride, his pupils dilating.

An overwhelming wave of pure, unfiltered abandonment violently flooded his nervous system. It wasn't just about tonight; the dark frequency acted like a catalyst, dragging old, buried wounds back to the surface of his consciousness.

Memory fragments flashed before his eyes with terrifying clarity.

He remembered the night Raynare first struck in the park. Issei had been impaled, dying on the grass, only for Rias Gremory to descend in a pillar of glorious crimson light, cradling Issei and resurrecting him as a Devil. Ren had been lying just feet away in the shadows, bleeding, invisible to the High-Class Devil's eyes. Nobody had offered him a miracle. He had been forced to drag his broken, blood-soaked body through the dark mud, crawling back to his apartment alone, stitching his own flesh in the dark.

Another memory surfaced: sitting on the roof with Matsuda and Motohama, eating cheap convenience store lunches. Ren had offhandedly suggested, *"Hey, what if we tried joining the Occult Research Club too?"*

Matsuda and Motohama had burst out laughing, waving their hands dismissively. *"Are you insane, Ren? That's the forbidden garden! Only guys like Issei get to be chosen by beauties like Rias Gremory! Normal trash like us don't belong in their world. We're just background characters."*

Standing in the abandoned church, the dark energy inside Ren’s chest violently expanded, thrashing like a trapped beast.

*It's a good thing I didn't follow them,* Ren realized, his analytical mind fighting to maintain control over the sudden emotional turbulence. *If I had walked into their clubroom with this dark energy raging inside me, it would have erupted into a chaotic mess.*

Driven by the explosive surge of sinister power flooding his muscles, Ren vaulted through the shattered stained-glass window. He didn't run like a human; he moved like a streak of black lightning across the rooftops of Kuoh, his physical limitations temporarily shattered by the raging dark energy.

He burst through his apartment window, landed silently on his floor, and immediately dropped into a cross-legged meditation pose, locking his mind down to regulate the chaotic force before it could destroy his body.

*I will not be consumed,* Ren declared internally, his mental voice ringing like a iron bell against the darkness encroaching on his consciousness. *I will not lose myself. I still want to be me.*

He probed the raging dark energy with his neuro-somatic awareness, analyzing its composition.

*I know what you are,* Ren told the darkness. *You are born from the feeling of abandonment. You are the bitterness of feeling like an anomaly that doesn't belong in this world.*

The dark whisper hissed violently in response, attempting to drown his mind in a wave of destructive rage against the world that had cast him aside.

*Listen to me!* Ren commanded, his willpower clamping down on the dark entity like a vice. *If I can't get the harem... if I'm not the destined hero of this story, fine! Then I want to be powerful! I want to enjoy the process of becoming stronger! I want to enjoy learning the hidden truths of this universe!*

Ren leaned into the dark energy, projecting his cold, unyielding resolve directly into its core.

*Lashing out at the world in a meaningless, temporary outburst of rage is pathetic,* Ren argued sharply. *You explode for five minutes, destroy a few buildings, and then fade away into nothingness. What's the point of that? Instead of wasting yourself on a childish tantrum... help me become stronger. Channel your force into my body. Help me cultivate. So that one day, if the time ever comes... I will have the power to fight the entire world.*

The raging dark energy paused its violent thrashing. The suffocating malice slowly shifted, resonating with Ren’s cold, absolute ambition. The dark entity accepted his terms.

The chaotic rage smoothed out, transforming into a dense, potent fuel stream. Ren immediately guided this dark power through his internal pathways, using it as a battering ram against his stagnant meridians.

*POP! POP!*

Two blocked meridian channels in his torso burst open under the immense pressure, allowing his internal energy to flow with three times its previous velocity. His physical body settled into a deep, harmonious state of cultivation.

With the dark storm contained and integrated, Ren’s mind returned to absolute, crystalline clarity.

He re-examined his memories of Issei and the Occult Research Club—this time without emotional bias.

*Issei didn't abandon me maliciously,* Ren realized calmly. *The night after Raynare attacked us, Issei came straight to my apartment. He didn't go to his parents or his other friends—he came to me, because I was the only person he trusted enough to share the terrifying truth with.*

*And when Dohnaseek ambushed us in the alley, Rias and her peerage didn't ignore me. They healed my injuries without hesitation because it was the natural, right thing to do.*

*They allowed me into their clubroom. They let me sit on their couch. Rias bantered with me about chess theory, even when she was half-dressed. They spoke to me normally, like an equal.*

*(End of Ren's Flashback)*

Ren slowly opened his eyes in the quiet, sunlit bedroom.

He took a deep, refreshing neuro-somatic breath, feeling the dense, newly cultivated energy humming steadily within his newly opened meridians. His mind was calm, sharp, and utterly devoid of resentment.

He stretched his limbs, feeling significantly stronger, faster, and more centered than he had been twenty-four hours ago.

*I'll head to school early today,* Ren thought, standing up from his bed with a faint, grounded smile. *Issei is probably stressing himself out thinking I'm mad at him.*

Ren walked over to his closet to grab his Kuoh Academy uniform, completely at peace with his place in the world.

He had no idea that the dark entity he had just bargained with and integrated into his meridian system was not a mere manifestation of his own subconscious emotions—it was the seed of darkness from the harrowing interstice, quietly sprouting deep within his soul.

#### Sub Section 3: The Strongest Pawn and the Twelve Gates

Issei sat across from Rias’s mahogany desk in the Occult Research Club, staring at the empty, velvet-lined wooden box she had just opened. The afternoon sun caught the dust motes drifting between them, underscoring the weight of the revelation she had just dropped.

"Eight pieces?" Issei repeated, his eyes wide. "Wait, President, I thought a Pawn was just one piece. I used all of them?"

"You did," Rias smiled, leaning back in her leather chair. "The Evil Piece system assigns a value based on the latent potential and raw power of the soul it resurrects. Because you host the Sacred Gear of a Heavenly Dragon, a single Pawn piece was not enough to anchor your soul to the living world. It took all eight of my Pawns to complete your reincarnation."

Issei looked down at his left hand, slowly clenching his fingers into a tight fist. He wasn't just a basic foot soldier; he was the entire frontline defense of the Gremory peerage condensed into a single entity. The sheer weight of Rias’s investment in him ignited a fiery, unyielding resolve in his chest.

"I won't let you down, President," Issei declared, his voice ringing with absolute certainty. "If I took all eight pieces, then I'll just have to do the work of eight men. I'm going to hold the title of the Strongest Pawn. No one will ever touch you or the others while I'm standing."

Near the clubroom sofa, a bright, melodic laugh broke the serious atmosphere. Asia Argento was sitting with Koneko, her eyes shining with genuine, profound happiness. The fear and loneliness that had haunted her in the church were entirely gone, replaced by the warmth of a family that had quite literally fought a war to bring her home.

"I am so happy to be here," Asia beamed, clasping her hands together naturally. "Thank you all so much. I must thank the Lord for guiding me to such wonderful—"

She closed her eyes to pray, and instantly let out a sharp, pained yelp, clutching her temples as if a spike had just been driven through her skull.

"Ouch! My head!" Asia whimpered, tears pricking the corners of her eyes.

Akeno walked over, giggling softly behind her hand as she poured Asia another cup of tea. "You have to be careful now, Asia. You are a Devil. The system of Heaven and the light of God are fundamentally opposed to our existence. Simply praying to Him or reading a holy text will give you a terrible migraine, and holy water will burn your skin."

Asia blinked, rubbing her forehead with a sheepish, slightly overwhelmed smile. "I... I suppose I will have to break a few old habits, then."

While the Occult Research Club settled into their new dynamic, miles away in his darkened apartment, Ren Nocturnus Veritas was breaking the biological limits of a human baseline.

He sat perfectly still on the floor, stripped to the waist, his skin slick with a heavy layer of sweat. The dark energy he had integrated earlier was not a passive resource; it was a violent, highly conductive fuel, and he was currently using it as a battering ram against his own internal architecture.

In classical eastern martial arts, the human body contained Twelve Great Meridians—primary energetic pathways that governed the flow of life force, stamina, and kinetic output. For a normal human, these pathways were narrow, clogged by physical impurities and biological limitations.

Ren isolated the dark energy within his lower abdomen and violently forced it upward.

*Crack.*

It wasn't a physical sound, but a deep, internal release. Ren gritted his teeth as the dark energy surged through the Lung Meridian, searing the pathway open, before cascading into the Large Intestine and Stomach Meridians. The pain was excruciating, like liquid nitrogen and boiling water being pumped through his veins simultaneously, but his neuro-somatic conditioning allowed him to compartmentalize the agony and maintain his breathing rhythm.

He pushed the dark tide further, systematically shattering the blockages in the Heart, Spleen, and Kidney meridians. With every gate that burst open, his physical senses expanded. He could hear the blood pumping through his own capillaries; he could feel the exact gravitational pull anchoring his bones to the floor.

When the twelfth and final meridian cleared, the chaotic dark energy suddenly smoothed into a frictionless, rushing river of absolute power. Ren opened his eyes. The room seemed sharper, the shadows deeper. He clenched his fist, feeling a terrifying, coiled kinetic density in his muscles that vastly eclipsed the physical capabilities of any normal martial artist. The seed of darkness had provided the key, and Ren had just unlocked the door to true superhuman cultivation.

He stood up, walking over to the sink to wash the sweat from his face, entirely unaware of the cosmic origins of the power now flowing through his veins.

The next morning, the sun rose over Kuoh Academy, marking a distinct shift in the air. Ren walked through the school gates, perfectly attuned to his newly opened meridians, and immediately spotted Issei standing near the courtyard entrance. Issei wasn't alone. Standing beside him, nervously adjusting the collar of her brand-new Kuoh Academy uniform, was Asia Argento.

She caught sight of Ren, her face instantly lighting up with a brilliant smile as she waved excitedly. As Ren walked over to greet the newest transfer student of Class 2-B, he realized the chessboard had completely reset. The skirmishes were over, but a much larger, more dangerous game was just beginning.

# Volume 2 

## Life 1 

### Section 1

#### Sub Section 1: The Angel in the Living Room

The plan orchestrated by Rias and the Occult Research Club had moved forward with surprising efficiency. Within twenty-four hours of her resurrection, Asia Argento was standing in the entry hallway of the Hyoudou household, clutching her small travel bag against her chest like a shield.

She bowed so deeply her forehead nearly touched her knees. "I-I am so terribly sorry for the intrusion! My name is Asia Argento. Please forgive me for taking up space in your home!"

Standing on the wooden flooring, Gorou and Miki Hyoudou—Issei’s parents—stared at the blonde girl in absolute, stunned silence.

Issei winced, frantically scratching the back of his neck. "Uh, Mom, Dad... like President Rias explained, Asia doesn't have anywhere else to go right now, so—"

*CLASP!*

Before Issei could finish his sentence, his mother lunged forward, grabbing both of Asia’s hands with a gasp that shook the ceiling. Her eyes were sparkling with sheer, unadulterated ecstasy.

"A real girl..." Miki breathed, her voice trembling with emotion. She turned her head toward her husband, tears swelling in the corners of her eyes. "Dear! Look! A real, polite, sweet young girl is standing in our hallway!"

Gorou pulled out a handkerchief, aggressively dabbing his eyes. "I never thought I'd see the day... I was convinced our bloodline was destined to end in a tragic swamp of perversion..."

Asia blinked, utterly bewildered by the warm reception. "Um... am I really not a burden?"

"A burden? Heavens no!" Miki cheered, practically dragging Asia toward the living room table. But as she pulled out a chair for the girl, Miki’s expression softened into a look of genuine, motherly concern. She leaned down, keeping her voice low. "Asia-chan... are you truly sure you want to stay here? Issei is... well..."

"Hey!" Issei squawked from the hallway.

Miki turned to look at her son with a sigh of profound disappointment. "Asia-chan, I have to be honest with you. Every time I go into Issei's room to do the laundry, I find thick, terrifying stacks of adult magazines hidden under his bed, behind his desk, and tucked inside his closet."

Issei’s entire brain short-circuited. His jaw dropped so far it nearly hit the floor, his face turning as red as a ripe tomato. "YOU KNEW?! YOU'VE KNOWN THIS ENTIRE TIME?!"

"Of course I knew, you idiot! You hide them behind the curtains like a middle schooler!" Miki snapped back, before turning her gentle gaze back to Asia. "So you see, Asia-chan... are you really alright staying under the same roof as a boy like him? He spends half his free time staring at swimsuit catalogs."

Issei held his breath, mortified beyond belief, fully expecting Asia to recoil in disgust.

Instead, Asia tilted her head, her deep emerald eyes shining with absolute, unfiltered purity. A breathtakingly radiant smile lit up her face.

"Issei-san is the kindest, most wonderful person in the entire world," Asia said softly, her voice brimming with absolute sincerity. "When I was all alone and had no one, Issei-san came for me. He risked everything just to save my life. I would be honored to stay here, to assist him, and to learn more about the world by his side."

Silence fell over the living room.

Gorou and Miki stared at Asia for three full seconds. Then, both parents broke into loud, hysterical blubbering, hugging each other tight as tears cascaded down their cheeks.

"AN ANGEL HAS DESCENDED UPON THE HYOUDOU HOUSEHOLD!" Gorou sobbed toward the ceiling.

"ISSEI, IF YOU EVER MAKE THIS GIRL CRY, WE ARE DISOWNING YOU!" Miki cried out, pointing a dramatic finger at her son.

Issei slumped against the wall, dragging a hand down his face in equal parts embarrassment and immense, quiet relief. "I won't... I swear I won't."

The next morning, the bell for homeroom at Kuoh Academy had not yet rung.

Issei walked into Class 2-B, his backpack slung over his shoulder. The lingering guilt from two nights ago still weighed heavily on his mind. He had barely slept, constantly replaying the moment they had walked out of the ruined church and completely forgotten his best friend on the altar.

He looked toward the window seat—and stopped dead in his tracks.

Ren Nocturnus Veritas was sitting quietly at his desk, casually adjusting his uniform tie. His posture was perfectly upright, his breathing calm, and his dark eyes were looking peacefully out at the cherry blossom trees in the courtyard. The dense, turbulent energy he had contained over the weekend had settled smoothly into his twelve cleared meridians, giving him an air of effortless, grounded composure.

"REN!" Issei screamed, abandoning all sense of decorum as he sprinted across the classroom.

Before Ren could even react, Issei threw his arms around Ren’s shoulders, pulling him into a desperate, tearful bear-hug. "BRO! YOU'RE ALIVE! I'M SO SORRY! WE LEFT YOU IN THAT CREEPY CHURCH AND I DIDN'T REALISE UNTIL WE WERE HALFWAY HOME!"

Ren didn't even flinch under the sudden impact. His newly cultivated physical density allowed him to absorb Issei’s frantic momentum without his chair sliding an inch.

Ren gently patted Issei’s back with one hand, his expression entirely deadpan. "Issei. Release me. You're cutting off the airflow to my trachea."

Issei pulled back, clutching Ren by the shoulders, searching his face for any sign of lingering anger. "Are you mad at us? You can hit me if you want! I deserve it!"

Ren let out a quiet, amused breath, resting his chin on his hand. "Issei, I walked home, ate dinner, slept for eight hours, and cleared my internal energy pathways. I am completely fine. There is no big deal. Sit down before the teacher gets here."

Issei wiped a tear from his cheek, grinning like a maniac. "Man... you really are the chillest guy on the planet."

Just as Issei took his seat beside Ren, the front door of the classroom slid open. Their homeroom teacher stepped inside, clapping her hands to get the class's attention.

"Alright, everyone, take your seats," the teacher announced with a warm smile. "Before we begin morning announcements, we have a new transfer student joining Class 2-B today. Please welcome her nicely."

The door slid open further, and Asia Argento stepped into the room, wearing the standard Kuoh Academy girl's uniform. She held her hands tightly together in front of her skirt, looking nervously at the rows of unfamiliar faces.

Then, her eyes scanned toward the back row—and locked straight onto Ren.

Asia’s eyes went wide. "Ren-san!"

Completely forgetting where she was, forgetting the teacher, and forgetting her planned speech, Asia gasped with pure joy and trotted straight down the aisle between the desks. Before anyone could process what was happening, she threw her arms around Ren, burying her face against his chest in a heartfelt hug.

"Ren-san! You're safe! I was so worried when I didn't see you at the clubroom yesterday!" Asia beamed, hugging him tightly.

The entire classroom went dead silent. Thirty pairs of eyes locked onto the scene in absolute shock.

Ren blinks, his dark eyes looking down at the blonde girl clinging to his blazer. He raised a hand and gently patted her head. "Asia. I am completely fine. But... I believe you have an introduction to give to the class."

Asia froze. She slowly turned her head, realizing that the teacher, Issei, and every single student in Class 2-B were staring at her.

A bright, fiery blush exploded across Asia's cheeks. "A-Ah! I-I am so terribly sorry!" She squeaked, bowing frantically to Ren before scrambling back to the front podium, her face burning like a lightbulb.

"Ahem," the teacher cleared her throat, chuckling softly. "Go ahead, dear."

Asia took a deep, shaky breath, placing her hands on her chest to steady her beating heart. "H-Hello, everyone. My name is Asia Argento. I recently moved here from Europe... I am still learning many things about Japan, but I hope we can all be wonderful friends!"

She offered a sweet, breathtakingly innocent smile to the room.

Instantly, the atmosphere in Class 2-B shifted.

"She's so cute!" several girls whispered, clutching their cheeks. "Look at how gentle her aura is!"

"An angel..." the boys murmured in collective reverent awe. "An actual angel just transferred into our class!"

Hands immediately shot up across the room.

"Argento-san! What are your hobbies?"

"I love taking walks and looking at flowers!" Asia answered with a bright, friendly smile.

"Argento-san! What kind of food do you like?"

"Issei-san's mother made delicious miso soup for me this morning!" Asia replied warmly.

The boys practically swooned. Her complete lack of pretense and sweet, approachable demeanor were miles apart from the distant, untouchable beauty of Rias Gremory or Akeno Himejima. To the male population of Class 2-B, Asia was a pure, heavenly gift.

Then, a student in the front row asked the fatal question.

"Argento-san, where are you living right now? Did your family move here with you?"

Asia smiled brightly, answering without a single drop of hesitation. "Oh! I don't have any family here, so I am staying at Issei-san's house!"

The air in the classroom instantly plummeted by twenty degrees.

Asia, completely unaware of the nuclear bomb she had just dropped, continued with pure, innocent enthusiasm. "Issei-san was kind enough to open his home to me! I get to witness so many of his daily habits, and I am just so happy to be around him and assist him every day!"

*SNAP.*

The sound of several mechanical pencils snapping simultaneously echoed through the dead-silent classroom.

Issei froze in his seat, his skin turning deathly pale as he felt a wave of absolute, murderous intent rolling toward him from every single male student in the room. Lenses gleamed in the light; knuckles cracked like lightning.

"Issei..." a boy in the front row growled, his eyes burning with demonic jealousy. "What... did she just say?"

"He's living with an angel..." another whispered, his voice dripping with dark rage. "HE'S LIVING WITH AN ACTUAL HOLY MAIDEN!"

Issei slowly raised his hands, sweating bullets. "W-Wait! Guys! It's not what you think! It's a complicated situation—"

"DEATH TO HYOUDOU ISSEI!"

By the time the lunch bell rang, Issei had barely survived four periods of intense, psychological warfare. The boys of Class 2-B had subjected him to endless glares, paper-airplane assaults, and ominous threats of exile. The only safe zone in the room was the small radius around Ren’s desk, where Ren quietly ate his bento, completely ignored by the angry mob due to his stoic, neutral presence.

Eventually, the initial wave of rage passed, leaving the classroom in a tense stalemate.

Over by the back window, Matsuda and Motohama sat slumped over their desks, staring blankly at the ceiling in soul-crushing despair.

"It's over, Motohama..." Matsuda whimpered, clutching his heart. "The world is fundamentally broken. Issei has betrayed the pervert alliance. First he hangs out with the Occult Research Club, and now a cute blonde transfer student is living in his house..."

"He's living the harem dream while we rot in the barren wasteland of singlehood..." Motohama groaned, pushing his glasses up his nose with a trembling, tearful hand. "All the girls are flocking to him... We're just trash..."

"Um... excuse me?"

A soft, melodic voice broke through their depression. Matsuda and Motohama slowly lifted their heads.

Asia was standing beside their desks, holding her neatly packed lunchbox. She looked at them with wide, curious eyes. "Are you Matsuda-san and Motohama-san?"

Matsuda blinked, pointing a thumb at his own chest. "Uh... yeah? That's us..."

Asia’s face instantly lit up with a brilliant, joyful smile. "I knew it! Issei-san told me so much about you two!"

Matsuda and Motohama froze. "He... he did?"

"Yes!" Asia clasped her hands together, nodding enthusiastically. "Issei-san said you two are his absolute best friends in the entire school! He told me how much he loves hanging out with you, and how you always stick together through thick and thin! He said that even though you can be a little wild sometimes, you're truly wonderful, reliable people!"

Asia took a small step closer, bowing slightly to them. "Issei-san cares about you both so much. He said he couldn't imagine high school without you. So... if it's alright with you, I would love to be your friend too!"

Matsuda and Motohama stared at Asia in absolute, breathless shock.

The image of Issei they had built in their minds—a traitorous, arrogant harem king who had abandoned them—instantly shattered into a million pieces, replaced by a heroic, glowing vision of their true brother.

*He... he talked about us to the cute girl?* Matsuda thought, tears welling up in his eyes.

*He didn't abandon us... He elevated us!* Motohama realized, his jaw trembling.

In unison, both perverts burst into loud, ugly, fountain-like tears of pure, unadulterated ecstasy, throwing their arms around each other's shoulders as they howled toward the ceiling.

"ISSEIIIIII!" Matsuda roared through his tears. "YOU BEAUTIFUL BASTARD! WE WERE SO WRONG ABOUT YOU!"

"YOU NEVER FORGOT THE BROTHERHOOD!" Motohama sobbed hysterically. "YOU'RE THE GREATEST FRIEND A MAN COULD EVER ASK FOR!"

From his desk a few feet away, Issei—who was currently nursing a bump on his head from an earlier eraser thrown by a jealous classmate—looked over at Matsuda and Motohama crying like babies in front of a smiling, clueless Asia.

Issei let out a long, exhausted sigh, a warm smile creeping onto his face as he looked at Ren.

Ren took a quiet bite of his rice, returning Issei’s look with a subtle, approving nod.

"See?" Ren murmured calmly, setting his chopsticks down. "A good move always opens up the rest of the board."

#### Sub Section 2: The Tactical Reconnaissance

The lunch hour was drawing to a close, and a comfortable, warm breeze drifted through the open windows of Class 2-B. Asia had happily trotted off to help the homeroom teacher sort some paperwork, leaving Issei alone at his desk.

Matsuda and Motohama slowly walked over, their earlier joyous tears drying into a slightly melancholic expression.

"Hey, Issei," Matsuda said, scratching his cheek awkwardly. "We really appreciate what you said to Argento-san... but, man, it feels like we never hang out anymore. You're always with the Occult Research Club after school."

Motohama adjusted his glasses, looking at the floor. "Yeah. It feels like the old trio is dead."

Issei looked at his two best friends, a heavy wave of guilt washing over him. It was true. Between the training, the demon contracts, and the life-or-death battles with Fallen Angels, he had completely neglected the normal, mundane high school life he used to share with them.

"You guys aren't dead to me," Issei said firmly, standing up from his desk. "I'm sorry I've been so distant. Tell you what—after school today, I'm all yours. We'll do whatever you want."

Matsuda’s eyes lit up instantly. "Really?! Then we're doing the classics! A high-risk, high-reward tactical infiltration of the physical education building! We're going peeping, brother!"

Issei’s smile froze.

In truth, the idea of crawling through dusty air ducts to catch a blurry, long-distance glimpse of a sports bra had entirely lost its appeal. Over the past week, he had accidentally walked in on Rias changing, seen Akeno in various states of undress, and shared a roof with Asia. His baseline for visual stimulation had been permanently shattered by High-Class Devils and Angels.

"Uh... sure," Issei lied through his teeth, trying to muster some enthusiasm for the sake of his friends. "Yeah, sounds great."

"We need a fourth," Motohama declared, turning his head toward the back row. "Ren! You're coming too, right?"

Ren was quietly reading a thick, leather-bound book on Eastern meridian theory. He didn't look up from the page. "I have absolutely no interest in watching teenagers change their gym clothes."

Matsuda and Motohama instantly deflated, their shoulders slumping in synchronized depression. "Come on, Ren... you used to be right there in the trenches with us..."

Ren let out a quiet sigh, gently closing his book. He looked at the three boys, his dark eyes analyzing their pathetic, pleading expressions.

"I am not interested in the objective," Ren stated calmly, standing up from his chair. "However, I acknowledge that the structural integrity of this friend group requires maintenance. Therefore, I will act as your vanguard. I will map the patrol routes, bypass the security locks, and deliver you to a secure observation point. Once the infiltration is complete, I am leaving."

Matsuda and Motohama gasped, their eyes shining with sheer awe. "A tactical extraction... Ren, you're a god!"

Three hours later, the afternoon sun cast long shadows across the physical education building.

The infiltration was flawless. It was almost frighteningly clinical.

Ren moved through the shadowy corridors like a ghost, his twelve cleared meridians allowing him to regulate his internal body temperature and heartbeat, rendering him virtually invisible to standard human awareness. He led the trio through a blind spot in the faculty patrol route, bypassed a locked utility door using a basic hairpin tension wrench in under four seconds, and guided them up a narrow maintenance shaft overlooking the first-year girls' locker room.

"Clear," Ren whispered, his voice barely a breath as he popped the ventilation grate open half an inch.

Matsuda and Motohama scrambled forward, pressing their faces against the narrow gap. Below them, a dozen first-year girls were chatting happily as they changed out of their gym uniforms.

"Jackpot..." Matsuda whispered, tears of joy streaming down his face.

"The tactical layout is perfect," Motohama breathed heavily, his glasses fogging up.

Issei, who had genuinely believed he was over this kind of mischief, found himself inching forward. As he caught sight of the lively, energetic first-years below, his dormant perverted instincts suddenly flared back to life.

*Wow... I guess I really am just a degenerate at heart!* Issei thought, his face flushing red as he eagerly joined his friends at the grate.

Ren stood in the shadows behind them, completely unmoved. He had completed his end of the social contract. Without making a single sound, Ren turned around, slipped back down the maintenance shaft, and vanished into the afternoon air.

Down below, the locker room door swung open. A petite, silver-haired girl with a stoic expression walked in, carrying her folded uniform.

It was Koneko Toujou.

Issei’s blood ran cold. *Wait... Koneko is a first-year! This is her class!*

Before Issei could grab Matsuda and Motohama to abort the mission, Koneko paused near her locker. She didn't look up. She didn't shout. She simply turned her head slightly, her golden eyes locking onto the exact coordinate of the ventilation grate hidden in the ceiling shadows. She had smelled them the moment they crawled into the shaft.

Issei swallowed hard. *We are so dead.*

Twenty minutes later, the Occult Research Club doors slammed open.

A heavily bruised, battered, and groaning Issei was unceremoniously dragged into the room by the collar of his shirt and dumped onto the carpet. Koneko stood over him, casually dusting off her hands with an expression of profound, absolute disgust.

"Trash," Koneko muttered, walking over to the sofa and sitting down.

Rias, Akeno, and Asia looked up from the tea table, blinking in surprise.

"Issei-san!" Asia gasped, rushing over to kneel beside him. "What happened? Were you attacked by Fallen Angels again?!"

"No," Koneko deadpanned, taking a sip of her tea. "He and his idiot friends were crawling through the ceiling of the first-year locker room like rats."

Rias paused, her teacup halfway to her lips, while Akeno let out a long, amused, "Oh my..."

Asia’s face fell into a look of deep, genuine confusion. She gently patted Issei’s bruised cheek. "Issei-san... if you really wanted to see a girl undress... why didn't you just ask? I would have undressed for you anytime you wanted."

Issei choked on his own spit, his face instantly turning a violent shade of red. "A-Asia! You can't just say things like that! And that wasn't what this was about! I swear!"

Rias sighed, resting her chin on her hand, a faint, teasing smile playing on her lips. "Really, Issei. You have already seen me without my clothes multiple times. If you are that desperate for visual stimulation, as your master, I suppose I could accommodate you. There is no need to resort to petty voyeurism."

"President, please! You're making it worse!" Issei pleaded from the floor, waving his hands frantically. "I wasn't actually trying to peep! Matsuda and Motohama were feeling depressed because we haven't been hanging out. I just agreed to go along with them to cheer them up! It was a bonding exercise!"

Koneko set her teacup down, narrowing her golden eyes slightly. "When I entered the room, I sensed four distinct heat signatures in the ventilation shaft. But when I broke through the ceiling, there were only three of you. Where was the fourth?"

Issei groaned, sitting up and rubbing the lump on his head. "That was Ren. But he wasn't peeping. He literally just picked the locks, guided us through the blind spots, opened the grate, and immediately walked away."

The clubroom fell silent.

"Ren?" Rias asked, her brow furrowing in genuine surprise. "He assisted a voyeuristic infiltration, but refused to actually partake in the objective?"

"Yeah," Issei sighed, looking down at the floor. "He told us he has zero interest in that stuff anymore. He only came along to act as our tactical guide because he felt bad for Matsuda and Motohama."

Akeno tapped her cheek, a thoughtful look crossing her face. "You know, President... I have been hearing rumors among the student body recently. Before the incident in the park with Raynare, Ren was heavily associated with Issei’s... particular hobbies."

"I remember," Rias nodded slowly. "They were the 'Perverted Quartet' of the second year."

"But the rumors say he changed drastically after that week," Akeno continued, her tone shifting into a more analytical register. "Students have reported seeing him walking through the school hallways entirely blindfolded, navigating purely by sound and air pressure. When asked why, he reportedly stated that closing his eyes was the most efficient way to purge visual distractions and perverted thoughts."

Koneko looked toward the window, her stoic expression softening into a look of quiet, begrudging respect. "He successfully masked his scent and thermal output well enough that I didn't sense his departure from the shaft."

Rias leaned back in her chair, a complex, assessing gleam in her crimson eyes. The boy they had left behind in the ruined church wasn't just a baseline human anomaly. He was evolving, shedding his adolescent flaws and forging himself into a weapon of pure, frictionless discipline.

"It seems," Rias murmured quietly, "the rumors are entirely true."

#### Sub Section 3: Rias Sona Rivalry

The rivalry between Rias Gremory and Sona Sitri, the Student Council President, was an open secret that practically dictated the social hierarchy of Kuoh Academy. Whenever the two heiresses clashed, the entire school ground to a halt to watch.

The spark for this particular afternoon’s conflict was ignited during the lunch hour, when Sona adjusted her glasses in the Occult Research Club room and formally introduced her newest pawn: a loud, aggressively eager blonde boy named Genshirou Saji. With both High-Class Devils having recently acquired new, highly unusual human pieces, a proxy war was inevitable.

It began with tennis. The school's tennis courts were completely swarmed by the male student body, who packed against the chain-link fences like sardines. Rias and Sona, dressed in incredibly form-fitting athletic wear, rallied the ball back and forth with terrifying, superhuman velocity. Every explosive volley sent shockwaves across the clay, while the boys in the stands howled in perverted ecstasy as the girls' skirts fluttered and their assets bounced with every sprint.

The athletic warfare escalated through a series of sports, ending in a massive, chaotic game of dodgeball in the main gymnasium. To settle the score, Sona and Rias conscripted members from the Student Council, the Occult Research Club, and a large handful of regular students from their respective classes to pad the teams.

The game quickly devolved into a supernatural battlefield thinly veiled as a gym class activity. Issei and Saji threw balls at each other with enough kinetic force to dent the wooden floorboards, while Kiba and the Student Council members unleashed a barrage of rapid-fire throws. The crowd roared, fully expecting the match to boil down to a final, dramatic showdown between Rias and Sona.

Instead, the gymnasium witnessed an absolute anomaly.

Ren Nocturnus Veritas had been drafted onto Rias’s side simply to fill the roster. He had stood near the backline for the entire game, completely silent, with his eyes half-closed and his hands casually stuffed into the pockets of his gym shorts.

When a stray ball, thrown at breakneck speed by the Student Council's rook, careened directly toward his face, Ren didn't even flinch. He simply tilted his head two inches to the left. The ball grazed his ear, blowing his hair back, and slammed into the wall behind him.

Sona narrowed her eyes, instantly signaling her entire frontline to target the quiet boy. Five dodgeballs were hurled at Ren simultaneously from different angles.

Ren let out a slow, neuro-somatic exhale. His twelve newly opened meridians hummed to life, projecting a spherical field of absolute spatial awareness around his body. He stepped forward. He pivoted on his heel, letting one ball whiz past his chest. He bent backward at a gravity-defying angle, allowing two more to sail over his nose. He swayed like a willow branch in a hurricane, completely untouchable.

It was a display of pure, predictive evasion that defied human biology, mirroring the profound haki training he had studied in his private time. He wasn't tracking the balls with his eyes; he was reading the displaced air pressure and the micro-movements of his opponents' shoulders before they even threw.

By the time the ten-minute timer rang out, the rest of the court was utterly exhausted, panting and bruised. Ren was the only player remaining untouched, casually holding a dodgeball in one hand, completely devoid of a single drop of sweat. He had won the match by sheer, impossible attrition.

The gymnasium fell into a stunned, deafening silence, shattered only when Rias began to clap, her crimson eyes gleaming with a mixture of awe and intense curiosity.

Two hours later, the sun was beginning to set, casting long, golden shadows across the empty back courtyard of the old school building.

Ren moved across the rooftops with absolute, frictionless grace. He was practicing urban parkour, utilizing the dense energy of his internal meridians to achieve explosive vertical leaps and silent landings. He vaulted over the edge of the physical education building, dropping twenty feet and rolling seamlessly onto the grass to disperse the kinetic impact.

As he stood up, he noticed a small gathering near the edge of the woods.

Issei was on the ground, his arms shaking violently as he attempted to push himself up from the dirt. He was drenched in sweat, his face purple with exertion. Rias stood over him with a stern, authoritative expression, while Asia hovered nearby, holding a towel and looking incredibly worried.

"Ninety-eight... ninety... nine..." Issei gasped, his elbows buckling.

"Push through it, Issei," Rias commanded firmly. "The Boosted Gear multiplies your base power, but if your physical vessel is weak, the recoil will shatter your bones. You need to do another set of fifty."

Issei collapsed flat onto his stomach, groaning into the dirt. "President... even if I wanted to... my arms are literally made of jelly right now. I can't lift an inch."

"If you repeatedly overload the anterior chain without allowing for muscular hypertrophy and cellular repair, you will only achieve muscle catabolism, not growth," a calm voice interrupted.

Rias, Issei, and Asia turned to see Ren walking out from the shadows of the trees, his breathing perfectly regulated despite his intense parkour routine.

"Ren-san!" Asia beamed, her worry instantly fading into a bright smile.

Ren nodded to her, then crouched down beside Issei. He pulled a small, leather-bound notebook from his pocket and flipped it open, revealing a highly detailed, hand-drawn schematic of the human muscular anatomy.

"Push-ups heavily tax the pectoralis major, the anterior deltoids, and the triceps brachii," Ren explained smoothly, tracing the muscle groups with his pen. "By forcing your body past total muscular failure on a single exercise, you are risking acute tendon inflammation. If you want a stronger vessel for your Sacred Gear, Issei, you need to engage the posterior chain—your latissimus dorsi, your rhomboids, and your lower body kinematics. Squats, pull-ups, and core stabilization will serve you far better right now than doing another fifty useless push-ups."

Issei pushed himself up onto his knees, staring at the detailed anatomy diagrams in Ren’s notebook with wide, bewildered eyes.

"Dude..." Issei panted, wiping sweat from his forehead. "Since when are you a fitness guru? I thought you were just a degenerate otaku like me, Matsuda, and Motohama!"

Ren closed the notebook with a soft snap, standing up. "I am an otaku, Issei. But my palette is significantly broader than yours. While you three were obsessing exclusively over harem tropes, ecchi, and forbidden romance, I was consuming everything. Shonen, seinen, dark fantasy, military history, tactical espionage, wuxia, and murim martial arts."

Ren looked down at his own hands, casually clenching his fists to feel the dense kinetic energy coiled in his forearms. "When I wasn't hanging out with you guys, I wasn't just sitting in my room reading manga. I was attempting to replicate the internal energy cultivation and martial principles I read about. This is what I’ve always done in my private time."

Issei blinked, a look of profound realization crossing his face. "So... you actually train?"

"I do," Ren admitted calmly. He looked at Issei, then at Rias, and finally at Asia. "I won't lie. I was absolutely invested in the perverted and harem genres alongside you three. But then... reality happened."

Ren’s dark eyes softened slightly, though his posture remained utterly grounded. "I watched you get resurrected by a beautiful, high-class demon. I watched you gain an incredible power. I see you now, hanging out with Rias, Akeno, Koneko, and living under the same roof as Asia. I'll admit it—at first, it made me incredibly depressed. I felt exactly the same despair that Matsuda and Motohama are feeling right now."

Issei winced, a heavy wave of guilt washing over him.

"But there is a distinct difference between me and the other two," Ren continued, his tone shifting from reflective to entirely analytical. "Matsuda and Motohama are still clinging to the fantasy. I just... lost interest. The appeal completely evaporated. If I read a harem or ecchi manga now, it's strictly to analyze the comedic timing. The underlying desire is gone."

Rias’s expression softened into one of genuine, aristocratic empathy. She took a step forward, resting a hand over her heart. "I am truly sorry to hear that, Ren. It was never my intention to make you feel inferior, or to cause you emotional distress by bringing Issei into my world."

Ren let out a light, breathy chuckle, his face breaking into a casual, highly approachable smile. He waved his hand dismissively.

"Don't apologize, President. It's perfectly fine," Ren said warmly, bearing no trace of malice or resentment. "I don't know about Issei, Motohama, or Matsuda, but I always had two very distinct goals for my life. One was the standard, degenerate teenage harem dream. The other was to push the absolute limits of human potential, become overwhelmingly powerful, and explore the deepest, most dangerous secrets this world has to offer."

Ren looked up at the twilight sky, his newly opened meridians humming in quiet resonance with the fading light.

"The universe looked at my situation and gave a very definitive, undeniable 'no' to the first goal," Ren smiled, shrugging his shoulders. "So, it just made my choices easier. I'm pivoting. I am going to pursue the second goal with everything I have."

He reached down, giving Issei a friendly, encouraging pat on the shoulder. "Don't skip leg day, Issei. Your dragon is heavy; you're going to need strong knees to carry it."

With a final, polite nod to Rias and Asia, Ren turned around and walked away across the courtyard. He vanished into the evening shadows, leaving the Red-Haired Ruin Princess, the Holy Maiden, and the Red Dragon Emperor staring in absolute silence at the retreating back of a boy who had just declared war on the limits of humanity.

#### Sub Section 4: The Familiar Hunt and the Crucible of Shadows

While the grand machinery of supernatural diplomacy churned forward, the Occult Research Club and the Student Council embarked on a joint expedition into the mythical Familiar Forest. Because Ren was an unaligned human possessing no devil contract or peerage piece, he was entirely excluded from the outing—a fact that suited his training schedule perfectly.

The expedition itself was an absolute comedy of errors.

Issei had spent three days hyping himself up, fully convinced he was going to tame a legendary, ultra-voluptuous female dragon or a drop-dead gorgeous cat-girl familiar. Instead, the team was greeted by the eccentric Familiar Master, Zatouji, who wore a ridiculous safari hat and spoke like an over-enthusiastic game show host.

The forest quickly spiraled into chaotic absurdity. Saji was hunted down and traumatized by a swarm of hyper-aggressive Sprite Sprites, screaming for his life while running in circles. Issei’s heroic dreams were utterly vaporized when he encountered a legendary Sprite Slime—a creature that possessed the terrifying, acidic ability to dissolve clothes while leaving the wearer completely unharmed. Issei wept tears of perverted joy as the slime targeted the girls, only to be promptly beaten into a bloody pulp by a combined strike from Koneko and Rias. In the end, while Saji bound himself to a bizarre Sprite Sprite, Issei walked away from the mystical realm with nothing more than a tiny, green, glass-like slime that he named "Ryuuteimaru," his grand dreams of a familiar harem thoroughly crushed.

Meanwhile, miles away from the chaos of the Familiar Forest, Ren was turning his body and soul into a crucible.

In the secluded, overgrown courtyard behind his apartment complex, Ren began his session by stripping his physical conditioning back down to raw geometry. Having shattered the limits of his Twelve Great Meridians, his body possessed an unprecedented density of kinetic potential. But power without elasticity was brittle.

He initiated deep, hyper-extensive stretching routines, forcing his tendons and fascia to adapt to his newly expanded energy threshold. He moved into complex acrobatic flows—cat-leaps, aerial flips, handstand wall-runs, and instant directional shifts. His joints popped in rhythmic succession, expanding his range of motion until his body could contort and spring without a single millisecond of mechanical delay.

Next came the tactical espionage protocols.

The church raid and his recent infiltration of the school gym had revealed a glaring gap in his movement repertoire: standard martial arts footwork was designed for ring fighting or open battle, not low-profile tactical infiltration.

Inspired by the tactical espionage mechanics of *Metal Gear Solid*, Ren spent hours practicing low-profile soldier mechanics. He executed high-speed prone crawls across the damp grass, dragging his torso with minimal vertical displacement. He practiced rapid sideways rolls, transitioning smoothly from a roll directly into an ankle-high low crawl, keeping his head down below shoulder height while maintaining an astonishing forward velocity. He mastered the crouch-dash—a low-center-of-gravity glide that allowed him to move at near-sprint speeds while keeping his body folded under standard window-height cover.

He then synthesized these military drills with classical ninja principles. He focused on weightless kinetic transfer—stepping from heel to lateral toe to completely silence his footfalls on dry leaves and gravel. He learned to collapse his presence, actively dampening his thermal output, heart rate, and air-pressure footprint until he could pass within inches of a prowling stray cat without setting off its instincts.

With his stealth and physical frame calibrated, Ren integrated his foundational martial arts: striking, grappling, dodging, evading, and blocking.

*THUMP.*

Ren drove a simple straight punch toward the air. But this time, he didn't just snap his hip. He channeled a surge of black internal energy straight from his lower dantian, surging it through his newly cleared Arm-Taiyin Lung Meridian and driving it out through his knuckles.

The displacement of air was instantaneous. A localized shockwave burst forward, flattening the tall grass fifteen feet ahead of him with a deafening crack.

Ren stared at his fist. The integration of his cleared meridians with the dark internal energy transformed basic physical mechanics into devastating spatial strikes. A standard palm strike now carried enough kinetic density to shatter a Fallen Angel's light spear at the point of origin; a simple rotational joint-lock could snap supernatural bone structure effortlessly. He no longer needed blessed steel or holy water to survive a confrontation. His bare hands were now fully capable of executing High-Class entities.

Yet, the more he pushed his physical training, the more prominent the dark energy became. It pulsed deep within his chest, heavy, dense, and unnaturally cold.

As the sun set, Ren sat cross-legged in his apartment, dropping into a deep state of neuro-somatic meditation.

Previously, he used neuro-somatic breathing merely to regulate his oxygen intake and smooth out lactic acid in his muscles. Now, he applied the protocol inward, using it to smooth the turbulent flow of the dark internal energy coursing through his twelve meridians.

He slowed his breath. *Four seconds in. Four seconds hold. Four seconds out.*

As he guided the dark stream through his internal pathways, smoothing out the jagged ripples of the interstice energy, strange, fragmented visions began to bleed into his conscious mind.

It did not feel like a memory. It felt like a surging, multi-layered projection delivered entirely in the second person.

*You are standing in the ash of a burning horizon,* a cold, collective whisper echoed in his mind, radiating a suffocating wave of ancient, abyssal sorrow. *You watched your sky fall. You were left behind in the dark while the light exalted others. You are forgotten. You are the echo of a world that failed to survive.*

A storm of random, agonizing negative emotions surged through Ren's chest—grief from a dead civilization, rage from a betrayal executed thousands of years ago, the crushing despair of infinite souls vanishing into the void across the multiverse.
