It was a psychic tide meant to drive a human mind into absolute, screaming madness.

Ren did not scream. He did not flinch.

He sat perfectly still in the darkness of his room, his heart rate remaining at a steady, rhythmic forty beats per minute. His neuro-somatic focus acted as an unbreakable, crystalline mirror. He didn't absorb the negative emotions, nor did he fight them. He simply observed them from a position of absolute, fascinated detachment.

*Fascinating,* Ren thought, watching the cosmic fragments of despair drift through his dark energy channels like luminescent dust. *So this energy isn't just raw kinetic fuel. It carries the emotional residue of countless fallen existences.*

He didn't fear the dark whispers. To an otaku who had spent a lifetime studying cosmic horrors, dark fantasy realms, and multiversal lore, the horrifying echoes of the interstice were not a curse—they were an incredible, endless library of raw, unfiltered data.

Ren smoothly wrapped his neuro-somatic breath around the surging negative emotions, compressing them, smoothing them, and weaving them seamlessly back into the steady, frictionless river of his twelve meridians.

He opened his eyes. A faint, imperceptible dark sheen flickered across his pupils before dissolving into total calm. The dark energy was fully smoothed, his meridians were humming with pristine efficiency, and his mind remained absolute master of its domain.

## Life 2: I Pick a Fight
### Section 1: Calm Before Storm

The afternoon sun cast long, lazy shadows across the Occult Research Club room. Rias Gremory sat at her mahogany desk, reviewing a stack of summon request logs. Akeno Himejima stood beside her, pouring a fresh cup of Darjeeling tea with her usual elegant, enigmatic smile.

"How is our newest Devil progressing with his clients?" Rias asked, not looking up from the paperwork.

"It is quite fascinating, President," Akeno giggled softly, resting a hand against her cheek. "Issei is still completely inept at manipulating demonic magic, so he hasn't successfully formalized a single contract yet. However... his clients absolutely adore him."

Rias paused, looking up in genuine surprise. "Really?"

"Yes. It seems his complete lack of aristocratic Devil pride is his greatest asset," Akeno explained. "Whether it is listening to an elderly man complain about his aching joints, or helping a lonely housewife carry her groceries, Issei treats them with a clumsy, genuine warmth. He isn't making magical pacts; he is making friends. The clients are having a genuinely enjoyable time with him."

Rias smiled warmly, setting her pen down. "A Pawn that leads with his heart. That is exactly why I chose him."

While Issei was unknowingly charming the human populace through sheer earnestness, Ren was systematically turning himself into a weapon.

In the quiet isolation of his apartment, Ren stood perfectly still in the center of the room. Having cleared the Twelve Great Meridians, his internal architecture was primed. It was time to transition from energy circulation to practical, combat-ready application.

He took a slow neuro-somatic breath and forced the dense, dark energy residing in his lower abdomen to flood his muscular tissue.

*Application One: Somatic Overclocking.*

Ren vanished from the center of the room. A split second later, the air cracked as he reappeared near the window, his fist stopped exactly one millimeter from the glass. By channeling the dark energy directly into his fast-twitch muscle fibers, his base speed and kinetic strength shattered baseline human limits, elevating his raw stats into the realm of supernatural entities.

*Application Two: Dermal Condensation.*

Ren shifted his focus, drawing the energy outward. Instead of letting it flow through his muscles, he pushed the dense dark matter to the very surface of his skin, compressing it into a microscopic, frictionless barrier. He picked up a stainless-steel utility knife from his desk and drove the blade directly into his forearm.

*Clink.*

The blade didn't even scratch his skin. It felt as though it had struck solid tungsten. The dark energy acted as an impenetrable kinetic armor, fully capable of stopping high-velocity sharp objects without relying on magical shields.

Satisfied with his physical applications, Ren sat down cross-legged on the floor. It was time to delve deeper.

In classical cultivation, the Twelve Great Meridians were rivers that flowed through the body. But the Eight Extraordinary Meridians were the reservoirs—deep, hidden lakes that stored immense volumes of latent power.

Ren closed his eyes, gripping the turbulent, icy power of the interstice, and violently forced it down into the dormant pathways of the Governing Vessel and the Conception Vessel.

The pressure inside his skull was immense. The dark energy fought back, resisting the confinement of the extraordinary channels, but Ren’s neuro-somatic willpower acted like an iron vise. He pushed relentlessly until, one by one, all Eight Extraordinary Meridians burst open, flooding his core with an ocean of condensed, abyssal power.

Suddenly, the absolute silence of his apartment shattered.

Previously, Ren could only feel the raw, second-person negative emotions of the dark energy. Now, with the extraordinary reservoirs open, his perception evolved. He could *hear* them.

A cacophony of thousands of overlapping, agonizing wails echoed directly into his auditory cortex. It was the screaming chorus of forgotten entities, the wailing of dead worlds lost in the harrowing interstice. There were no visual hallucinations yet, but the sheer volume of the multiversal despair was enough to rupture a normal human's sanity.

Ren simply listened. He sat quietly in the dark, his expression completely neutral, observing the agonizing cries of the multiverse with pure, clinical fascination.

Later that evening, Issei was lying on his bed, reading a manga and thoroughly enjoying the peacefulness of his room.

A sudden flash of crimson light illuminated his ceiling.

Issei dropped his manga in shock as a teleportation circle etched itself into his floorboards. Rias Gremory stepped out of the magic circle, her face pale, her breathing heavy, and her crimson eyes filled with an uncharacteristic, wild desperation.

"President?!" Issei yelped, scrambling backward against his headboard. "What are you doing here—"

Without a single word, Rias grabbed the hem of her school uniform and pulled it over her head, tossing it onto the floor. She immediately reached back to unhook her bra.

Issei’s brain instantly flatlined. "W-W-Wait! President! What is happening?!"

"There is no time to explain, Issei!" Rias commanded, her voice trembling slightly as she stepped toward the bed, her stunning figure completely exposed to the moonlight. "I need you to take my virginity right now! You took sex education classes, didn't you? Be a man and do it!"

Issei’s heart hammered against his ribs like a jackhammer. He was paralyzed, caught between the absolute peak of his degenerate dreams and the terrifying realization that his master was acting completely out of character.

Before Issei could even formulate a response, the temperature in the bedroom plummeted to a freezing zero.

A second magic circle, this one shining with a cold, brilliant silver light, materialized directly behind Rias.

"I must say, my lady," a smooth, chillingly calm female voice echoed through the bedroom.

Rias froze in terror. Issei peeked around her shoulder to see a beautiful woman with long silver hair, dressed in an immaculate French maid outfit, stepping out of the circle. Her eyes were completely devoid of warmth.

"To go so far as to throw away your purity to a low-class reincarnated Devil," Grayfia Lucifuge said coldly, adjusting the cuffs of her uniform. "It is a truly desperate, unbecoming attempt to cancel your engagement. But I am afraid you are out of time."

### Section 2: Riser Phenix
#### Sub Section 1: Childish Disappointment

"As a noblewoman, and especially as the heir to the House of Gremory, you are expected to conduct yourself with elegance and wisdom," Grayfia stated, her voice devoid of inflection but carrying the crushing weight of a glacier. She did not avert her gaze as she looked down at the trembling redhead. "Please put your clothes back on, my lady."

Rias’s face burned with a mixture of profound shame and terror. Without a word, she hurriedly picked up her uniform from the floor and pulled it back over her shoulders, her previous desperation entirely neutralized by the maid’s freezing presence.

Grayfia turned her silver eyes toward the boy still frozen against his headboard. "I am Grayfia Lucifuge. I serve the House of Gremory."

Rias finished buttoning her blouse, her hands shaking slightly. "Grayfia... did you come here on your own? Or were you sent by my family? By my brother?"

"Everyone," Grayfia answered smoothly, her gaze remaining entirely unreadable. "Your father, your brother, and myself. Your actions tonight were highly erratic, Rias."

Realizing exactly how grave the political and familial situation had become, the fight completely drained out of Rias. She turned to Issei, a look of genuine sorrow and regret crossing her beautiful features. "I'm so sorry, Issei. Please... just forget everything that happened here tonight. I lost my mind for a moment."

Grayfia tilted her head slightly, her sharp eyes scanning Issei’s magical signature. "Issei... Is this the boy your reports mentioned? The one possessing the soul of the Red Dragon Emperor?"

"Yes," Rias confirmed quietly.

"I see. Then we will speak of this later. Come, my lady." Grayfia turned, stepping back into the silver magic circle. Rias gave Issei one last, lingering look of apology before following the silver-haired maid, vanishing into the night and leaving the bedroom in total silence.

Issei sat alone on his bed, the cold night air drifting in through the window. The terrifying political tension of the underworld completely bypassed his brain, replaced instead by a singular, soul-crushing realization: he had been less than ten seconds away from achieving his ultimate harem dream, and it had been snatched away. A profound, hollow depression settled over him.

While Issei wallowed in his ruined teenage fantasy, Ren Nocturnus Veritas was plunging into the deepest, most esoteric layer of martial philosophy.

Having cleared his extraordinary meridians and smoothed the dark interstice energy, Ren recognized that raw kinetic output and elemental manipulation were ultimately finite. To truly ascend, he needed to master the origin point of all supernatural phenomena: *Will upon reality*.

He had studied the concept through countless fictional mediums. The Occult Research Club called it demonic imagination; mages in *Fairy Tail* relied on strong emotions; warriors in *One Piece* utilized Haki—a profound sense of self that allowed them to strike untouchable Logia users or nullify immortal abilities. But stripped of their stylistic flavor, they all traced back to a single, fundamental cosmic truth: divine authority. It was the act of a conscious entity imposing its absolute desire upon the physical world.

Standing in the center of his dark apartment, Ren began to practice.

The vast majority of martial artists only practiced mechanics, forms, and breath control. When they threw a punch, they left the ultimate result to physics and fate. Even the greatest masters only embedded their *intent* to harm. Ren, however, was practicing how to embed absolute *will*.

He threw a slow, deliberate palm strike at the empty air. He didn't just visualize the kinetic transfer; he mentally commanded the space to fracture. *When I strike, the opponent falls. It is not a probability; it is a law.*

He visualized a reinforced magical barrier. He didn't imagine hitting it hard enough to break it; he imposed the absolute reality that the shield was already shattered by his touch. He visualized environmental hazards—a man made of lava, a cloud of corrosive poison. He channeled his dark energy not to fight the elements, but to violently erase their right to exist within his immediate vicinity. It was incredibly taxing, a mental strain that dwarfed physical exhaustion, but slowly, the air around Ren’s fists began to warp, bending slightly under the sheer gravitational weight of his imposed reality.

The next afternoon, the atmosphere inside the Occult Research Club was incredibly heavy.

Issei sat on the sofa, still radiating a miserable, zombie-like depression over the previous night’s interrupted climax. Rias sat at her desk with Grayfia standing dutifully behind her, while Akeno, Kiba, Koneko, and Asia stood in tense silence.

Rias took a deep breath, preparing to explain the situation to her peerage.

Before she could speak, a massive magic circle erupted in the center of the room. It wasn't the crimson of the Gremory clan, but a blazing, fiery orange. A pillar of intense heat washed over the room, and from the flames emerged a tall, blonde young man in a maroon suit, radiating an aura of absolute, unchecked arrogance.

"Rias, my beloved," Riser Phenex smirked, stepping out of the flames. "I have come to collect you. It is time we return to the Underworld and prepare for our wedding ceremony."

Rias stood up, her crimson aura flaring in instant defiance. "I have told you a hundred times, Riser. I will not marry you. I will choose my own husband, and I will live my life on my own terms."

Grayfia stepped forward, her voice cutting through the tension as she looked at Issei and the confused peerage. "Allow me to introduce Riser Phenex, the third son of the noble House of Phenex. He is Lady Rias's officially arranged fiancé."

Riser scoffed, waving a hand dismissively. "I truly do not understand how long you intend to keep up this stubborn charade, Rias. You know the reality of our world. The Great War against the Angels and Fallen Angels decimated our population. We lost countless Pureblood Devils. This engagement isn't just about us; it is a mandate to preserve the Pureblood lineages so our race can survive."

Sitting on the sofa, Issei froze. The fog of his perverted depression instantly evaporated. He looked at Rias, seeing the deep, painful burden in her eyes. This wasn't just a rich girl rebelling against her parents; this was the crushing weight of an entire species’ survival being forced onto her shoulders. For the first time, Issei truly understood the political cage his master was trapped in.

"Even so, I refuse to be a broodmare for a man I do not love," Rias sneered.

Riser’s arrogant smile vanished, replaced by a dark, volatile scowl. He looked around the clubroom, visibly turning up his nose. "I cannot stand the impure, filthy air of this human world. You have played your little games long enough, Rias. If you will not come willingly, I will drag you back by force. Even if I have to burn this miserable human school to the ground and slaughter every single one of your low-class pieces to do it."

"Try it, Riser," Rias challenged, her demonic power surging violently enough to crack the windows.

Flames erupted from Riser’s hands, melting the carpet beneath his feet as the two High-Class Devils prepared to tear the room apart.

*CRACK.*

The temperature in the room instantly dropped below freezing. A suffocating, terrifying pressure slammed into everyone present, forcing Issei and Asia to their knees. Grayfia had taken a single step forward, a fraction of her silver aura leaking into the room.

"In the name of Sirzechs Lucifer, I ask that neither of you take this altercation any further," Grayfia said, her voice eerily calm, yet promising absolute annihilation. "If you ignore this request, I will personally intervene and subdue you both."

Rias and Riser immediately broke into a cold sweat. The flames died down, and the crimson aura vanished.

"Hmph," Riser clicked his tongue, taking a step back. "I am not foolish enough to pick a fight with the strongest Queen in the Underworld. I will back down. For now."

As the suffocating pressure lifted, Grayfia adjusted her collar, her expression returning to perfect neutrality. Then, she paused, her silver eyes narrowing slightly as she recalled a specific detail from her intelligence briefings.

"Lady Rias," Grayfia began, turning her head. "When I confirmed the Red Dragon Emperor's identity last night, I neglected to ask about another anomaly in the reports. My intelligence network noted that during the incident with the Fallen Angel Raynare, a normal human teenager directly engaged the Fallen Angels and survived. Where is this individual?"

"Oh! That's Ren!" Issei blurted out, jumping up from the sofa. "Hold on, I'll go get him!"

Before anyone could stop him, Issei sprinted out the door. Less than three minutes later, he returned, dragging a completely unbothered Ren by the sleeve of his uniform.

Ren stepped into the clubroom. He took one look at the melted carpet, the lingering thermal radiation of Riser's flames, the residual cryogenic chill of Grayfia's aura, and the intense, assessing stares of every supernatural being in the room.

He didn't say a word. He simply shoved his hands into his pockets and stood there, observing the aristocratic Devils with the calm, detached fascination of a scientist looking at a new breed of volatile insects.

#### Sub Section 2: The Fried Chicken and the Rating Game

All eyes in the Occult Research Club shifted to the human who had just been dragged into the room. Riser narrowed his eyes, searching for a demonic aura, a sacred gear, or any trace of magical energy. Finding absolutely nothing, he scoffed.

"This is the human anomaly?" Riser mocked, shaking his head. "He has no mana. No aura. He is just a baseline mortal. Have your standards fallen so low, Rias, that you are employing standard humans to do your dirty work?"

Akeno smiled behind her hand. "Looks can be deceiving, Lord Phenex. He is quite the interesting variable."

Ren ignored the commentary entirely. He leaned against the wall near the door, his eyes scanning Riser's posture, thermal output, and center of gravity, quietly calculating exactly how much force it would take to snap the arrogant Devil's neck.

Before the tension could escalate further, Grayfia stepped to the center of the room. "On behalf of the Gremory and Phenex families, a formal resolution has been proposed to settle this dispute. A Rating Game. Will you reject this as well, Lady Rias?"

"I accept," Rias answered instantly, her eyes flashing with determination. "I want to do this and get it over with."

For the benefit of the newer members in the room, Grayfia seamlessly transitioned into an explanation. "A Rating Game is a traditional battle between Devils and their peerages, conducted in a pocket dimension. Historically, it was used to measure a Devil's military might. In the modern era, it serves a dual purpose: it provides crucial battle experience for the servants, and it acts as a stage for the master to display their tactical leadership and strategic merit."

Riser let out a condescending laugh. "Are you entirely sure about this, Rias? You have never participated in a single Rating Game. I have fought in several, and I remain undefeated. Furthermore, look at your peerage. You have a Knight, a Bishop, a Rook, and a completely untrained Pawn. That is hardly a vanguard."

Riser snapped his fingers.

The room was instantly flooded with a blinding array of teleportation circles. When the light faded, fifteen girls materialized behind Riser, filling the clubroom to the brim.

"Allow me to introduce my complete set," Riser smirked, gesturing grandly. "My Queen, Yubelluna. My Bishop, Mihae. My Knights, Karlamine and Siris. My Rooks, Isabela and Xuelan. And my Pawns: the twins Ile and Nel, the twins Ni and Li, Marion, Bülent, Shuriya, and Mira."

Riser spread his arms, basking in the glory of his fully assembled, entirely female peerage. He expected Rias to look intimidated. He expected the Gremory servants to tremble.

What he did not expect was for Issei to fall to his knees, clutching his chest as he openly wept tears of profound, agonizing complexity.

Issei was vibrating with a mixture of overwhelming awe, sheer terror, and soul-crushing envy. A master with an entire harem of beautiful women representing every single archetype—maids, knights, twins in gym bloomers, mature women—it was the exact dream Issei had sworn his life to achieve.

Riser blinked, completely thrown off balance by the crying boy. He pointed a finger at Issei. "Rias... what is wrong with your Pawn? Is he having a seizure?"

Rias dragged a hand down her face, sighing heavily. "Issei's ultimate life goal is to become a Harem King. You are currently standing in front of his manifested dream."

Ile and Nel, the twin Pawns dressed in pristine school gym uniforms, looked down at Issei in disgust. "Gross," they said in perfect unison.

"Now, now, my sweet Pawns," Riser purred, walking over to the twins and pulling them flush against his chest. "Do not be too harsh. It is completely natural for a filthy, low-class Devil to look upon the glory of the high-class and weep with envy."

Riser smirked. He grabbed Ile by the chin and leaned down, pressing his lips against hers in a deep, aggressive French kiss. A second later, he pulled back and captured Nel’s lips, continuing the passionate display. Throughout the entire kiss, Riser kept his eyes locked dead onto Issei, his gaze transmitting a clear, agonizing message: *Look at this. You will never, ever have this.*

Riser pulled back from the panting twins, wiping his mouth. "You will never have what I have, boy."

"DON'T SAY EXACTLY WHAT I WAS THINKING!" Issei shrieked, scrambling to his feet, his face red with rage and jealousy. "You're the worst! You're a terrible match for the President! Even if you marry her, you're just going to keep messing around with all these other girls!"

"I thought you admired players, boy," Riser laughed, wrapping an arm around the waist of Bülent, who was dressed in a classic maid outfit. "I am their master, their hero. They demand my affection, and as a noble, it is my duty to provide it."

"You aren't a hero, you're a seed-scattering bird!" Issei yelled, pointing an accusing finger. "Actually, no, you're just a giant fried chicken! You're a rooster trying to mate with as many hens as possible!"

Riser’s smug expression instantly vanished, replaced by a dark, murderous glare. "How dare you... Rias, how exactly have you raised your servants to speak to a pureblood noble this way?"

Rias simply looked away, ignoring him while silently agreeing with every word Issei had just said.

"I'll show you how she raised me!" Issei roared. A crimson gauntlet materialized on his left arm, the Boosted Gear flaring to life with a mechanical hum. "I'm going to beat the aristocratic smugness right out of you!"

Riser let out a long, exhausted sigh. "What an absolute pain. Mira."

A small girl wielding a heavy metal staff stepped forward.

Issei charged, pulling his fist back. But as he looked at Mira, his hesitation spiked. She was just a small girl. He didn't want to punch her in the face. Instead of striking, Issei awkwardly lunged forward, trying to grab the staff out of her hands to disarm her.

It was a fatal, amateur mistake.

Mira didn't even blink. She sidestepped Issei’s clumsy grab, swept her staff behind his knees, and drove the blunt end directly into his sternum. Issei gasped, the wind completely knocked out of his lungs, and crashed hard onto the floorboards, utterly incapacitated in less than two seconds.

Riser walked over, gently patting Mira on the head. Her stoic battle expression instantly melted into a bright, happy smile as she leaned into her master's touch.

"Mira is the absolute weakest of my Pawns," Riser sneered, looking down at Issei wheezing on the floor. "But in terms of quality, combat experience, and sheer instinct as a Devil, she eclipses you completely. Do not make me laugh, boy. Even the most frightening Sacred Gear in history is useless if the user is incompetent. Pearls before swine."

Riser turned his back on Issei, looking thoughtfully at Rias. "Actually... if I crush him now, this Rating Game will be horribly boring. A show requires at least a little tension." Riser smiled arrogantly. "I will grant you ten days to prepare before the Rating Game."

"That is not necessary," Rias shot back, her pride stinging. "We can begin the match immediately."

"Do not be foolish, Rias," Riser said, his tone dropping its theatrical arrogance for a moment of cold, undeniable logic. He pointed at Issei on the floor. "Look at what just happened. Your Pawn hesitated because he was fighting a girl, and he was instantly neutralized. I have seen countless geniuses fall on the battlefield because their masters could not lead them properly. If you honestly believe you can defeat my entire experienced peerage with nothing but your naive passion, you are thoroughly delusional."

Rias gritted her teeth. She couldn't refute a single word. Her tactical leadership was untested, and her strongest piece was currently gasping for air on the carpet. "I understand," she said quietly.

"Good." Riser turned his gaze toward Issei, and then, his eyes drifted to the quiet human still leaning against the wall by the door. "Make use of these ten days, boy, so you can at least entertain me before you die. And that goes for your little human friend, too."

#### Sub Section 3: The Mortal Vanguard

Ren pushed himself off the wall, his hands casually slipping out of his pockets as he walked toward the center of the clubroom.

"I am not a part of the Gremory peerage," Ren stated, his voice perfectly even, betraying no intimidation in the face of the Phenex heir. "Even if President Rias allowed me to join her vanguard, the rules of a Rating Game strictly govern reincarnated and pureblood Devils. I am a baseline human. I cannot participate."

Riser blinked, looking down his nose at the boy. "Pardon me, then. I assumed the trash was collected together."

"However," Ren continued, stopping just a few feet away from the aristocrat, "if you genuinely want to fight me, it has to be right now. Because you will not get another chance on the board."

Riser let out a sharp, incredulous laugh. "Another cheeky brat."

He didn't even need to give a command. Mira, having just easily dispatched Issei, immediately spun her heavy metal staff and launched herself at Ren, aiming a brutal sweeping strike at his ribs.

Ren didn't blink. He didn't even shift into a combat stance. As the metal staff blurred toward him, he simply stepped a half-inch inside her guard, bypassing the weapon's kinetic arc entirely. With a clinical, effortless motion, Ren drove the edge of his hand directly into the side of Mira’s neck. The strike pinched her vagus nerve with pinpoint accuracy. Mira’s eyes rolled back, and she collapsed to the carpet without a single sound.

The clubroom went dead silent.

"MIRA!" Ile and Nel shrieked in unison.

The twin Pawns instantly summoned massive, roaring chainsaws, their gym uniforms fluttering as they charged Ren with murderous intent.

Ren exhaled a quiet neuro-somatic breath. As Ile swung horizontally, Ren ducked underneath the roaring teeth of the blade, stepping into her space. He executed a flawless CQC disarm, grabbing her wrist, twisting the joint past its mechanical limit, and using her own momentum to hurl her directly into Nel. As the twins collided in a tangle of limbs and dropped chainsaws, Ren delivered a swift, devastating sweeping kick that sent them crashing into the wall, completely unconscious.

The remaining Pawns—Ni, Li, Marion, Bülent, and Shuriya—erupted in outrage. Abandoning all formation, the five girls charged Ren simultaneously from multiple angles.

It was a massacre disguised as a martial arts exhibition.

Ren flowed through their coordinated strikes like water slipping through jagged rocks. His cleared meridians hummed, feeding him impossible kinetic speed and spatial awareness. He parried Marion's strike with the back of his hand, used the momentum to drive an elbow into Bülent's solar plexus, and swept Ni and Li off their feet with a fluid, low-altitude leg sweep. Shuriya tried to attack from his blind spot, but Ren simply pivoted on his heel and delivered a palm strike to her chest that launched her halfway across the room. In less than ten seconds, five high-class Devil Pawns were groaning on the floor.

"Stand back!" a fierce voice commanded.

Xuelan, one of Riser’s Rooks, stepped forward. She dropped into a flawless, traditional Chinese Kung Fu stance, her eyes burning with the thrill of a martial challenge.

"Finally," Ren murmured, recognizing the grounded balance of her footwork. "Someone who actually knows how to fight."

Xuelan charged. The exchange was a blistering blur of parries, strikes, and deflections. Xuelan fought with the overwhelming physical strength and dense durability granted by her Rook trait, fully expecting to tank Ren’s counterattacks.

As she threw a devastating straight punch, Ren stepped inside. He didn't just meet her with physical force; he drew upon the dark interstice energy pooling in his extraordinary meridians. He embedded his sheer *will* into his right fist—imposing the absolute, undeniable reality that her demonic defense was already shattered.

His fist collided with her sternum.

*CRACK.*

The sound of Xuelan’s defensive aura shattering echoed like a gunshot. The physical strength of a Rook was entirely bypassed by Ren’s imposed reality. Xuelan gasped, her eyes going wide with shock before her legs gave out, dropping her to her knees in instant defeat.

"Impossible..." Riser whispered, his arrogant smirk entirely wiped from his face. "A human just broke a Rook's defense..."

Karlamine, a Knight adorned in light armor, stepped over her fallen comrade. She drew her broadsword, pointing the gleaming blade at Ren. Unlike the others, her eyes held deep, honorable respect. "You are a warrior of incredible skill. I challenge you."

"Accepted," Ren nodded calmly.

Karlamine exploded forward, moving at the blinding, supernatural speed inherent to the Knight piece. She was nothing but a blur of steel.

But to Ren, she was moving in slow motion. Thanks to his rigorous blindfolded training, he didn't need to track her with his eyes; he felt the displacement of air pressure and the linear trajectory of her thermal signature.

"High speed is completely useless if the attack is entirely straightforward," Ren stated calmly.

He didn't dodge until the blade was a millimeter from his nose. With a microscopic tilt of his torso, the sword sliced empty air. Before Karlamine could correct her momentum, Ren stepped into her guard, trapping her sword arm and delivering three rapid, localized strikes to her joints. The Knight collapsed to the floor, completely immobilized.

Ren stood amidst the groaning, unconscious bodies of eight Devil peerage members. He slowly turned his head, his dark, calculating eyes locking onto the two remaining frontline fighters.

Isabela and Siris immediately dropped into defensive guards, sweat pouring down their faces as they stared in absolute terror at the human anomaly standing before them.

"Who is next?" Ren asked.

#### Sub Section 4: The Honor of the Peerage

"It appears Akeno’s assessment of you was entirely accurate," Grayfia’s calm, chilling voice cut through the heavy tension of the clubroom.

The silver-haired maid stepped smoothly between Ren and the two terrified Devils remaining on Riser’s frontline. She looked at the unconscious bodies of the Phenex peerage scattered across the floor, then turned her composed gaze to the human boy.

"However, Ren, I must ask you to cease your assault," Grayfia said politely. "If you dismantle the rest of Lord Phenex’s vanguard here in this room, Lady Rias will not have much of an opponent left for the actual Rating Game. Is that not correct, my lady?"

There was a long, suffocating pause.

Rias was still standing frozen near her desk, her crimson eyes wide as she processed the absolute massacre that had just occurred in under two minutes. A normal, uncontracted human had just single-handedly broken the martial line of a veteran Devil peerage without breaking a sweat.

Grayfia’s question finally snapped her out of her shock. Rias shook her head, forcing her aristocratic composure back into place.

"Yes... that is correct," Rias said, her voice steadying as she looked at Ren with a profound, newfound respect. "Thank you, Ren. But I need to have a real fight. I have to defeat Riser myself, on the official stage, to prove my independence."

Ren smoothly dropped his combat posture. The oppressive, localized gravity of his embedded will evaporated into the air. He slipped his hands casually back into his pockets and took a step back.

"No problem, President," Ren replied easily. "He issued a challenge, and I merely accepted it. I have no intention of interfering with your official business."

Riser Phenex stood near the back of the room, his fists clenched tight as his healing magic washed over his unconscious servants, slowly reviving them. The arrogant, theatrical smirk that had defined his entire presence was completely gone, replaced by a grim, wary realization.

"I am genuinely glad that you are a baseline human," Riser said, his voice entirely devoid of mockery as he looked at Ren. "If you were eligible to participate in the Rating Game, I would have a tremendous amount of tactical preparation to do. You are a terrifying anomaly."

Ren offered a slight, polite bow of his head. "I am honored to hear that."

Riser then turned his gaze down to Issei, who had finally managed to sit up from the floor, clutching his bruised chest. Riser’s expression hardened into the stern, uncompromising mask of a true Pureblood noble.

"Listen to me carefully, boy," Riser said coldly. "Whether you like it or not, you are a member of the House of Gremory now. Every single action you take, every word you speak, and every time you fall on your face reflects directly upon Rias’s reputation. Do not sully her honor with your pathetic, unrefined behavior."

Riser gestured toward Ren. "Take an example from your human friend. If you want to protect your master, you need to forge yourself into a weapon, not a clown."

With a snap of his fingers, the blazing orange teleportation circles reappeared. Gathering his battered and bruised peerage, Riser and his servants vanished in a pillar of fire, leaving the Occult Research Club in a heavy, echoing silence.

Issei stared at the scorch marks on the carpet, Riser's final words ringing in his ears like a death knell. He looked at Ren, who was completely unbothered, and then at Rias, whose future depended entirely on a battle they were currently destined to lose. The gravity of the supernatural world finally crushed the last remnants of Issei's naive fantasies.

Rias walked around her desk, looking at her weary, outmatched peerage. She took a deep breath.

"We are hopelessly outclassed in experience and technique," Rias admitted firmly, looking at Issei, Asia, Kiba, and Koneko. "If we fight him today, we lose. Therefore, we are going to use every single second of the ten days he gave us. The Occult Research Club will be excusing ourselves from school starting tomorrow. We are heading into the mountains for an intensive training camp."

She turned her gaze to Ren, offering him a warm, deeply appreciative smile. "Thank you for defending my honor today, Ren. You showed us exactly what true martial discipline looks like."

Ren walked toward the clubroom door, offering a casual wave over his shoulder. "Don't mention it. Good luck in the mountains, President. Make sure Issei doesn't skip leg day."

## Life 3: Training Camp

### Section 1: The Mountain Camp and the Multiversal Echoes

The Gremory training camp was situated deep within the secluded mountains, far away from the prying eyes of human civilization. With only ten days to close the massive experience gap between Rias’s peerage and Riser’s veteran vanguard, the schedule was utterly relentless.

Issei was subjected to a brutal rotational gauntlet, passing through the hands of every senior member.

Under Akeno’s tutelage, the focus was demonic magic and manifestation. "Demonic power is entirely dependent on your imagination, Issei," Akeno instructed, demonstrating a perfectly formed orb of lightning. "You must visualize the outcome completely and without hesitation."

Asia, despite her angelic origins, adapted to the demonic magic system flawlessly, easily manifesting elemental light and spending the rest of the camp honing her healing Sacred Gear. Issei, however, failed spectacularly, producing nothing but pathetic sparks.

Akeno sighed, handing him a basket of apples. "If combat magic is too abstract, start with fine control. Peel these fruits using only your demonic aura."

Issei sat by the cabin for hours, staring intensely at the apples. As he visualized the skin of the fruit peeling away, his deeply ingrained degenerate instincts took over. *Peeling the outer layer to reveal the soft flesh underneath... wait... isn't that exactly like stripping a girl's clothes?!* The sudden, perverted epiphany sparked a massive surge in his demonic aura, planting the foundational seed for a spell that would eventually become his signature technique: Dress Break.

When he transitioned to unarmed combat with Koneko, the results were far more painful. Issei spent hours being thrown into the dirt, entirely unable to read her stoic, heavy strikes.

"Trash footwork," Koneko deadpanned, effortlessly sweeping his legs out from under him for the twentieth time.

Issei groaned from the grass. "Man... Ren would have been the perfect trainer for this. He took down your entire counterpart frontline without even trying."

Koneko paused, a rare flicker of agreement crossing her golden eyes. "Yes. We should have brought him."

Kiba took over in the afternoon, putting Issei through a dizzying crash course in swordsmanship and evasion, forcing him to track blindingly fast strikes to improve his kinetic reflexes. Finally, as the sun set, Rias oversaw his physical stamina training.

"Ninety-nine... one hundred..." Issei gasped, his arms giving out as he collapsed from another set of push-ups.

"Get up, Issei, you have to push harder," Rias began, before suddenly pausing. She remembered the leather-bound notebook Ren had pulled out in the school courtyard, detailing the severe drawbacks of localized muscle failure. She sighed, adjusting her strategy. "Actually, stop. Stand up. We are switching to squats and core stabilization. You need to develop your posterior chain, not just your chest."

Outside of Issei's grueling rotation, the rest of the peerage trained individually. Rias locked herself in the cabin, obsessively studying the tactical layouts of historical Rating Games. Akeno ventured into the deep woods, unleashing massive, terrifying lightning storms to increase her voltage output. Koneko repeatedly hurled massive boulders across ravines to push her physical limits, while Kiba ran high-speed evasion drills through the dense treeline.

However, despite their intense dedication, their methodology was fundamentally flawed.

They were training in isolated silos. Had Ren been officially admitted into the peerage, his tactical mindset would have immediately restructured the entire camp. Individual conditioning was a waste of a ten-day limit. Ren would have forced them into brutal, synchronized cross-sparring. He would have suggested throwing Issei into genuine, near-death combat against Akeno and Rias, relying entirely on Asia’s Twilight Healing to instantaneously repair the tissue damage. By abusing the healer, they could have bypassed biological recovery times entirely, compressing years of actual battlefield survival experience into a single week. Instead, they settled for traditional, compartmentalized drills.

While the Gremory peerage prepared for war in the mountains, Ren remained in the city, sinking deeper into the abyssal depths of his own internal architecture.

Having cleared his major pathways and extraordinary reservoirs, Ren turned his focus to the micro-circuitry of his body: the minor chakras.

Sitting in the center of his darkened living room, Ren directed the dense, frictionless dark energy through the hundreds of smaller energetic nodes scattered across his joints, spine, and cranial cavity. The process was painstakingly delicate, requiring surgical neuro-somatic precision to prevent the dark interstice energy from rupturing the fragile pathways.

Once the minor chakras were fully aligned, Ren initiated the final phase of his current cultivation stage. He drove the energy directly into his three core Dantians—the Lower Dantian at his naval, the Middle Dantian at his heart, and the Upper Dantian at his pineal gland.

*Crack.*

The three centers ignited simultaneously, forming a perfect, circulating trinity of absolute power.

Instantly, Ren’s perception of the interstice energy evolved. He no longer just felt the sorrow. He no longer just heard the wails.

The darkness behind his closed eyelids violently shattered, replaced by a blinding, multiversal kaleidoscope of pure, unadulterated tragedy. He saw the visions. He watched ancient, forgotten empires burn to ash under skies of bleeding glass. He saw the faces of millions of entities betrayed by their creators, screaming in terror as they were cast into the void. He witnessed the visceral, raw envy of beings watching the light exalt others while they were left to rot in the dark.

It was a psychic avalanche of multiversal despair, projecting the collective nightmare of every forgotten soul directly into his visual cortex.

Ren sat perfectly still. His breathing remained at a flawless forty beats per minute. He did not look away, nor did he flinch. With absolute, chilling detachment, the human anomaly observed the infinite horrors of the interstice, smoothly integrating their power into his own accelerating evolution.

### Section 2: The Hot Springs and the Physics of Destruction

The sun finally dipped below the mountain peaks, signaling an end to the brutal, bone-crushing schedule of the training camp. For the Occult Research Club, the evening transitioned into the most anticipated recovery activity: the traditional open-air hot springs.

Initially, the baths were strictly separated by gender. However, despite the fact that Issei had already bathed with Rias and Asia multiple times back at his house, his deeply ingrained, degenerate instincts from his days with Matsuda and Motohama completely overrode his exhaustion. The mere presence of a bamboo partition separating him from the girls was an unbearable psychological challenge.

"Issei, you are practically dead on your feet," Kiba advised gently, sinking into the steaming water with a relaxed sigh. "You exhausted your demonic aura trying to peel apples all day. You really need to just rest."

"Rest is for the weak, Kiba!" Issei hissed, his eyes completely bloodshot as he pressed his face against the wooden divider. "There is a wall. A wall meant to be conquered! If my imagination dictates my demonic power, then I just need to visualize seeing *through* the wood!"

Ignoring Kiba's exasperated warnings, Issei clamped his hands to his temples and forced every last drop of his remaining demonic energy into his optic nerves, desperately trying to invent a functional X-ray spell on the spot. He strained so hard that a blood vessel popped in his nose. Ten seconds later, Issei’s eyes rolled back into his head, and he collapsed face-first into the hot spring with a pathetic splash, completely unconscious from magical exhaustion.

Kiba dragged the unconscious Red Dragon Emperor out of the water and carried him to the partition door.

"President?" Kiba called out apologetically. "Issei just knocked himself out trying to invent X-ray magic."

On the other side of the wall, Rias dragged a wet hand down her face. She was genuinely worried that her Pawn was going to cause permanent neurological damage to himself just to catch a glimpse of them. "If he is going to literally kill himself trying to peep, it is safer to just remove the obstacle," Rias sighed.

Ten minutes later, Issei woke up to the absolute greatest miracle of his life.

The partition had been ignored, and the hot spring was now a fully mixed bath. Rias, Akeno, and Asia were soaking in the steaming water, completely bare and entirely comfortable with his presence, chatting casually as if it were the most normal thing in the world. Issei sat in the corner, tears of absolute joy streaming down his face as he thanked the heavens for his master’s boundless generosity.

The only exception was Koneko. The petite first-year sat on a submerged rock at the edge of the pool, tightly wrapped in a thick white towel, glaring daggers at Issei. "Pervert," she muttered, clearly still uncomfortable with the new arrangement.

Hundreds of miles away from the mountain retreat, Ren was standing in the center of an abandoned, concrete industrial lot on the outskirts of Kuoh Town.

Having successfully mapped his minor chakras and synchronized his three core Dantians, Ren transitioned to clearing his major chakras—the primary energy gates located along the spine. He systematically forced the dark interstice energy through the Root, Sacral, Solar Plexus, Heart, Throat, Third Eye, and Crown chakras. The process was agonizing, but once completed, his internal circuitry was flawlessly unblocked. His body was no longer just a vessel; it was a perfect, unobstructed conduit for multiversal energy.

It was time to practice externalization.

Drawing upon classic *Dragon Ball* tropes, Ren extended his right hand. He didn't want to just strike with his energy; he wanted to project it. He commanded the dense, dark energy from his Dantians to flow down his arm and pool in his palm.

A sphere of condensed, swirling black energy materialized. It hummed with terrifying kinetic weight, warping the air around it.

"Fire," Ren commanded.

He threw the dark energy sphere like a baseball at a rusted, junked sedan thirty feet away. The projectile crossed the distance instantly.

*CRASH.*

The black sphere slammed into the side of the car, completely crushing the reinforced steel door and flipping the two-ton vehicle onto its roof through sheer kinetic force. The sphere then destabilized and simply fizzled out into black mist.

Ren frowned, lowering his hand.

It hadn't exploded. It had merely acted like a bowling ball made of incredibly dense gravity.

Ren walked over to the dented car, analyzing the failure. Magic users, like Devils, relied on pre-established magical formulas to create explosions. But Ren didn't have magic; he operated on the principle of imposing his *will* upon reality using raw interstice energy.

"I told the energy to form a sphere and hit the target, so it did exactly that," Ren muttered to himself, tracing the dented steel. "But an explosion isn't just a heavy impact. It is a rapid exothermic reaction. It is the sudden, violent expansion of gases and thermal energy."

He realized the flaw in his cultivation. He possessed the raw power to impose reality, but his conceptual framework was too vague. If he wanted his energy constructs to behave like explosive plasma, he couldn't just visualize a generic flash of light from a shonen manga.

He needed to understand the exact mechanics of what he was commanding the universe to do.

Ren turned away from the junked car and walked back toward the city streets. The physical training was put on hold. It was time to visit the local library and heavily study the advanced physics of thermodynamics, the chemistry of combustion, and the exact molecular triggers of exothermic reactions.

### Section 3: Mixed Bath and Harrowing Interstice

The steam rising from the mountain hot spring curled into the crisp night air, carrying with it the gentle sounds of water splashing and relaxed conversation. Issei sat submerged up to his chin, his face flushed a deep, dangerous shade of crimson. He was trying, and utterly failing, to keep his eyes fixed firmly on the starry sky.

"You are staring awfully hard at the moon, Issei," Akeno teased, resting her arms on the smooth stones at the edge of the pool. She leaned forward slightly, allowing the water to ripple against her bare collarbones. "Are you sure you wouldn't rather look down here? You went through so much trouble to see through the wall, after all."

"I am practicing supreme mental discipline, Akeno-senpai!" Issei squeaked, his voice cracking violently as his peripheral vision betrayed him.

Rias chuckled softly from across the spring, sweeping her wet crimson hair over her shoulder. "Let him be, Akeno. He used up all his demonic aura on a very... creative endeavor. If his heart rate goes any higher, I am afraid my Pawn will go into cardiac arrest before we even reach the Rating Game."

Asia, entirely oblivious to the heavy, teasing tension, scooped up some warm water and poured it over her shoulders with a bright smile. "The water is so nice and warm! It feels like all the aches from today's magic training are just melting away. Don't you think so, Issei?"

"Y-Yeah, Asia! Melting! Everything is melting!" Issei stammered, frantically splashing cold water onto his burning cheeks.

Sitting on a submerged boulder near the shallow end, Koneko pulled her white towel tighter around her small frame, narrowing her golden eyes at him. "Disgusting. Your degenerate aura is contaminating the water supply."

"I haven't even done anything!" Issei protested defensively.

Kiba simply laughed from his corner of the spring, leaning his head back against the rocks. "Enjoy the peace while it lasts, Issei. Tomorrow we increase the gravity on your evasion drills. Riser gave us ten days, and we are going to use every single hour."

Rias’s smile softened into a look of quiet determination. She looked around the spring at her peerage, the people who were currently breaking themselves to protect her freedom. "Thank you, all of you. For putting up with this grueling camp. I promise you, when we step onto that battlefield, I will not let your efforts go to waste."

While the Occult Research Club bonded in the secluded mountains, Ren was standing on the roof of his apartment building under that same starry sky, pushing the boundaries of human limitation.

Having mastered the internal circuitry of his chakras and the theoretical physics of external projection, Ren moved to the next logical stage of advanced control: manipulating his own mass.

His goal was levitation. He closed his eyes, synchronizing his three Dantians, and began to externalize the dark interstice energy. But instead of projecting it outward as a destructive force, he directed it downward, enveloping his own body in a hyper-dense, frictionless aura that actively repelled the Earth's gravitational pull.

Slowly, his sneakers lifted off the concrete.

One inch. Six inches. A full foot in the air.

Ren opened his eyes, hovering effortlessly above the rooftop. But as he severed his physical tether to the earth, something fundamental shifted. The moment he broke gravity's hold, his resonance with the dark energy spiked exponentially. The wails of multiversal despair he had been hearing in his mind suddenly amplified, turning from distant echoes into a deafening, immediate roar.

The Harrowing Interstice was reacting to him. It was pulling on the energy flowing through his meridians.

Ren didn't resist. Intrigued, he closed his eyes again and focused entirely on that pull, amplifying his internal frequency to match the violent, sorrowful resonance of the void. He pushed his will outward, demanding the space in front of him to yield.

The air on the rooftop tore open.

It didn't form a neat, magical circle like Devil transportation magic. Reality simply ruptured like a ragged wound, bleeding pitch-black mist.

Ren slowly lowered himself to the concrete, his eyes fixed on the tear. The sheer atmospheric pressure leaking from the portal was suffocating. It smelled of ozone, ash, and absolute zero. It was a dimension fundamentally hostile to normal life—a place where no sane creature would ever willingly tread.

Without a second thought, Ren stepped through the rift.

The portal sealed shut behind him, leaving him standing in a sprawling, endless wasteland of shattered gray rock and skies bleeding with dark, violet nebulas. The gravity here was immense, pressing down on his shoulders with the weight of deep ocean depths, and the air was so dense with raw, negative energy that it felt like breathing liquid glass.

Ren looked around, his analytical mind instantly evaluating the environment. The hostility of this dimension was perfect. The intense atmospheric pressure and the boundless density of the dark energy made this place an ultimate hyperbolic training ground. A baseline human would be crushed into paste in seconds, but for someone cultivating this exact energy, the potential for rapid physiological evolution was limitless.

Yet, as Ren walked forward, his boots crunching against the ashen ground, the tactical assessment faded, replaced by a profound, deeply unsettling sensation.

He felt entirely at peace.

The wails of despair swirling through the violet storms didn't frighten him; they sounded like a choir. The crushing gravity felt like a warm embrace. Against all logic, standing in the most terrifying, desolate corner of the multiverse, Ren felt a bizarre, overwhelming sense of coming home.

A primal urge gripped his chest—a sudden, intense desire to just start walking into the abyss, to explore the endless, dead horizons and never look back.

Ren stopped in his tracks, his supreme neuro-somatic control catching the emotional spike before it could fully hijack his logic. He calculated the variables. If he gave in to the urge to explore this wasteland, time dilation and the sheer scale of the dimension meant he likely wouldn't return for months, maybe years. Perhaps never.

He was perfectly fine with leaving, but disappearing without a trace was highly inefficient. It left loose ends.

"Not yet," Ren murmured to the howling wind.

He extended his will, forcing the fabric of the interstice to rupture once more. Stepping back through the tear, Ren materialized in his dark, quiet apartment in Kuoh. He looked around the mundane room, the lingering chill of the void still clinging to his skin. If he was going to plunge into the abyss for an extended period, he needed to properly sever his ties to this world first. He would pack his essential gear, tie up his lease, and at the very least, say his farewells.

## Life 4: Rating Game
### Section 1: Preparation
#### Sub Section 1: Forgotten Ren

The atmosphere inside the Occult Research Club was thick with nervous anticipation. The ten-day grace period had evaporated. Rias Gremory stood at the head of the room, adorned in her formal crimson combat uniform, projecting an aura of aristocratic determination. Behind her stood Akeno, Kiba, Koneko, Asia, and Issei—each bearing the quiet, heavy tension of soldiers stepping onto a battlefield for the very first time.

Standing before them, completely unbothered by the heavy atmosphere, was Grayfia Lucifuge. The silver-haired maid held a pocket watch, snapping it shut with a crisp click.

"You are on time, Lady Rias," Grayfia noted smoothly. Her icy silver eyes then swept across the assembled peerage, calculating their numbers. She frowned slightly. "However, there appears to be someone missing. Where is Ren?"

Rias blinked, slightly taken aback by the question. "Ren? He is a baseline human, Grayfia. He is not part of my peerage, nor is he eligible to participate in a Rating Game. I did not think to involve him."

"Technically, you are correct," Grayfia replied, her tone perfectly level. "However, Lord Sirzechs was under the impression that the boy had become intimately acquainted with your affairs. He assumed Ren would tag along, as Lord Sirzechs wishes to see this human anomaly with his own eyes."

A heavy silence fell over Rias. A sudden, crushing wave of tactical regret washed over her. She remembered Ren effortlessly dismantling Riser’s veteran vanguard in this very room. She remembered Koneko and Issei mentioning how drastically Ren’s combat insight could have accelerated their training in the mountains. Because she had been so entirely consumed by the political urgency of the engagement, the thought of simply hiring or inviting Ren as a tactical consultant for the camp had completely bypassed her mind.

"I... I missed an incredible opportunity," Rias admitted quietly, pinching the bridge of her nose. "He would have been an invaluable asset during our training camp. It simply did not cross my mind."

"A lack of peripheral vision in times of crisis is a common flaw in inexperienced commanders," Grayfia stated without malice. "No matter. I will personally retrieve him so he may spectate the match alongside Lord Sirzechs and myself."

Issei, who had been vibrating with nervous energy, finally raised his hand. "Uh, excuse me, Grayfia-san. You keep mentioning this Sirzechs guy. Who exactly is he?"

Grayfia turned her gaze to the ignorant Pawn, though it was Rias who answered, her voice laced with quiet pride.

"Sirzechs is my older brother, Issei," Rias explained, adjusting her gauntlet. "But he is no longer a Gremory. During the aftermath of the Great War, he was chosen to inherit the title of the Crimson Satan. He is Sirzechs Lucifer, the current absolute ruler of the Underworld."

Asia gasped softly, and Issei’s jaw practically unhinged. The sheer scale of the political hierarchy he had stumbled into was staggering.

"Come," Grayfia instructed, gesturing to the massive, glowing transportation circle manifesting in the center of the room. "The stage is set. The spectators are waiting. It is time."

In his dimly lit apartment, Ren was meticulously packing a heavy canvas duffel bag. He had calculated exactly how many non-perishable rations, synthetic water filters, and medical supplies he could comfortably carry under 300 times Earth's gravity. He was preparing for his one-way trip into the Harrowing Interstice.

The temperature in his living room suddenly plummeted. Frost formed on the edges of his windows, and a blinding silver magic circle illuminated the dark floorboards.

Grayfia materialized, her posture immaculate, taking in the sight of the packed duffel bag and the tactical gear laid out on Ren's bed.

"Leaving town?" she asked neutrally.

"Relocating to a more intensive environment," Ren answered without missing a beat, zipping a pouch on his tactical vest. "To what do I owe the pleasure, Grayfia? The Rating Game should be starting momentarily."

"I have come to invite you to the VIP viewing gallery," Grayfia said, stepping fully into the room.

Ren paused, crossing his arms. "I am not a Devil. I am not contracted to the Gremory House, and I am not a member of the Occult Research Club. Politically and socially, I have absolutely no place at an aristocratic event."

"Lord Sirzechs took a keen interest in you following the incident with the Fallen Angel Raynare," Grayfia countered smoothly. "When intelligence reports confirmed that a baseline human neutralized eight members of the Phenex vanguard in under two minutes without utilizing mana or a Sacred Gear, that interest evolved into a demand. He wishes to have a conversation with you."

Grayfia tilted her head slightly, a microscopic challenge gleaming in her silver eyes. "Or is the prospect of analyzing a high-level tactical war game too boring for you?"

A slow, genuine smirk touched the corner of Ren's mouth. He recognized the bait, but he didn't care. It was a flawless counter.

"On the contrary," Ren replied, grabbing his coat and leaving the duffel bag on the bed. "I absolutely love a good show. Lead the way."

The VIP spectator box was an architectural marvel suspended high above a one-to-one replica of Kuoh Academy, entirely contained within a pocket dimension. The room was lavishly decorated in crimson and gold, featuring massive crystalline screens projecting every angle of the battlefield below.

Standing by the panoramic glass was a tall, handsome man with flowing crimson hair that perfectly matched Rias’s. He radiated an aura so incomprehensibly dense that a normal human would have been instantly crushed into dust just by standing in the same room.

Ren stepped out of the silver teleportation circle, entirely unaffected. His cleared meridians and synchronized Dantians automatically neutralized the oppressive atmospheric pressure of the Devil King’s ambient power.

"Lord Sirzechs," Grayfia announced, bowing perfectly. "I present Ren Nocturnus Veritas."

Sirzechs Lucifer turned, his warm, charismatic smile greeting the human boy. He expected awe. He expected terror. He expected the boy to fall to his knees, as was standard for almost any lesser being in his presence.

Instead, Ren offered a polite, measured bow—the exact degree of respect one might give to a visiting university professor—and then straightened up, looking directly into the Devil King's eyes with calm, calculating appraisal.

Sirzechs’s smile widened into something incredibly genuine. The absolute lack of sycophancy or fear was profoundly refreshing. "Welcome, Ren. Please, have a seat. I hope you will enjoy the match with us."

"Thank you for the invitation, Lord Lucifer," Ren replied, taking a seat in one of the plush velvet chairs overlooking the battlefield. "It is an honor to observe."

"Grayfia tells me you are a close friend of my sister's Pawn," Sirzechs began, taking the seat beside him and pouring two glasses of high-end Underworld tea.

"Issei and I have a history," Ren confirmed, accepting the glass. "I used to be a founding member of the school's perverted quartet. We spent most of our time calculating the structural integrity of the girls' locker room walls."

Sirzechs let out a sudden, booming laugh, entirely delighted by the blunt admission. "A noble pursuit for a teenage boy! But I imagine there is more to you than voyeurism. The reports on your combat capabilities defy basic physics."

Ren took a slow sip of his tea. "Outside of my former associations, I am a scholar. My pursuits lie in the absolute mastery of martial arts, tactical espionage, asymmetrical warfare, and multiversal abilities. I seek a total understanding of the world's mechanics." Ren glanced down at the projection of the battlefield, where Rias and Riser’s pieces were taking their starting positions. "I am also an avid player of classic chess. Which makes this particular event highly relevant to my interests."

Sirzechs’s eyes gleamed. "A Rating Game is indeed nothing more than a magical translation of the chessboard. Which brings me to my primary curiosity. How does a human chess player with no magical lineage blind a Fallen Angel and dismantle a Devil vanguard?"

"Necessity," Ren answered simply. "It started when Raynare targeted Issei. I intervened. Against a creature that can manifest light spears that travel at terminal velocity, standard human vision is a lethal liability. Light is too distracting; it forces the brain to process unnecessary data. So, I blinded myself."

Sirzechs leaned forward, resting his chin on his hands, completely captivated. "You intentionally robbed yourself of sight?"

"I engaged in intensive blindfolded neuro-somatic training," Ren corrected. "I honed my other senses—spatial pressure, thermal shifts, kinetic displacement—until I could perfectly predict the trajectory of her attacks without needing to see them. From there, it was simple chemistry. A combat knife coated in highly concentrated holy water. I bypassed her guard, disrupted her flight mechanics, and nearly killed her before she retreated."

"Incredible," Sirzechs murmured. "A purely tactical deconstruction of a supernatural threat."

"Long story short," Ren concluded, his dark eyes fixed on the crystalline screens below, "that encounter proved that baseline humanity is insufficient for survival. I continued down a different path. I cultivate an energy source external to the standard magical systems of this world. That is why I can shatter a Rook's defense and bypass a Knight's speed. I do not use mana. I impose my own kinetic reality."

Sirzechs sat back, processing the terrifying, limitless potential of the boy sitting beside him. He looked down at the battlefield, where the first tactical maneuvers of the Rating Game were beginning to unfold.

"A scholar of war who imposes his own reality," Sirzechs mused, an excited glint in his crimson eyes. "Tell me then, Ren. As a grandmaster of the board, how do you predict my sister's first foray into the Rating Game will end?"

#### Sub Section 2: The Logic of the Board

"There is a very high probability that Rias will lose," Ren answered straightforwardly, his eyes tracking the initial movements of the Gremory peerage on the crystalline screens.

Sirzechs paused his teacup halfway to his mouth, utterly amused by the boy's bluntness. Most Devil nobles spent hours dancing around political niceties before daring to criticize a pureblood heiress. "Is that so? Please, continue."

"In terms of classical chess, Rias is starting the game down a massive amount of material," Ren explained, leaning back in his velvet chair. "She lacks a full frontline, whereas Riser commands a complete, highly experienced vanguard. Her only genuine trump cards are Issei's Boosted Gear and Asia's Twilight Healing. Furthermore, her tactical mindset is deeply compromised by her emotions. When Riser teleported his peerage into the clubroom to demonstrate the overwhelming imbalance, he actually gave her an out. He asked her to confirm if she truly wanted to proceed with the Rating Game. Rias did not even look at the board; she challenged him immediately out of pure passion and societal pressure."

Ren took a sip of his tea, his tone remaining clinical. "Even Riser realized how boring a one-sided slaughter would be and offered her ten days to prepare. And initially, she refused even that. Riser actually had to lecture her on the cold reality of battlefield leadership before she accepted the grace period. Passion does not close a systemic gap in material and experience."

Sirzechs let out a bright, booming laugh that echoed through the VIP box. He completely agreed with every single word. "A flawlessly brutal assessment! Tell me then, Ren, if you were in Rias's shoes, what exactly would you have done?"

Before Ren could open his mouth to answer, the heavy double doors of the spectator box swung open. Two distinguished, older Devils strode into the room, both radiating immense aristocratic authority.

"Well, this is going to be quite the problem," sighed the blonde man, Lord Phenex, as he took a seat near Sirzechs. He looked at the projection of his son, Riser, with a mixture of fondness and deep exhaustion. "I was genuinely hoping Riser would lose this match. His arrogance has grown entirely out of control, and I wanted him to experience the crushing reality of defeat for once."

The man beside him, boasting the same vibrant crimson hair as Rias and Sirzechs, chuckled dryly. Lord Gremory settled into an armchair, keeping his eyes on his daughter on the screen. "It appears Rias will be the first to be taught the harsh lesson of humility today, my friend. She is painfully underprepared."

Sirzechs smiled warmly at the two patriarchs before turning his attention back to the human anomaly sitting beside him. "The question stands, Ren. What would you have done?"

"First, I would have fully analyzed the board state and weaponized the blatant unfairness of the situation," Ren answered without hesitation. "I would have argued the sheer imbalance of material and leveraged my status as the Gremory heiress to negotiate for significantly more preparation time. Thirty days, at minimum."

Ren set his teacup down, his dark eyes sharpening as he looked at Rias's digital projection. "However, the real battlefield is inherently unfair, and negotiations often fail. If I were locked into the ten-day limit, I would have completely restructured the training camp. Ten days of standard exercise is a waste of time. I would have put Issei and the rest of the frontline into a continuous, near-death combat loop against Akeno and Rias, relying entirely on Asia's Sacred Gear to instantly heal their tissue damage and bypass biological recovery time. But, as Grayfia noted earlier, I was not invited to the mountains."

Sirzechs nodded slowly, his crimson eyes gleaming with profound respect for the human’s ruthlessly pragmatic mind. He completely agreed with the tactical breakdown.

"Given that you clearly see the insurmountable imbalance in materials," Ren asked, shifting his gaze from the screens to the Crimson Satan, "did you anticipate this outcome from the very beginning?"

"I did," Sirzechs admitted softly, his charismatic smile fading into the calculating gaze of a multiversal ruler. "I already have a contingency plan prepared for the immediate aftermath of Rias's defeat. Unlike you, Ren, I have the vast resources of the Underworld at my disposal, and I have been closely monitoring Issei and the shifting currents of the world."

Sirzechs looked down at the Red Dragon Emperor, who was currently charging through the replica of the Kuoh Academy woods. "The cosmic balance is shifting. The Red Dragon Emperor is forcefully resurfacing, and my intelligence suggests it is in direct, instinctual response to the awakening of the White Dragon Emperor."

### Section 2: The Tactical Depravity and the Inevitable Defeat

The digital projection of the Kuoh Academy battlefield materialized across the crystalline screens in the VIP gallery. The official broadcast of the Rating Game had begun, and the tension in the room thickened as the initial pieces moved into position.

In the canonical timeline, the Gremory training camp had pushed Issei to a respectable threshold—allowing him to maintain several consecutive Boosts and ultimately unleash a concentrated draconic blast capable of cratering a mountainside. However, the trajectory of this timeline had been subtly warped. The rigorous posterior-chain stabilization routines and localized muscular endurance drills Rias had implemented, directly referencing Ren’s anatomical journal, had fortified Issei’s physical foundation far beyond normal parameters.

This enhancement became glaringly obvious during his first engagement against the Phenex vanguard.

Instead of desperately scrambling through the woods, continuously dodging the synchronized strikes of Mira and the twin Pawns, Ile and Nel, Issei actually held his ground. His reinforced core and improved kinetic awareness allowed him to slip beneath their roaring chainsaws and heavy metal staff. He didn't just evade; he managed to check their momentum with precise, light counter-strikes to their joints, disrupting their formation long enough to finally land a solid, mana-infused touch on all three girls.

The battlefield fell silent for a fraction of a second.

And then, Issei unleashed the degenerate culmination of his agonizing, fruit-peeling magical training.

*“Dress Break!”*

With a concussive snap of demonic energy, the gym bloomers and battle armors of the three Pawns instantaneously shredded into confetti. The girls shrieked, desperately covering themselves as the sheer shock and overwhelming embarrassment completely shattered their will to fight. They collapsed to the ground in tears, effectively resigning from the match on the spot.

Back in the Gremory base, the reaction was universally horrified. Even Koneko, who had just flawlessly pinned Xuelan to the ground by exploiting the Rook’s ruptured defensive aura, stared at the projection with dead, hollow eyes. *“I have officially lost all remaining respect for you,”* Koneko’s voice echoed flatly over the comms.

Up in the VIP box, Sirzechs choked on his tea. Lord Phenex covered his eyes and groaned, while Lord Gremory stared at the screen in absolute, bewildered silence.

Ren, however, leaned forward in his velvet chair, his dark eyes analyzing the chaotic scene with cold, terrifying pragmatism.

"I was operating under the assumption that I had absolutely nothing to learn from Issei's approach to combat," Ren stated, his voice carrying clearly across the shocked silence of the gallery. "I was entirely wrong. He has just demonstrated a tactical maneuver that my logical framework never could have conceived."

Sirzechs wiped his mouth with a silk handkerchief, looking at the boy incredulously. "You consider *that* a legitimate tactical maneuver?"

"Dress Break fundamentally weaponizes societal conditioning to induce a state of profound psychological shock," Ren explained clinically, tapping a finger against his armrest. "Yes, it instantly makes him the mortal enemy of every female combatant on the board. But tactically, it is devastatingly convenient. It is entirely unprecedented, which makes it an absolute wildcard. It completely bypasses physical defense and directly targets the opponent's psychological guard, dropping their situational awareness to zero for a critical three-second window."

Ren’s eyes narrowed slightly as he watched Issei celebrating his victory on the screen. "However, Issei lacks the killer instinct to capitalize on it. I would have followed up the psychological shock by immediately crushing their tracheas or striking the vagus nerve to render them unconscious. Furthermore, his success here is a fluke dependent entirely on the inexperience of his opponents. Those three girls resigned purely out of embarrassment, demonstrating a severe lack of battlefield motivation. A true veteran would fight through the shame."

As if the universe were eager to prove Ren’s theory, the battlefield shifted violently.

Koneko, having just eliminated Xuelan, was suddenly ambushed by a massive, concentrated bombardment of explosive magic. Yubelluna, Riser’s Queen, descended from the sky, her staff glowing with volatile arcane energy. Issei rushed forward, intent on activating his Boosted Gear, but the skies crackled with blinding yellow electricity. Akeno intercepted the explosive bombardment with a massive wall of lightning.

*“Go, Issei! Leave the Queen to me!”* Akeno commanded, her eyes burning with the thrill of battle.

Issei nodded, sprinting past the clash of the Queens and diving deeper into the replica of the school building, eventually linking up with Kiba.

The Knight of Gremory was currently locked in a blindingly fast, high-stakes duel against Karlamine. The Phenex Knight fought with fierce, honorable precision, her broadsword clashing against Kiba’s summoned blades. But Kiba’s training in the mountains had refined his kinetic tracking. In a dazzling display of speed and tactical misdirection, Kiba bypassed Karlamine’s guard by sequentially manifesting multiple, distinct Demonic Swords from his Sword Birth Sacred Gear, overwhelming her linear defense and securing the victory.

Issei, meanwhile, finally ran into a wall of pure, unadulterated physical force.

Isabela, Riser’s remaining Rook, stood in the center of the gymnasium, radiating dense, hostile aura. Unlike the twins, Isabela fought with brutal, uncompromising power. Issei was battered across the room, forced to continuously evade as he bought time for the Boosted Gear to cycle through its power multiplications.

After taking a heavy hit to the ribs, Issei finally managed to slip inside her guard, slapping his glowing gauntlet against her armor.

*“Dress Break!”* Issei roared.

Isabela’s heavy armor exploded into fragments. She gasped, her face flushing crimson as she instinctively crossed her arms over her chest.

*“Now!”* Issei cheered internally, expecting her to collapse in tears like the others.

But Ren’s cold tactical analysis from the VIP box proved absolute. Not every soldier breaks under psychological shock. Isabela’s embarrassment lasted for exactly one second before it violently mutated into pure, murderous rage.

"You filthy, degenerate little rat!" Isabela screamed, abandoning her defensive posture and charging Issei with terrifying speed, her fist pulled back for a skull-crushing strike.

Caught completely off guard by the retaliation, Issei panicked. He had successfully cycled through enough multiplications, and acting purely on survival instinct, he threw his left arm forward and released the built-up draconic energy.

*“Dragon Shot!”*

A massive, concentrated beam of crimson energy erupted from the gauntlet, engulfing Isabela point-blank and blasting her through three consecutive reinforced concrete walls, instantly eliminating her from the match. Issei collapsed to his knees, gasping for breath, having learned a terrifying lesson about assumptions on the battlefield.

With Isabela defeated, the remaining members of the Phenex peerage—minus Riser and Yubelluna—converged on the gymnasium, intent on overwhelming Issei and Kiba through sheer numbers.

Driven into a corner, the camaraderie forged during their brutal mountain training finally bore fruit. Issei bypassed his usual selfish applications of power and forcefully unlocked the *Gift* function of the Boosted Gear. He channeled his multiplied draconic aura and transferred it directly into Kiba.

Empowered by the overwhelming surge of draconic energy, Kiba’s speed transcended the limits of a standard Knight. He manifested a massive, devastating array of demonic blades, moving faster than the eye could track. In a single, fluid kinetic storm, Kiba annihilated the remaining Phenex vanguard, clearing the board of all minor pieces.

The Gremory peerage had achieved the impossible. The numbers were even.

But the victory was tragically short-lived.

A massive explosion rocked the sky above the academy. Akeno, battered and exhausted, plummeted from the sky in a shower of sparks, her lightning finally overpowered by a relentless barrage of arcane artillery. Yubelluna descended like an angel of death, instantly locking onto Kiba. Before the Knight could recover from his massive energy expenditure, the Phenex Queen unleashed a point-blank explosive spell, eliminating him from the board.

Without pausing to savor the double elimination, Yubelluna immediately cast a spatial displacement spell, teleporting directly to Riser’s location at the center of the academy roof.

Realizing the terrifying shift in momentum, Issei sprinted toward the rooftop. Knowing he was now deep within enemy territory, he utilized the Pawn’s unique trait, legally promoting himself to the Queen piece to drastically boost his overall stats. He burst onto the roof, joining Rias and Asia, completely prepared for a desperate, three-on-two final stand against Riser and his Queen.

But the reality of aristocratic warfare was about to crush him.

A magical projection flickered to life in the sky above the academy, revealing Ravel Phenex, Riser’s younger sister, sipping tea in absolute comfort.

"I have absolutely no need to dirty my own hands in this petty squabble," Ravel announced haughtily. "But you should all be aware of exactly why Lady Yubelluna was able to defeat the powerful Priestess of Thunder. My brother provided her with a vial of Phoenix Tears."

Issei’s eyes went wide with fury. "That's cheating! You used a consumable to instantly heal your Queen mid-battle!"
